// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-Machines]]
= Unprivileged Architecture: Machines
ifdef::env-site[]
// On the site each page stands alone; the PDF gets these from src/Zkl.adoc.
include::partial$Zkl-symbols.adoc[]
endif::[]

_This chapter is normative._

Every Machine follows <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>> for values, bit slices, concatenations and finite-field operations: the lowest-addressed byte occupies the least significant bits; `@` places its left operand in the more significant bits, and `{vvert}` places its left operand at the lower offsets (<<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation-operators>>).

This chapter specifies KLE instruction behavior for supported Machines, defined either generically over compatible primitives (e.g., GCM with AES or SM4) or for a specific primitive (e.g., Ascon).

<<KLE-exec-encodings>> encodes each Machine in the MDH's _Machine_ field: _Type_ in bits [11:4] and _Mode_ in bits [3:0].
_Mode_ specifies a subtype for some classes; other groups may subdivide _Machine_ differently.

have _KeyType_ = 0 in its MDH; any other _KeyType_ is invalid Metadata.

//

== Definition of a Machine in KLE

The following information fully defines a Machine:

Parameters::
+
--
These include:

* the _block size_ `b`, the bit length of the basic unit the Machine processes, usually the block size of its main underlying primitive;
* the _key size_ `k`, in bits, for Machines that hold a key;
* the _granularity_, usually equal to `b`. “Last blocks”, such as those of the AD or of the plaintext or ciphertext, may be shorter than `b`. Where the length of such a block is set explicitly, a longer input that carries it is truncated: its excess most significant bits are ignored; and
* any other parameter needed to instantiate the Machine with compatible primitives.
--

Data Structures::
+
--
These include:

. *Provisioning Input*:
its fields, in the order in which they appear in the PI,
each with its length.
Every table ends with a zero Padding to a multiple of 128 bits, of a size `pad` as defined for the Serialized Content; provisioning ignores the padding bits.
+
Every PI starts with the MDH, at position i.
The *Provisioning Input* tables of the Machines omit it and start at position ii:
+
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|   i  | 128           | MDH
|===

. *Internal State*:
the information kept in the internal state of a CC, needed to define the Machine and its semantics.
It must exist internally, but its representation and format are not prescribed.
The Internal State always contains the MDH.
A key field of the Internal State holds the key itself, even when the PI or the SCC carries a System Key Identifier in its place (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-system-keys>>).

. *Serialized Content*:
the plaintext of the `Content1` of the SCC:
its fields, in the order in which they appear in an SCC, each with its length.
It does not include the MDH, and ends with a zero Padding to a multiple of 128 bits; a size `pad` is (−_s_) mod 128 bits, _s_ being the total size of the Positions before it, with the sizes in effect.
In the formulas for `pad`, `kf` is the size of the key field: `k`, or 64 when it holds a System Key Identifier.
The Machine may designate public fields, which it places first and an SCC carries in clear; their number of whole 128-bit blocks is `SCC_ClearLen1` (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-scc-clear-blocks>>), zero where the Machine designates none.
--

Machine-Specific Functions::
Any other function specific to the Machine.

State Machine::
+
--
The state machine is defined by:

. the list of *States* the Machine may assume, each with its representation as <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-State-field>> specifies;
. the complete list of *allowed state transitions*, with the prerequisites of each, the native `kl.setst` Form and the meaning of any auxiliary parameter to it, and any other condition that triggers a transition without a `kl.setst`; and
. the *allowed* or *expected* instructions in each State, and their semantics.
  Besides configuration and export sequences, a State typically permits only certain Forms of `kl.exec` and `kl.setst`, and may be an endpoint of `kl.derive` (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>).
With `Zklio`, a substitution may replace any native Form of `kl.exec`, however the Machine words it
(<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-usage-input-output>>, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-substitution>>):
a Form D `kl.exec` replaces a Form A, B or C `kl.exec`.
A `kl.setst` into a State for which the Machine names no Form is of Form A.
Where a State expects a Form B or D `kl.setst`, it also admits Form A with a zero operand; Rule <<KLE-MGR-form-D-where-C>> gives the alternatives to Form C for short inputs.
A substitution issued without the required KLIOBUF setup fails as Rules <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-iobuf-configured>> and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-substitution-failures>> state.
Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-form-mismatch>> defines a wrong Form.
`kl.input` and `kl.output` access no locker, so the state machine does not restrict them.
Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-outcome-valid-state>> states the behavior precisely.
--

//

The informative examples in <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-examples>> use vector Forms to illustrate the Machines.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-algo-notation]]
=== Notation in the Machine Descriptions

The following nomenclature is specific to the Machine specifications:

* `INPUT` is the input data of `kl.exec` or `kl.setst`: the input vector register,
  the 128-bit GPR operand of a Form D `kl.setst`, the `Xs` of a Form B `kl.setst` that a State admits in place of Form C, zero-extended to 128 bits, or the KLIOBUF (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-usage-input-output>>).

* `OUTPUT` is the output data of `kl.exec`: the output
  vector register, or the KLIOBUF (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-usage-input-output>>).

* `Xs` is the auxiliary operand of a Form B `kl.setst`: a 64-bit value, in one GPR on RV64 and in the pair `X[s+1] @ X[s]` on RV32 (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-multiple-GPRs>>). A Form A `kl.setst` is a Form B one with `Xs` = 0.
* `KLLEN` is the operand length in bits: `vl` {times} `SEW` for a vector operand, and
  `kliobuftop` {times} 8 for a KLIOBUF operand, and 128 for the GPR operand of a Form D `kl.setst`, or for the zero-extended `Xs` of a Form B one. `kliobuftop`, like every
  other KLIOBUF CSR and `klstart`, is a number of bytes.
  For a KLIOBUF operand, `kliobuftop` plays the role of `VL`, and the transfer
  window is bytes [0, `kliobuftop`-1] (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-CSR-kliobuftop>>).
  For either operand of `kl.exec`, `klstart` plays the role of `vstart`: it is the byte
  position within the transfer window at which processing starts or resumes
  (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-CSR-klstart>>).
  `kl.setst` does not honor `klstart`: it reads its whole operand.

* `enc_blk(K, p) -> c` and `dec_blk(K, c) -> p`: the encryption of block `p`, resp. the decryption of block `c`, under key `K` by the block cipher of the Machine.

* `trunc(V, n)`, for 0 {le} `n` {le} `b` and a value `V` of at least `n` bits: the `b`-bit value `zeros(b-n) @ V[n-1:0]`, which keeps the `n` least significant bits of `V` and ignores the others; `trunc(V, b)` = `V` for a `b`-bit `V`.

* `pad10(X, n)`: for `n` a multiple of 8 with 0 {le} `n` < `b`, the `b`-bit value holding the ``n``-bit partial block `X[n-1:0]` followed, in string order, by a single `1` bit and then zeros, `zeros(b-8-n) @ 0b10000000 @ X[n-1:0]`.
  The terminating `1` is the most significant bit of byte `n`/8, not bit `n` of the value: within a byte, the first bit of the string is the most significant (<<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>).
  It is the padding of cite:[RFC7253] {sect}4.2 and cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}6.2.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-Machine-rules]]
== General Rules for Machines

The Machine Rules (*MR*) supplement the General Rules (GR) of <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-unpriv>>, including <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-State-management>>, and apply to every Machine unless stated otherwise.

=== Machine Definition and Checks

[horizontal]
[[KLE-MGR-policy-gate,MR1]]MR1:: _Policy gate and entry conditions._
+
--
Each Machine states, for each of its States:

* the bit of _MachinePolicy_ (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Machine-field>>), if any, that enables its operation; “if encryption is allowed” refers to _MachinePolicy_[0] and “if decryption is allowed” to _MachinePolicy_[1].
A bit gates the States of its operation and every State that follows uniquely from them, its _Gated States_:
** encryption: _Encrypt_, _Enc_Last_Block_, _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ and, where it emits the tag of the encryption, _Hash_Output_;
** decryption: _Decrypt_, _Dec_Last_Block_, _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ and, in <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, <<KLE-OCB-mode>> and <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, _Hash_Verify_;
** signature generation: _Sign_Generate_, _Output_ with a signature, and _Sign_Output_;
** signature verification: _Sign_Input_ and _Sign_Verify_;
** MAC generation: _Hash_Output_ and _Hash_Output_Bits_; MAC verification: _Hash_Verify_; and
* its _entry conditions_: what the State requires, such as the `Has` flags, the mode, and the other configuration of the CC that it uses.

Unless the Machine states otherwise, a zero _MachinePolicy_ is invalid Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>) for a Machine that encrypts and decrypts, or that generates and verifies MACs, and valid for one that generates and verifies signatures: it then disables these operations and leaves the others, if any, available.

<<KLE-GCM-with-IV-mode>>, <<KLE-OCB-with-nonce-mode>> and <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-wsn>>, which carry their IV or nonce in the PI, admit only _MachinePolicy_ = `0b10`: they only decrypt.
Any other value is invalid Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>), and a narrowing to it transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-policy-narrowing-state>>).

A `kl.exec`, `kl.derive`, or `kl.setst` that is a usage instruction (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-properties>>) is checked:

. *At issue, against _MachinePolicy_.* The locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_, and the instruction performs no operation, if the instruction:
.. is issued in a State whose operation _MachinePolicy_ disables, or from which every operation that the Machine can complete is disabled, unless Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>> admits it or it is a `kl.setst` naming a State that still leads to an enabled operation; or
.. would enter a State whose operation _MachinePolicy_ disables, by naming it or on its completion (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>).
. *After it acts, against the entry conditions of the State that it enters.* They are checked after the operations of leaving the current State, on the content that these and the instruction produce (such as the last part of the IV that the completing `kl.exec` of <<KLE-GCM-mode>> writes); if they fail, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ instead of entering the State.

Within a State, an instruction checks a condition only if it can change in that State or the check is one of Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>. The Machine states the check with the instruction; a failed check transitions the locker to _Invalid_ unless it detects a data error (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>).
--

[[KLE-MGR-config-data-error,MR2]]MR2:: _Configuration errors and data errors._
Each Machine states which of its checks detect a configuration error, and which a data error:

* A _configuration error_ transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_. It is either:
** a field that sets a parameter or a key of the CC, not the data of an operation, with a value that the referenced standard rejects, such as a malformed private key; or
** a field (a peer point or a signature included) whose encoding violates a constraint that is checked when the field is written, such as the zero most significant bits of secp521r1 (<<KLE-ECC>>).
A Machine may classify such a check as a data error instead, as <<KLE-PQC-ML-KEM>> does for the input checks of cite:[nist-fips-203] on `encapsk` and `ciphertext`.
A constraint that the standard checks only when an operation decodes the value, such as the hint encoding of an ML-DSA `signature` (cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 21), is a data error detected by that operation.
* A _data error_ transitions the locker to _Failure_, a Valid State. It is either:
** a ciphertext, tag, signature, message representative or public key that is malformed, does not verify, or fails an input check that the Machine classifies as a data error, such as the check of an ML-KEM encapsulation key; or
** a point an operation uses, such as a peer's point, a public key or a generator, that is not a valid point of the curve.

[[KLE-MGR-check-on-write,MR3]]MR3:: _Checks of a field._
A check that the Machine attaches to a field and that depends on that field alone is made whenever the field is written: at the completion of its load (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>), of a `kl.derive` into it, of the provisioning that carries it, and of an import that restores it.
Examples are the zero most significant bits of secp521r1, the well-formedness of an ML-DSA `privkey`, and the input checks of cite:[nist-fips-203] on an ML-KEM key.
The check need not be repeated when the field is read, since the KLF guarantees the integrity of the locker (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-KLF>>); a Machine may repeat it on a value that an interrupted operation resumes with, as secp521r1 does (<<KLE-ECC>>), and a repeated check that fails transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
+
A check that depends on the operation or on more than one field is made by the operation that reads the field, before it changes any state.
Examples are the validity of a point in the role that the operation gives it, the range of `Scalar` against the group order, and the consistency of a public key with a private key.
Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>> classifies the failure of any check other than a repeated one.

[[KLE-MGR-sysfmt-standard,MR4]]MR4:: _System-specific import._
A system-specific import never circumvents the referenced standard of the Machine (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-diligence>>).
The converted Content undergoes the checks of Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>, and a field that the Machine derives from another is derived from the converted Content, or checked against it, never taken from the format unchecked.
A Machine whose Provisioning Input does not carry a field that a format may configure states how the import checks and marks that field, as <<KLE-PQC-ML-KEM>> and <<KLE-PQC-ML-DSA>> do.

=== Operands, Outputs and Fields

[horizontal]
[[KLE-MGR-form-D-where-C,MR5]]MR5:: _Form of a `kl.setst` with a short `INPUT`._
Where a State expects a Form C `kl.setst` whose `INPUT` is at most 128 bits, a Form D one is admitted too, as is a Form A one, whose `INPUT` is a 128-bit zero.
A Form B one is admitted only where the State says so, for an integer `INPUT` of at most 64 bits.

[[KLE-MGR-granularity,MR6]]MR6:: _Granularity._ The Machine constrains `KLLEN` in each State: whole-block transfers may require a multiple of the block size; a final block or piece requires `KLLEN` {ge} `last_block_len`.
Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>> governs fixed-width inputs; the Machine defines the constraint on a truncated tag.
On a violation, the instruction performs no operation and the locker enters Error State _Invalid_, with the output clearing of Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-outcomes>>.
For a `kl.exec` with a transfer window, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-transfer-window-checks>> orders these checks after State admission.

[[KLE-MGR-recomputed-fields,MR7]]MR7:: A field that a Machine can derive inexpensively from other fields of the same CC may be omitted from the Serialized Content.
It is then recomputed when an import completes, by the `kl.mgmt` with `#kl_cfg_management_end`, whatever the format (a system-specific one included, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-completion>>), and whenever a field it depends on is modified, for instance by a `kl.derive` into a key field or an explicit operation of the Machine.
The Machine states only the derivation, and marks the field “(derived)” in its *Internal State*.

[[KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input,MR8]]MR8:: A State consuming a single value of fixed width from `INPUT` uses that many least significant bits and ignores any excess.
The width is that of its assigned field or defined operand, which may differ from `b`; a smaller `KLLEN` violates the granularity (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).

[[KLE-MGR-clear-output,MR9]]MR9:: A State writes `OUTPUT` only within its window, bits [8 {times} `klstart`, `KLLEN` − 1] (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-outcomes>>).
A State that writes fewer bits than the window holds clears the rest.
An output longer than `KLLEN` is truncated to its `KLLEN` least significant bits, unless emitted over several `kl.exec`, as a long field (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>), digest or XOF output.
A `KLLEN` below the Machine's minimum violates the granularity (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>); the tail beyond `VL` follows <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-KLV>>.

[[KLE-MGR-single-last-block,MR10]]MR10:: _Final fractional block._
A Machine processes a final fractional block in a dedicated _final-block State_, such as _Enc_Last_Block_, _Dec_Last_Block_ or _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, as follows unless it defines otherwise.
The check order is that of Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-outcome-valid-state>>.
+
--
. The caller determines the final block's bit length `n` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
. If `n` = 0, no `kl.exec` is needed: a `kl.setst` into the final-block State with `n` = 0 (`Xs` = 0, or Form A) records `last_block_len` = 0 and performs the operations of the final block on empty input; where the Machine has a single successor of the final-block State, the locker enters it at once (item 3), unless the Machine defines otherwise.
A `kl.setst` from the State preceding the final-block State to its successor, bypassing the final-block State, is equivalent to this and must be of Form A, unless the Machine names another Form, as <<KLE-GCM-mode>> does; where `n` is determined by the Machine (`max_len` != 0 in <<KLE-process-VLI>>) and is not zero, the bypass transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. If `n` != 0, a `kl.setst` into the final-block State records `last_block_len` = `n`.
A single `kl.exec` processes that fixed-width input (Rules <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>> and <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>); an empty window does not reach the Machine (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-transfer-window-checks>>).
Completion enters the successor that the Machine names, without a `kl.setst`.
. Where software chooses among several successors, as for the digest of a hash function (_Hash_Output_, _Hash_Output_Bits_ or _Hash_Verify_, which may be gated separately by Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>), or the entry of the successor takes an operand that the final-block State cannot supply, as the length block of _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ and _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ in <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, the Machine names no successor: the locker stays in the final-block State once the block is processed, or on entering it with `n` = 0, and software selects the successor with a `kl.setst`.
A State whose final block is empty or already processed (`last_block_len` = 0) admits no `kl.exec` of Forms A to C, whatever its transfer window, even an empty one: one transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. A `kl.setst`, other than one naming _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>), leaving the final-block State with `n` != 0 before its `kl.exec` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_, unless the Machine defines otherwise.
--
+
A Machine may require a native Form D `kl.exec`, which has no transfer window, as _Enc_Last_Block_ and _Dec_Last_Block_ of <<KLE-OCB-mode>> do: with `n` = 0 the locker stays in the State until that `kl.exec`, and item 2 does not apply.
New Machines follow this Rule: <<KLE-OCB-mode>> and <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>> illustrate a single successor, and <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>> a choice.
+
NOTE: A second final block is not a continuation of the input but an attempt to manipulate the internal state of the Machine.

[[KLE-MGR-last-blk-len,MR11]]MR11::
+
--
A `last_block_len`, or any other parameter giving the bit length of a final input piece, is a multiple of 8, unless the Machine either:

* states that its referenced standard orders the bits within a byte as <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>> does, so that a truncation of the last byte to its least significant bits is the bit string that the standard defines (as for <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>); or
* defines which bits of the partial final byte carry the piece (as for the nonce of <<KLE-OCB-mode>>).

A value that the Machine does not admit transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
Whether zero is admissible depends on the Machine.
--

[[KLE-MGR-tag-verify,MR12]]MR12:: _Verification of a tag._
A Machine that verifies a tag or a MAC without disclosing the value it computes does so in constant time, and transitions to _Success_ if the verification succeeds and to _Failure_ otherwise (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>).
The instruction that provides the tag or MAC to verify performs the verification. It is:
+
--
* by default, a single Form B `kl.exec` in _Hash_Verify_, which supplies the expected value and compares the number of bits that the Machine holds in `tag_len`;
** a Form B `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Verify_ sets `tag_len`, as for CMAC, GCM, HMAC and KMAC (<<KLE-CMAC-mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, <<KLE-HMAC>>, <<KLE-KMAC>>);
** or `tag_len` was set earlier and the Machine enters _Hash_Verify_ by itself, as OCB and Ascon-AEAD128 do (<<KLE-OCB-mode>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>);
* where the expected value is supplied first, as the SIV of GCM-SIV is (<<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>>), the `kl.exec` that completes the internal tag computation.
--
+
<<KLE-CMAC-mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-with-IV-mode>>, <<KLE-HMAC>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-wsn>> and <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-N-masking>> compare a prefix of the tag or MAC.
Every verification of a locker of these Machines compares the same number of bits, `tag_len_p`, which the _AuxInfo_ field of its MDH holds (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-MachineUse>>):
+
--
* `tag_len_p` is one of the tag lengths that the Machine admits, and the full length if _KeyType_ = 1: 128 for GCM and Ascon-AEAD128, `b` for CMAC and `t` for HMAC.
A provisioning or import whose _AuxInfo_ holds another value completes into Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
* Provisioning keeps the _AuxInfo_ of the PI, and entering _Ready_ leaves it unchanged; these Machines define all its bits, so `kl.clearads` and an import without ADS leave it unchanged too.
* A locker whose `tag_len_p` is shorter than the full length is not a `kl.derive` destination (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>).
* A `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Verify_ whose resulting `tag_len` differs from `tag_len_p` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
* In Ascon-AEAD128 and its variants, entering _Ready_ sets `tag_len` {leftarrow} `tag_len_p`, and the _Set_Aux_Value_ operation transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ if its `Xs` differs from `tag_len_p`.
* GCM and CMAC output exactly `tag_len_p` bits and require `KLLEN` {ge} `tag_len_p` (Rules <<KLE-MGR-granularity>> and <<KLE-MGR-clear-output>>); Ascon-AEAD128 and its variants output `tag_len` = `tag_len_p` bits.
HMAC, which also serves as a pseudorandom function, outputs as many bits as the caller chooses for each message; a caller that uses them as MACs keeps one length per key (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
--

=== Multi-block Operations

[horizontal]
[[KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block,MR13]]MR13:: _Single- and Multi-block `kl.exec`._
In a State that processes whole blocks of `b` bits, one `kl.exec` applies the per-block operation of the State `KLLEN`/`b` times, for `i` = 0, `b`, `2b`, {ellipsis}, `KLLEN` − `b` in that order, consuming `INPUT[i+b-1:i]` if the State takes an input and producing `OUTPUT[i+b-1:i]` if it produces an output.
Blocks are taken and produced from the least significant position upward, here and throughout this specification.
A resumed `kl.exec` performs only the iterations from `i` = 8 {times} `klstart` onwards (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-CSR-klstart>>).
+
A State that the Machine describes as processing a single block, such as a last-block, tag-finalize or verify State, or XEX _Decrypt_ during ciphertext stealing (<<KLE-XEX-XTS-modes>>), processes exactly one block whatever `KLLEN` is, unless its transfer window is invalid (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>) or empty (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-transfer-window-checks>>).
Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>> governs a further `kl.exec` in a last-block State.
The `last_block_state` of <<KLE-process-VLI>> instead processes exactly its final piece, which may first complete the current block.
The only interruption point of a `kl.exec` in such a State, or of one that processes a final piece, is 0 (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-interruption-point>>).

[[KLE-MGR-load-long-field,MR14]]MR14:: _Long fields transferred by several `kl.exec`._
A State _X_ that loads a long field from `INPUT`, or emits one to `OUTPUT`, over several `kl.exec` names a state field _W_ that records the amount transferred so far, in bits or bytes.
Where _W_ is a field of the MDH, such as _MachineUse_, `kl.rstr*` cannot write it (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-restrict>>).
A digest or XOF output that a State emits from its internal state is not a long field: its Machine defines the progress and the outcome of an excess `KLLEN`, as <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>> does.

. *Entry.* Entering _X_ zeroes _W_; entering a loading State also zeroes the field.
. *Progress.* _W_ rises by the amount transferred: for a `kl.exec` that completes, by `KLLEN` − 8 {times} `klstart` bits or `KLLEN`/8 − `klstart` bytes, up to the size of the field; for one that halts, by its committed prefix (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-precise-halt>>), so that its resumption adds only the remainder.
. *Completion.* The transfer is complete once _W_ reaches the size of the field.
  Completion does not reset _W_; a transition that the Machine then makes zeroes it.
  Unless the Machine defines another transition, an emitting State transitions to _Success_, and a loading State admits no further `kl.exec` (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-outcome-valid-state>>).
. *Excess.* Excess data in the final load is ignored; an emitting `kl.exec` that would carry _W_ past the field size transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. *Incomplete load.* A `kl.setst` that leaves a loading State _X_ before the load is complete leaves the field unconfigured: it zeroes the field and any length of it set on entering _X_, and leaves its flag, if any, clear.
  A same-State `kl.setst` (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>) or `kl.clearads` restarts the load: as on entering _X_, it zeroes _W_ and the field, and clears its flag, if any.
  A Machine whose next State needs the field states this as an entry condition (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
. *Flags.* A Machine may keep in _StateExtension_ a flag per loaded field that records a configured value, such as the `Has` flags of the ECC, ML-KEM and ML-DSA Machines and the `HasKey` flag of HMAC.
  Unless the Machine states otherwise, entering the loading State clears the flag, and a load that completes and passes the checks of Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>, or an operation of the Machine that computes the field, sets it; `kl.getstx` reads it.
  Each Machine lists its flags, their bit positions and any departure from this pattern.
. *`PROCESS_VLI`.* A load by `PROCESS_VLI` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>) passes `max_len` = the size of the field and `cumul_len` = _W_.
  A field stored as loaded also passes `block` = `state`, `b` = `n` = its size, `mode` = `assign` and `process_block` = `finalize` = `None`; a field processed as loaded, such as the IV of <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, passes the functions and `mode` that its Machine states.

[[KLE-MGR-caller-limits,MR15]]MR15:: A Machine enforces only the per-message limits that its correctness needs at practical lengths and that it can track from its own state, such as the 32-bit counters of <<KLE-GCM-mode>> and <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>> and the bound on the `L[i]` array of <<KLE-OCB-mode>>.
A Machine also fixes the verification length of each locker (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>).
The unified AEAD interface counts and enforces the per-message input limits of its selected standard (<<KLE-unified-AEAD>>).
The caller keeps every other limit:
+
--
* for an interface other than <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>, the length of a message is limited by the referenced standard, such as the 2^20^ blocks of a data unit of cite:[nist-SP-800-38E], and by the width of a field that records it, such as the 64 bits of `cumul_len`, a width that no message reaches in practice; beyond the latter limit, the digest or tag is unspecified, but computed from the Content and the message alone and not otherwise secret Content (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-no-extraction>>);
* the use of a key across messages is limited as the referenced standard states, for instance in the total data processed, the number of failed verifications, or a parameter fixed for the lifespan of the key; the limit applies across every locker and SCC that holds the key, including its copies by `kl.clone` and its re-imports;
* the uniqueness of a nonce, IV, counter or tag mask, as the referenced standard requires: no Machine prevents its reuse, whether by re-importing an earlier SCC, by `kl.clone` or by returning to _Ready_; a Machine that carries its IV or nonce in the PI only decrypts (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
--

=== Operations and Transitions

[horizontal]
[[KLE-operation-immediates,MR16]]MR16:: A Machine may declare a `kl.setst` an _operation_ if it names a constant of <<KLE-state-constants-symmetric>> or a Machine-defined immediate, 66–127 (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-setst>>).
An operation performs the stated action and leaves _State_ unchanged; it changes _StateExtension_ only where the Machine states so.
It is not a transition: it performs none of the operations of leaving or entering a State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-management-no-state-operations>>), and Rules <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>, <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>> and <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>> do not treat it as one.
The Machine states the States in which the operation is allowed, and its Form; in any other State, a constant of <<KLE-state-constants-symmetric>> names a State, and a Machine-defined immediate is not admitted (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-setst-not-admitted>>).
No Machine declares an operation in a State of Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>>.
_Set_Aux_Value_, for instance, is an operation in <<KLE-keystream-modes>>, <<KLE-tweakable>> and <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, and a State in <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>> and <<KLE-OCB-mode>>; `kl_xex_state_steal` is an operation in _Decrypt_ of <<KLE-XEX-XTS-modes>>.

[[KLE-MGR-management-no-state-operations,MR17]]MR17:: State entry, exit and change operations apply only to transitions between Valid States made by usage instructions, including a `kl.setst` from _Ready_ to _Ready_, which performs _Ready_ entry operations.
Management moves into or out of Configuration States, and restoration of a saved State at export or import completion, perform none of them.
These events also perform _Ready_ entry operations:
+
--
* the completion of a provisioning, or of a system-specific import unless its format defines other initialization operations in their place (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-completion>>); and
* a `kl.derive` that writes a key in _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-checks>> item 6).
--

[[KLE-MGR-machine-transitions,MR18]]MR18:: _Transitions that a Machine makes._
A Machine may make a transition by itself: on completing a `kl.exec`, or the part of a `kl.derive` that acts at an endpoint as that `kl.exec` would (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-derive-rule-both-fixed-size>>), or within a `kl.setst` that names another State.
Such a transition is made only that way: no source State allows a `kl.setst` naming its destination (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-setst-not-admitted>>), unless the destination is _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>) or the Machine lists that `kl.setst`.

=== Randomness

[horizontal]
[[KLE-MGR-atomic-randomness,MR19]]MR19:: A Machine operation that needs random bits from an RBG or DRBG, such as the generation of a nonce, an ephemeral scalar or a signature into the locker, is an instruction of its own.
That instruction works only on the locker and on scalar operands captured at issue, transfers no data to or from memory, vector registers, the KLIOBUF or another locker, and may be long-running (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-no-data>>).
If the RBG or DRBG cannot supply the bits, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-rbg-failure>> applies.
+
An instruction that transfers data never waits or fails for lack of random bits, even when resumed, restored or restarted; further randomness that its Machine needs comes from another instruction.
+
Randomness that the implementation needs, such as masks, is supplied in advance, without weakening the required protection.
+
An internal algorithmic random value, such as a signing nonce or an ephemeral scalar, serves only the one instance of the scheme that requires it, and no other instance or purpose.
It is never reused, not even when an interrupted operation restarts (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>>).
Resuming copies of one interrupted operation, restored from one image, completes that one instance on the same inputs and is not a reuse.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-truncation-vs-length]]
== Input and Output Padding, Truncation, Byte Granularity

Unless the Machine specifies otherwise and pads internally, the caller must pad an input whose length the granularity of the State does not admit to a `KLLEN` that the Machine admits, and pass its actual length to the Machine, typically through `kl.setst`.

Cryptographic inputs are normally supplied through vector registers or the KLIOBUF, in whole bytes. For a standard that permits arbitrary bit lengths, the caller specifies the bit length of the final input piece, usually as a `kl.setst` parameter, and the Machine truncates that input to it. The Machine clears the output bits beyond the final piece, also within its last byte (Rule <<KLE-MGR-clear-output>>).

This occurs, for instance, with the `last_block_len` parameters in <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>>, <<KLE-OCB-mode>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, and <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>. Such a parameter gives the length to which the Machine truncates the passed input, not the length `KLLEN` of that input. Macro `PROCESS_VLI` receives field lengths in bits, but `kl.exec` always transfers whole bytes, and the last input may be truncated internally.

Where a standard's formula requires the bit count of data that the Machine receives in whole bytes, it is the byte count multiplied by 8, as with the length block of GHASH in <<KLE-GCM-mode>>.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

// [[KLE-DRBG]]
// == Deterministic Random Bit Generator
//
// This Machine defines a deterministic random bit generator (DRBG) that can be used to generate random numbers.
// It must be based on a NIST SP 800-90A Rev. 1 cite:[nist-SP-800-90-r1] and correspond to a Security Strength
// of 256 bits.
//
// The *Provisioning Input* consists only of the MDH.
//
// The *Internal State* may contain all the necessary data for the DRBG to function.
//
// The *Serialized Content* consists only of the MDH.
//
// State Machine::
//
// * *States:*
//  ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
//
// * *Allowed State Transitions:*
//  ** None.
//
// * *Behavior:*
//  ** When the locker is provisioned or an SCC is imported into it, the internal state of the Machine obtains new true random bits from the underlying RNG and is accordingly initialized.
//  ** This is the only algorithm that allows ``kl.exec`` instructions in State _Ready_.
//     In State _Ready_, (multi-block) ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected to be of Form C.
//     "``kl.exec OUTPUT, Kn``" produces KLLEN bytes of pseudo-random bits.
//
// Since the SCC of the DRBG Machine consists of the MDH only, and the DRBG is reset at each import,
// the export and import routines are the same as for lockers in an Error State, i.e., there is no authentication.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-states-symmetric]]
== Symmetric Cryptography

This table, with <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-state-off>>, <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-states-valid>> and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-stateexts-error>>,
defines the States of the symmetric Machines.
A Machine may override these values and says so where it does; the public-key Machines define their own mnemonics.

[[KLE-state-constants-symmetric]]
.Common Constants defining CC states for Symmetric Machines
[float="center",align="center",width="100%",cols="^7%,<30%,<17%,<46%",options="header"]
|===
| Value | Mnemonic for the Constant        | State Name          | Description
.2+| 2  |  `kl_state_operate`              |  _Operate_          | Generic operational state.
<|  `kl_state_hash_absorb`                 | _Hash_Absorb_       | (Same value as _Operate_, with a mnemonic more intuitive for hash functions.)
Absorb data: the message of a hash function, MAC or XOF, or the associated data of an AEAD scheme; on the encryption path of <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>>, also the plaintext. On entering the State, the Machine may initialize its tag or MAC computation.
|   3   |  `kl_state_hash_last_block`      | _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_   | Absorb a final input piece whose length the `kl.setst` sets, such as the last partial block of the associated data or of a MAC input, or the final piece of a hashed message, of any bit length that the standard admits.
|   4   |  `kl_state_hash_finalize`        | _Hash_Finalize_     | Finalize the internal state of a hash/tag function.
|   5   |  `kl_state_hash_verify`          | _Hash_Verify_       | Compare the internal hash/tag value to the provided one.
|   6   |  `kl_state_hash_output`          | _Hash_Output_       | Enter the hash output phase of a hash/tag function.
|   7   |  `kl_state_encrypt`              | _Encrypt_           | Perform encryption, and any associated tag computation.
|   8   |  `kl_state_decrypt`              | _Decrypt_           | Perform decryption, and any associated tag computation.
|   9   |  `kl_state_enc_last_block`       | _Enc_Last_Block_    | Process the last block separately, on the encryption path of an authenticated encryption mode.
|  10   |  `kl_state_dec_last_block`       | _Dec_Last_Block_    | Process the last block separately, on the decryption path of an authenticated encryption mode.
|  11   |  `kl_state_enc_tag_finalize`     | _Enc_Tag_Finalize_  | Finalize the tag computation for AE mode encryption.
|  12   |  `kl_state_dec_tag_finalize`     | _Dec_Tag_Finalize_  | Finalize the internal tag computation for AE mode decryption; where the expected tag was supplied first, as in <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>>, also verify it (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>).
|  13   |  `kl_state_set_aux_value`        | _Set_Aux_Value_     | Set a public/auxiliary value in a cryptographic scheme implemented by the Machine.
|  14   |  `kl_state_set_aux_value_2`      | _Set_Aux_Value_2_   | Set a second public/auxiliary value in a cryptographic scheme implemented by the Machine.
|  15   |  `kl_state_set_key`              | _Set_Key_           | Reconfigure a key field.
|  16   |  `kl_state_hash_output_bits`     | _Hash_Output_Bits_  | Output the hash a set number of bits at a time.
|  17   |  `kl_state_aead_prepass`         | _AEAD_Prepass_      | Read plaintext before the encryption pass of an AEAD Machine that requires it.
|  18   |  `kl_state_aead_prepass_last`    | _AEAD_Prepass_Last_ | Process the final piece of that plaintext prepass.
|  19   |  `kl_state_aead_ct_prepass`      | _AEAD_CT_Prepass_   | Read ciphertext before the decryption pass of an AEAD Machine that requires it.
|  20   |  `kl_state_aead_ct_prepass_last` | _AEAD_CT_Prepass_Last_ | Process the final piece of that ciphertext prepass.
|===

Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>> applies to the Machines that use
<<KLE-state-constants-symmetric>> and to every Machine-defined immediate; Rules <<KLE-MGR-management-no-state-operations>> to <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>
apply to every Machine.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-unified-AEAD]]
=== AEAD Interface

This interface defines processing for GCM, GCM-SIV, OCB and Ascon-AEAD128, including the preset-nonce and nonce-masking variants that each Machine defines.
It supersedes the AEAD state transitions, input handling, final-block handling and caller-supplied length blocks in the following Machine sections.
Those sections continue to define the selected algorithm's primitive, key and nonce requirements, tag length and cryptographic functions.
Where a following AEAD section describes a conflicting State transition, caller padding or caller-supplied length block, this section governs.

The interface keeps the standard algorithm, tag, nonce and key requirements of the selected Machine.
It changes only how software supplies associated data (AD), plaintext (PT) and ciphertext (CT), and moves their final padding and length encodings into the Machine.
PT and CT name the meaningful plaintext and ciphertext bit strings, not their transport buffers.

[cols="<22%,<22%,<20%,<18%,<18%",options="header"]
|===
| Machine profiles | Key and primitive | Nonce | Tag length | Passes
| GCM | AES-128, AES-192, AES-256 or SM4 | Supplied IV, in the existing GCM range | Existing GCM `tag_len_p` values | One PT, one CT
| GCM with Preset IV | AES-128, AES-192, AES-256 or SM4 | Preset 96-bit IV; decryption only | Existing GCM `tag_len_p` values | One CT
| GCM-SIV | AES-128 or AES-256 | Supplied 96-bit nonce | 128 bits | Two PT, one CT
| OCB | AES-128, AES-192 or AES-256 | Supplied 6- to 120-bit nonce | 64, 96 or 128 bits | One PT, one CT
| OCB with Preset Nonce | AES-128, AES-192 or AES-256 | Preset 6- to 120-bit nonce; decryption only | 64, 96 or 128 bits | One CT
| Ascon-AEAD128 | Ascon | Supplied 128-bit nonce | Existing Ascon `tag_len_p` values | One PT, one CT
| Ascon-AEAD128 with Preset Nonce | Ascon | Preset 128-bit nonce; decryption only | Existing Ascon `tag_len_p` values | One CT
| Ascon-AEAD128 Nonce Masking | Ascon | Supplied 128-bit nonce, masked internally | Existing Ascon `tag_len_p` values | One PT, one CT
|===

The supplied-nonce setup, AD phase, payload phase and completion below apply to every row.
The preset-nonce rows begin at the AD phase and retain their existing decryption-only _MachinePolicy_.

Parameters::

* `b` = 128 is the transfer block size for every current unified AEAD Machine.
* `glog` is the base-2 logarithm of the meaningful-input granularity in bits. It is 3 for GCM, GCM-SIV and OCB, which take byte strings, and 0 for Ascon-AEAD128, which takes bit strings. `glog` = 7 would denote 128-bit meaningful inputs; `glog` = 8 would denote 256-bit inputs.
* The Machine's existing `tag_len_p` continues to determine the tag length. It remains in _AuxInfo_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>).

Data Structures::
+
--
The Unified AEAD Content begins with two public 128-bit blocks:

[cols="^8%,<17%,<75%",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Field | Description
| i | `ad_len`, `data_len` | Unsigned 64-bit lengths, in meaningful bits, of the AD and PT or CT committed in this message.
| ii | `replay_len`, `nonce_len`, `nonce_done`, `last_block_len`, `flags`, Padding | `replay_len` is the number of PT bits accepted by the encryption replay of a two-pass Machine. `nonce_len` and `nonce_done` record a resumable nonce load. The remaining padding bits are zero.
|===

`flags` records, in order, that the nonce, received tag, AD final block, prepass final block and generated SIV are present.
All counters reset on entering _Ready_. They increase only when a `kl.exec` commits meaningful input; they exclude ignored transport bits, zero fill, cryptographic padding, tags and Machine-generated length blocks.
The remaining Content is the private state of the selected Machine, including the received tag and any saved authentication state.
The two public blocks precede it and therefore contribute two blocks to `SCC_ClearLen1`; SCC authentication binds them before an import restores them.

An import rejects a nonzero reserved bit, an inconsistent flag, a counter outside the selected standard's bound, `replay_len` > `data_len`, or a State inconsistent with the saved progress.
Each selected Machine enforces its standard per-message limits as the counters advance.
Rules <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>> still assign nonce uniqueness, aggregate use of a key and failed-verification limits to the caller.
--

State Machine::
+
--
Every AEAD Machine uses a subset of these States:

[cols="<27%,<73%",options="header"]
|===
| State | Purpose
| _Set_Aux_Value_ | Load a supplied nonce; a preset-nonce Machine omits it.
| _Hash_Absorb_, _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ | Process AD, then its final piece.
| _Set_Aux_Value_2_ | Load the received tag before decryption, then return to _Hash_Absorb_.
| _AEAD_Prepass_, _AEAD_Prepass_Last_ | Read PT without output when `TwoPTPasses` is set.
| _AEAD_CT_Prepass_, _AEAD_CT_Prepass_Last_ | Read CT without output when `TwoCTPasses` is set.
| _Encrypt_, _Enc_Last_Block_; _Decrypt_, _Dec_Last_Block_ | Process PT or CT and its final piece.
| _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ | Produce, respectively verify, the tag.
|===

* *Admissible State Transitions:* Each Machine admits only the paths that use its State subset.
 ** Encryption: _Ready_ -> { _Set_Aux_Value_ -> } _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_. A Machine with `TwoPTPasses` then takes _AEAD_Prepass_ -> _AEAD_Prepass_Last_ -> _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Encrypt_; every other Machine enters _Encrypt_ directly.
 ** Encryption completes _Encrypt_ -> _Enc_Last_Block_ -> _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Success_.
 ** Decryption: _Ready_ -> { _Set_Aux_Value_ -> } _Set_Aux_Value_2_ -> _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_. A Machine with `TwoCTPasses` then takes _AEAD_CT_Prepass_ -> _AEAD_CT_Prepass_Last_ -> _Decrypt_; every other Machine enters _Decrypt_ directly.
 ** Decryption completes _Decrypt_ -> _Dec_Last_Block_ -> _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
--

Whole AD blocks use Form B and whole PT/CT blocks Form A. A final-piece State takes its meaningful length from Form B and, when nonzero, one `kl.exec`; it ignores transport bits above that length, zero-fills, and applies the algorithm's padding. The Machine forms every required length block itself. A zero-length piece completes its final action. A Machine rejects every other transition, repeated finalization, or AD after its final piece.

The prepass authenticates its input. A replayed PT or CT is cryptographically bound to that prepass; a changed replay enters _Failure_. Rule <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-SR-unverified-plaintext>> applies to emitted PT until _Success_. If `CiphertextStealing` is set, the final `kl.exec` takes the withheld full block followed by the partial block; its length is at least `b + last_block_len`.

GCM and GCM-SIV therefore form their GHASH or POLYVAL length blocks internally. OCB and Ascon apply their standard final-block rules internally. In particular, Ascon applies no AD padding to empty AD, and applies its extra padded AD block when nonempty AD ends at a block boundary.

`kl.exec` transfers whole bytes. For an Ascon final bit string whose length is not byte aligned, the least significant bits of the final byte are meaningful; the Machine ignores the rest. It does not round the message length upward.

[[KLE-unified-AEAD-info]]
`kl.getinfo` returns the following public descriptor for an AEAD locker:

[cols="^15%,^10%,<75%",options="header"]
|===
| Bits | Name | Meaning
| [3:0] | `glog` | Base-2 logarithm of meaningful-input granularity in bits.
| 4 | `TwoPTPasses` | Set when encryption reads PT once to authenticate it and again to encrypt it.
| 5 | `TwoCTPasses` | Set when decryption requires two CT traversals.
| 6 | `CiphertextStealing` | Set when the AEAD algorithm uses ciphertext stealing.
| [XLEN-1:7] | Reserved | Zero.
|===

All current unified Machines return zero for `TwoCTPasses` and `CiphertextStealing`.
Ordinary final partial blocks in GCM, GCM-SIV, OCB and Ascon are not ciphertext stealing.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-ECB-mode]]
=== Electronic Codebook Mode

ECB mode cite:[nist-SP-800-38A-1] is the simplest way to use a block cipher.
(((KLE, Machine, ECB)))

Parameters::

* `b` = bit length of a block (`b` a multiple of 8), with `b` {ge} 64.
* `k` = bit length of a key (`k` a multiple of 8).
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::
* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64      | `key` or System Key Identifier
| iii  | `pad`          | Padding: (−`kf`) mod 128.
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  i    | `k` or 64      | `key` or System Key Identifier
|  ii   | `pad`         | Padding: (−`kf`) mod 128.
|===
--

Machine-Specific Functions::
`enc_blk` and `dec_blk` (<<KLE-algo-notation>>).

State Machine::

* *States:*
 ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
 ** _Encrypt_: Encrypting blocks of input.
 ** _Decrypt_: Decrypting blocks of input.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Encrypt_, if encryption is allowed.
 ** _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Decrypt_, if decryption is allowed.
 ** _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** The Machine allows a same-State `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>), which changes nothing.
 ** In _Encrypt_ and _Decrypt_, (multi-block) Form A `kl.exec` instructions are expected.
    For each block, `OUTPUT <- enc_blk(key, INPUT)` in _Encrypt_, and `OUTPUT <- dec_blk(key, INPUT)` in _Decrypt_.

//

Code examples (one or eight blocks per `kl.exec`): <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-ECB-encryption>>, <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-ECB-encryption-multi-block>>.

// // ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-chaining]]
=== Chaining/Feedback Modes (CBC, CFB, and OFB)

(((KLE, Machine, CBC))) Cipher Block Chaining (CBC), (((KLE, Machine, CFB))) Cipher Feedback (CFB) and (((KLE, Machine, OFB))) Output Feedback (OFB) cite:[nist-SP-800-38A-1] are defined here for a block cipher of block size `b`.
CFB has a segment of `u` bits.
CBC takes no padding or ciphertext stealing: the caller supplies whole blocks.
CBC and CFB process data; OFB, like CTR (<<KLE-keystream-modes>>), generates a keystream.

Parameters::
* `b` = block size (`b` a multiple of 8), with 64 {le} `b` {le} 128.
* `k` = key size (`k` a multiple of 8).
* `u` = segment size in bits (CFB only), with 0 < `u` {le} `b`, a parameter of the CC held in _AuxInfo_ (see the Behavior below).
* Granularity: `b` bits in CBC and OFB; 8 bits in CFB, where a `KLLEN` below `u` violates it (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).

Data Structures::
* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
| iii  | `pad`         | Padding: (−`kf`) mod 128.
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits.
 ** `IV`: `b` bits, the chaining value: the initialization vector at first; then the last ciphertext block in CBC, the `brev8` of the shift register of cite:[nist-SP-800-38A-1] in CFB (the last bits of the initialization vector followed by the ciphertext segments), and the last keystream block in OFB.
 ** `u`: 8 bits (CFB only), held in _AuxInfo_[7:0] of the MDH, not serialized.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|    i  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
|   ii  | `b`           | `IV`
|  iii  | `pad`         | Padding: (−(`kf` + `b`)) mod 128.
|===
--

Machine-Specific Functions::
`enc_blk` and `dec_blk` (<<KLE-algo-notation>>).

State Machine::

* *States:*
 ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
 ** _Encrypt_ and _Decrypt_ (CBC and CFB): Encrypting, resp. decrypting, blocks (CBC) or segments (CFB) of input.
 ** _Operate_ (OFB): The CC produces a keystream.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** CBC and CFB: _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Encrypt_, if encryption is allowed, and -> _Decrypt_, if decryption is allowed.
 ** OFB: _Ready_ or _Operate_ -> _Operate_, if encryption or decryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Encrypt_, _Decrypt_ or _Operate_ -> _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** When entering _Ready_, `IV` {leftarrow} 0.

 ** In CFB, the segment size is fixed at provisioning: `u` = _AuxInfo_[7:0] (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-MachineUse>>), and _AuxInfo_[15:8] is zero.
    A provisioning or import whose _AuxInfo_ violates this, or has `u` outside 0 < `u` {le} `b`, completes into Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
    Provisioning keeps the _AuxInfo_ of the PI, and no operation changes it: CFB defines all its bits, so entering _Ready_, `kl.clearads` and an import without ADS leave it unchanged.
    With `u` = `b` the Machine is the full-block CFB.

 ** When entering _Encrypt_, _Decrypt_ or _Operate_, `LOAD_FIXED_FIELD(IV, b)` (<<KLE-op-load-fixed-field>>) loads the initialization vector.
    The Machine allows a same-State `kl.setst` into these States (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>), which replaces `IV` and keeps `key`.

 ** In _Encrypt_ and _Decrypt_, (multi-block) Form A `kl.exec` instructions are expected.
    In CBC, for each block, with `INPUT` the block of input:
+
--
[%autowidth,options="header"]
|===
| Mode    | Operation | Semantics
.2+| CBC  | _Encrypt_ | `OUTPUT <- enc_blk(key, INPUT xor IV)`,  `IV <- OUTPUT`
|           _Decrypt_ | `OUTPUT <- dec_blk(key, INPUT) xor IV`, `IV <- INPUT`
|===
--
+
In CFB, `INPUT` is processed in `n` = {lfloor}`KLLEN`/`u`{rfloor} segments of `u` bits; the other bits are ignored, and software shifts them into the next `kl.exec`.
The standard cite:[nist-SP-800-38A-1] numbers bits from the most significant bit of a byte.
For each `j` from 0 to `n`-1, in increasing order, with `C~j~` the ciphertext segment, `Y~j~` in _Encrypt_ and `X~j~` in _Decrypt_:
+
--
. `W <- brev8(IV)`;
. `Q <- brev8(enc_blk(key, IV))[u-1:0]`;
. `X~j~ <- brev8(INPUT)[(j+1)u-1:ju]`;
. `Y~j~ <- X~j~ xor Q`;
. `IV <- brev8(C~j~ @ W[b-1:u])`, with `W[b-1:u]` empty if `u` = `b`; and
. `OUTPUT` = `brev8(Y)`, where `Y` has `Y~j~` in bits `[(j+1)u-1:ju]` and 0 above bit `nu`-1.
--
+
The iterations of Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>> are over the segments; the interruption points (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-interruption-point>>) are the multiples of `lcm(8, u)/8` bytes.
For `u` a multiple of 8 the `brev8` cancel: for each segment, `Q` = `enc_blk(key, IV)[u-1:0]` with the current `IV`, and `IV` {leftarrow} `C~j~ @ IV[b-1:u]`, with `C~j~` the bytes of the segment in `OUTPUT` or `INPUT`.
+
For each block or segment, `INPUT` is read before `OUTPUT` is written, so `OUTPUT` may be computed in place.

 ** In _Operate_ (OFB), (multi-block) Form C `kl.exec` instructions are expected.
    For each block, `IV <- enc_blk(key, IV)` and `OUTPUT <- IV`.
    As a `kl.derive` source (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>), _Operate_ supplies the keystream of whole-block Form C `kl.exec` instructions, discarding the unused bytes of the last block.

NOTE: The decryption of CFB uses `enc_blk` only. With `u` = `b` it is the full-block CFB; `u` = 8 and `u` = 1 are CFB8 and CFB1 of cite:[nist-SP-800-38A-1].
The caller ensures the uniqueness and unpredictability of the `IV` that the standard requires (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>); a Form A `kl.setst` sets it to zero.

Code examples (CBC, CFB): <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-CBC-encryption>>, <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-CBC-encryption-multi-block>>, <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-CFB8-encryption>>.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-keystream-modes]]
=== CTR/XCTR Keystream Generation

In (((KLE, Machine, CTR))) CTR mode, the IV and the counter are concatenated to form the input block of the underlying keyed PRP or PRF, so their lengths sum to its block size.

In (((KLE, Machine, XCTR))) XCTR mode, the IV has the block size; the counter is zero-extended to the block size, and its XOR with the IV forms the input block.

Parameters::
* `b` = block size (`b` a multiple of 8), with `b` {ge} 64.
* `k` = key size (`k` a multiple of 8).
* `j` = counter size: 32 in CTR mode, 64 in XCTR mode.
* `n` = IV size (`n` a multiple of 8).
* In CTR mode, `b = n + j`; in XCTR mode, `b = n`.
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::
* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
| iii  | `pad`         | Padding: (−`kf`) mod 128.
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits.
 ** `IV`: `n` bits.
 ** `ctr`: `j` bits.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|    i  | `k` or 64       | `key` or System Key Identifier
|   ii  | `n`             | `IV`
|  iii  | `j`             | `ctr`
|   iv  | `pad`           | Padding: (−(`kf` + `n` + `j`)) mod 128.
|===
--

Machine-Specific Functions::
* `keystream_block(p) -> c`: `enc_blk(key, p)` (<<KLE-algo-notation>>), the encryption of the counter block `p` under `key`.
* `tick_ctr()`: increases `ctr` by one, modulo 2^`j`^.

State Machine::
* *States:*
  ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
  ** _Operate_: The CC produces a keystream.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ or _Operate_ -> _Operate_, if encryption or decryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Operate_ -> _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** When entering _Ready_, `IV` {leftarrow} 0, and `ctr` {leftarrow} 0 in CTR mode and 1 in XCTR mode
    (as in the XCTR of HCTR2 cite:[cryptoeprint-2021-1441]).

 ** When entering _Operate_, the `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_operate` invokes `LOAD_FIXED_FIELD(IV, n, B)` (<<KLE-op-load-fixed-field>>), which loads `IV` and leaves `ctr` unchanged.
    The Machine allows a same-State `kl.setst` into _Operate_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>), which does the same.

 ** In State _Operate_, (multi-block) Form C ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected.
    For each block, they perform:
+
--
  . In CTR mode, `tmp <- keystream_block(bswap(ctr) @ IV)`;
    in XCTR mode, `tmp <- keystream_block(IV xor (zeros(b - j) @ ctr))`.
  . `tick_ctr()`.
  . `OUTPUT <- tmp`.
--
+
As a `kl.derive` source (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>), _Operate_ supplies the keystream of whole-block Form C `kl.exec` instructions, discarding the unused bytes of the last block.

 ** In States _Ready_ and _Operate_, the Form B
    `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `Xs` sets the counter:
    `ctr` {leftarrow} `bin(Xs, j)`. It is an operation, not a State
    (Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>>).

NOTE: Setting the counter gives random access into a keystream, for example to resume an interrupted stream.
`ctr` wraps modulo 2^`j`^ and never carries into `IV`, so one `IV` yields at most 2^`j`^ distinct counter blocks (in CTR mode, the incrementing function of cite:[nist-SP-800-38A-1] Appendix B.1 with m = `j`).
No check detects the wrap (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>), since the Machine does not know where a message starts.
In CTR mode, software that follows a protocol incrementing the whole counter block splits the `kl.exec` at the wrap, then sets the incremented `IV` and `ctr` = 0.

Code examples (`b` = 128): <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-keystream-modes>>.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-XEX-XTS-modes]]
=== XEX/XTS Modes

(((KLE, Machine, XEX)))XEX cite:[DBLP-conf-asiacrypt-Rogaway04] turns a block cipher into a _Tweakable Block Cipher_ (TBC) cite:[DBLP-conf-crypto-LiskovRW02], whose key and tweak together select the permutation that the cipher computes.
Its two-key form builds a TBC `Ẽ` from a block cipher `E`, with keys `K1` and `K2`, tweak `T`, and plaintext `P`:

`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `Ẽ(K1, K2, T, P) = E(K1, P xor M) xor M` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` where `{nbsp}{nbsp}` `M = E(K2, T)` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` is called a _mask_.

The single-key XEX of cite:[DBLP-conf-asiacrypt-Rogaway04], whose mask is `x {interpunct} E(K, T)`, is not architected.
The Machine computes the mask once per tweak, by the `kl.setst` that sets the tweak; each block uses the current mask and then updates it by a multiplication in GF(2^b^).
`key1` is `K1`, which encrypts the data, and `key2` is `K2`, which encrypts the tweak.

(((KLE, Machine, XTS)))XTS cite:[DBLP-journals-ieeesp-Martin10], designed for storage confidentiality and a NIST Recommendation since 2010, adds two things to XEX: the tweak identifies the data unit, and a data unit whose length is not a multiple of `b` is completed by ciphertext stealing.
Software obtains both from the XEX CC (<<KLE-XTS-from-XEX>>).
The variants differ:

* `AES128_XEX` and `AES256_XEX` follow cite:[nist-SP-800-38E] and cite:[IEEE-1619-2018]:
** `update_mask` takes `V[i]` as the coefficient of `x^i^`;
** the tweak, the `INPUT` of the `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_, is `bin(i, b)` (<<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>), the data unit sequence number `i` as a `b`-bit little-endian value;
** a data unit has at most 2^20^ blocks (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
* `SM4_XEX` follows cite:[GBT-17964-2021]:
** `update_mask` takes the bits of each byte in the reverse order;
** the tweak is the 128-bit tweak of cite:[GBT-17964-2021], its first byte in byte 0.

(((KLE, Machine, XEX))) The Machine cannot check that the two keys are independent: the provisioner must supply two independently generated keys,
a System Key Identifier must resolve to two independently generated keys in the SKS, and software that derives `key1 {vvert} key2`
(<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>) must take it from a source whose two halves are independent, such as a keystream.

Parameters::

* `b` = 128 = block size.
* `k` = key size (`k` a multiple of 8).
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)     | Field
|  ii   | `k` or 64       | `key1` or System Key Identifier
| iii   | `k` or 0        | `key2`, or empty if _KeyType_ = 1 (a single SKID retrieves both keys, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>)
|  iv   | `pad`           | Padding: (−2``k``) mod 128, or 64 with a System Key Identifier.
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key1`: `k` bits.
 ** `key2`: `k` bits. `key2` = `key1` is a configuration error (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>).
    It is checked whenever the keys are written: by a provisioning, by an import, and by a `kl.derive` into `key1 {vvert} key2`.
    A provisioning checks them whether the PI gives them by value or by a System Key Identifier, or the provisioning generates them (_KeyType_ = 2).
 ** `mask`: `b` bits.
 ** `StealSkip` and `StealBack`: bits 0 and 1 of _StateExtension_ respectively, which record the two steps of ciphertext stealing in _Decrypt_ (<<KLE-XTS-from-XEX>>).
    At most one of them is set, and only in _Decrypt_; the Machine supports no other _StateExtension_ @ _State_.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)  | Field
|  i   | `k` or 64    | `key1` or System Key Identifier
| ii   | `k` or 0     | `key2`, or empty if _KeyType_ = 1 (a single SKID retrieves both keys, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>)
| iii  | `b`          | `mask`
| iv   | `pad`        | Padding: (−2``k``) mod 128, or 64 with a System Key Identifier.
|===
--

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `update_mask(mask)` multiplies `mask` by a generator of GF(2^128^): it is
the multiplication by `x` modulo `x^128^ + x^7^ + x^2^ + x + 1`.
For `AES128_XEX` and `AES256_XEX` (cite:[IEEE-1619-2018] {sect}5.2),
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `update_mask(V)` = `V << 1` `{fournbsp}` if `V[127]` = `0`
+
and
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `update_mask(V)` = `(V << 1) xor (zeros(120) @ 0b10000111)`
`{nbsp}{nbsp}` otherwise.
+
For `SM4_XEX`, with
`B~j~` = `V[8j+7:8j]`, byte `j` of `V`, `update_mask(V)` = `B'~15~ @` {ellipsis} `@ B'~0~`, where
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `B'~j~` = `B~j-1~[0] @ B~j~[7:1]` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` for `0 < j < 16`,
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `B'~0~` = `B~0~ >> 1` `{fournbsp}` if `B~15~[0]` = `0`, `{nbsp}{nbsp}` and
`B'~0~` = `(B~0~ >> 1) xor 0xE1` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` otherwise.
* `mask <- update_mask_inv(mask)` where `update_mask_inv` inverts `update_mask`, dividing by `x`. +
For `AES128_XEX` and `AES256_XEX`,
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `update_mask_inv(V)` = `V >> 1` `{fournbsp}` if `V[0]` = `0`
+
and
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `update_mask_inv(V)` = `\((V xor (zeros(120) @ 0b10000111)) >> 1)` +
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `xor (0b1 @ zeros(127))` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` otherwise.
+
For `SM4_XEX`, with `B~j~` byte `j` of `V` as above, `c` = `B~0~[7]`, and `A` = `B~0~` if `c` = `0` and
`A` = `B~0~ xor 0xE1` otherwise, `update_mask_inv(V)` = `B'~15~ @` {ellipsis} `@ B'~0~`, where
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `B'~j~` = `B~j~[6:0] @ B~j+1~[7]` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` for `0 < j < 15`,
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `B'~0~` = `A[6:0] @ B~1~[7]` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` and `{nbsp}{nbsp}` `B'~15~` = `B~15~[6:0] @ c`.

State Machine::

* *States:* _Ready_, _Encrypt_, and _Decrypt_ (defined as before, cf. <<KLE-ECB-mode>>), and _Success_.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Encrypt_, if encryption is allowed.
 ** _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Decrypt_, if decryption is allowed.
 ** _Decrypt_ with `StealBack` set -> _Success_, by the Form A `kl.exec` that ends ciphertext stealing.
 ** _Encrypt_, _Decrypt_ or _Success_ -> _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** Every transition, same-State `kl.setst` included, clears `StealSkip` and `StealBack`.
 ** When entering _Ready_, `mask` {leftarrow} 0.
 ** When entering _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_, `LOAD_FIXED_FIELD(mask, b, B)` (<<KLE-op-load-fixed-field>>) loads the tweak into `mask`, and then `mask <- enc_blk(key2, mask)` computes the initial mask.
    The Machine allows a same-State `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>), which does the same: it takes a new tweak and performs both steps.

 ** In States _Encrypt_ and _Decrypt_, with `StealSkip` and `StealBack` clear, (multi-block) Form A ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected.
    For each block, they read the `INPUT` and determine the `OUTPUT`:
+
--
  . In State _Encrypt_, `OUTPUT <- mask xor enc_blk(key1, INPUT xor mask)`;
    in State _Decrypt_, `OUTPUT <- mask xor dec_blk(key1, INPUT xor mask)`.
  . `mask <- update_mask(mask)`.
--
 ** In State _Decrypt_, the Form A `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_xex_state_steal` (66) starts ciphertext stealing.
    It is an operation (Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>>).
    If `StealSkip` or `StealBack` is set, it transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_; otherwise it:
  . `mask <- update_mask(mask)`;
  . sets `StealSkip`.
 ** In State _Decrypt_ with `StealSkip` set, a Form A `kl.exec` processes a single block (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>):
  . `OUTPUT <- mask xor dec_blk(key1, INPUT xor mask)`;
  . `mask <- update_mask_inv(mask)`;
  . it clears `StealSkip` and sets `StealBack`.
 ** In State _Decrypt_ with `StealBack` set, a Form A `kl.exec` processes a single block (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>):
  . `OUTPUT <- mask xor dec_blk(key1, INPUT xor mask)`;
  . the locker transitions to _Success_.
//

Code examples, with ciphertext stealing: <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-basic-IV-mode>>, <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-XEX-decryption>>.

[[KLE-XTS-from-XEX,XTS ciphertext stealing]]
*Ciphertext stealing.* A data unit of whole blocks is processed, in both directions, by the plain XEX sequence over its blocks.
Otherwise, let it be `m` {ge} 1 full blocks `P~0~` {ellipsis} `P~m-1~` followed by a partial block `P~m~` of `s` bits,
with `0` < `s` < `b` and `s` a multiple of 8.
The locker is at _mask index_ `j` when its `mask` is `update_mask` applied `j` times to `enc_blk(key2, T)`, where `T` is the tweak.
Blocks are processed in order (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>), so the `j`-th block after the tweak is set, counting from 0, uses mask index `j`.
`CP @ P~m~` and `CP @ C~m~` place the partial block in the least significant bits, where the first `s`/8 bytes of a string are (<<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>).

Encryption consumes the mask indices in order:

  . Issue `kl.exec` for `P~0~` {ellipsis} `P~m-2~`, obtaining `C~0~` {ellipsis} `C~m-2~` (no instruction if `m` = 1).
  . Issue `kl.exec` for `P~m-1~`, obtaining `CC` at mask index `m-1`; `C~m~` {leftarrow} `CC[s-1:0]` and `CP` {leftarrow} `CC[b-1:s]`.
  . Issue `kl.exec` for `CP @ P~m~`, obtaining `C~m-1~` at mask index `m`.
  . The ciphertext is `C~0~` {ellipsis} `C~m-1~`, `C~m~`, with `C~m~` the final `s` bits.

Decryption consumes mask index `m` before mask index `m-1`; the stealing steps of the Machine reorder the two:

  . Issue `kl.exec` for `C~0~` {ellipsis} `C~m-2~`, obtaining `P~0~` {ellipsis} `P~m-2~` (no instruction if `m` = 1); the `mask` is at index `m-1`.
  . Issue the Form A `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_xex_state_steal`, which skips mask index `m-1`.
  . Issue `kl.exec` for `C~m-1~`, obtaining `PP` at mask index `m`; the `mask` returns to index `m-1`.
  . `P~m~` {leftarrow} `PP[s-1:0]` and `CP` {leftarrow} `PP[b-1:s]`.
  . Issue `kl.exec` for `CP @ C~m~`, obtaining `P~m-1~` at mask index `m-1`; the locker transitions to _Success_.

Decryption with stealing adds the `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_xex_state_steal` to the data unit.
The data unit ends in _Success_, so a `kl.setst` into _Ready_ precedes the `kl.setst` that sets the next tweak.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-tweakable]]
=== Generic Tweakable Block Ciphers

The specification of a generic tweakable block cipher resembles that of a block cipher in ECB mode.
Since `kl.exec` takes a single input, a separate instruction sets the tweak.
A tweak longer than the block may need the vector length reconfigured between setting the tweak and processing blocks.

Some commonly used TBCs are `Deoxys-BC` cite:[Deoxys-141], `SKINNY` cite:[DBLP-conf-crypto-BeierleJKL0PSSS16], `QARMA` cite:[DBLP-journals-tosc-Avanzi17], and `QARMAv2` cite:[DBLP-journals-tosc-AvanziBDEGNR23].

Parameters::

* `b` = block size (`b` a multiple of 8), with `b` {ge} 64.
* `k` = key size (`k` a multiple of 8).
* `t` = tweak size (`t` a multiple of 8).
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
| iii  | `pad`         | Padding: (−`kf`) mod 128.
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits.
 ** `tweak`: `t` bits.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|   i  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
|  ii  | `t`           | `tweak`
| iii  | `pad`         | Padding: (−(`kf` + `t`)) mod 128.
|===
--

State Machine::

* *States:* _Ready_, _Encrypt_, and _Decrypt_ (cf. <<KLE-ECB-mode>>).
Setting the tweak is an operation, not a State (Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>>).

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Encrypt_, if encryption is allowed.
 ** _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Decrypt_, if decryption is allowed.
 ** _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** When entering _Ready_, the `tweak` field is cleared.
 ** The Machine allows a same-State `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>), which leaves `tweak` unchanged.
 ** In States _Ready_, _Encrypt_ and _Decrypt_, the Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and
    auxiliary operand `INPUT` is the set-tweak operation: it invokes `LOAD_FIXED_FIELD(tweak, t, B)` (<<KLE-op-load-fixed-field>>), which loads `tweak`.
 ** In _Encrypt_ and _Decrypt_, (multi-block) Form A `kl.exec` instructions are expected.
    For each block, they encrypt, resp. decrypt, `INPUT` into `OUTPUT` under `key` and `tweak`.
    Every block uses the current `tweak`; the Machine never updates it by itself.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-process-VLI]]
=== Macro: Processing Variable Length Inputs into a Fixed Length Output

`PROCESS_VLI` specifies how `kl.exec` absorbs variable-length input for hashing, IV derivation or field loading.
_Current_State_ denotes the invoking State; `last_block_state` is an optional State for the final piece.
An invocation splices the steps below into the calling Machine, substituting its variables and functions and applying any changes it states, such as SHA-2's final-piece processing or BLAKE2's postponed full-block processing.

==== Interface

```
PROCESS_VLI(mode,
            max_len, block, block_len, state, state_len,
            input_base, block_base, state_offset, cumul_len,
            granularity, process_block(),
            last_block_state, process_last_block(), last_block_len,
            finalize())
```

Arguments bind the calling Machine's variables or functions by reference, or supply permitted values such as `None`.

[cols="<2,<6",options="header"]
|===
| Parameter | Meaning
| `mode` | `assign` fills `block`; `xor_accumulate` XORs input into `state` from `state_offset` (steps 3.b–3.c). The choice is explicit, regardless of whether `block` and `state` denote the same variable.
| `max_len` | Total input length; 0 if neither software nor the Machine sets it.
| `block`, `block_len` | Current block buffer and its size.
| `state`, `state_len` | Output buffer and its size. `state` may be the same as `block`.
| `input_base` | Offset of the next bit to absorb within the current instruction's `INPUT`.
| `block_base` | Offset of the next bit to absorb within `block` for `assign`, or within the portion of `state` starting at `state_offset` for `xor_accumulate`. Reaching `block_len` processes the block (step 3.g).
| `state_offset` | Offset within `state` where input is XORed, as in a sponge.
| `cumul_len` | Total length absorbed so far, or `None` if not tracked.
| `granularity` | Transfer unit and interruption-point constraint, as specified below; optional.
| `process_block()` | `process_block(block, state)` processes a full block into `state`; `None` skips the call.
| `last_block_state` | State that absorbs a final piece, or `None`.
| `process_last_block()` | `process_last_block(block, state)` processes the block containing the final bit in `last_block_state`, in place of `process_block()`; `None` processes it as usual.
| `last_block_len` | Remaining length of the final piece; `None` when `last_block_state` is `None`.
| `finalize()` | Finishes input processing, completing the current block where the Machine requires it; `None` skips the call.
|===

The three final-piece parameters default to `None`; when `last_block_state` is `None`, its steps below do not apply.
With `process_block()` and `finalize()` both `None`, the macro can fill a long field and track partial progress without per-block processing.

All lengths, offsets and `amount` are in bits.
`block_len` and `state_len` must be multiples of 8, and `kl.exec` transfers whole bytes.
`input_base` and `block_base` are therefore multiples of 8 except after the final transfer of a `max_len` that is not a multiple of 8, which ends the instruction (step 3.h), or after a final piece in `last_block_state`.

==== Caller Obligations

[IMPORTANT]
====
* `input_base`, `block_base`, `cumul_len` and `last_block_len`, the last two when provided, must bind distinct storage locations. Aliasing them is malformed: an iteration would update the same location several times. Each instruction resets `input_base` (step 2); `block_base` and `cumul_len` persist.
* `cumul_len` must be provided when `max_len` != 0 or `last_block_state` is not `None`, since the macro evaluates it unconditionally in those cases.
* For `xor_accumulate`, `state_offset` + `block_len` {le} `state_len` must hold. Otherwise step 3.c can write past `state`, since `block_base` + `amount` can reach `block_len`.
* Sponges require `xor_accumulate`; fields loaded rather than absorbed, including keys and key-like material, require `assign`. Using XOR to load a field combines the incoming value with its prior contents and can silently produce a key neither party chose.
====

The macro does not initialize `block_base` or `cumul_len`: the Machine zeroes them where needed.
An `assign` destination that must hold a defined value after loading must be zeroed on State entry.
Keeping the persistent counters allows successive invocations to concatenate input.
`max_len` stays constant throughout the computation; the Machine may define further variables for its processing functions.

==== Transfer Limits

A `kl.setst` supplies no input data.
An admitted `kl.exec` with an empty transfer window does not reach the macro and changes none of its variables or the State, even when `cumul_len` {ge} `max_len`.
A State that does not admit the instruction transitions to Error State _Invalid_ before the macro (Rules <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-outcome-valid-state>> and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-transfer-window-checks>>).

When given, `granularity` has these effects:

* With `max_len` = 0, every `KLLEN` that is a multiple of 8 meets the granularity constraint; `granularity` restricts only interruption points (step 3.i).
* With `max_len` != 0, a transfer violates the State's granularity (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>) if `KLLEN` is not a multiple of `granularity` and `cumul_len` + `KLLEN` < `max_len`. Use `cumul_len` at instruction issue, whether starting or resuming.
* Every other `KLLEN` meets the constraint; `max_len` need not be a multiple of `granularity`.

When `max_len` != 0, input beyond it is ignored if `last_block_state` is `None` (step 3.h); otherwise each transfer in _Current_State_ must end before that limit (step 1).
For example, with `granularity` = 128, a 96-bit IV fits either a 96-bit transfer or a 128-bit transfer with excess bits truncated.
Because `KLLEN` includes the prefix skipped by `klstart`, an admitted resumption with a non-multiple of `granularity` can end before `max_len` with a partial block.

==== Processing Input in _Current_State_
