On entry to _Current_State_:

. If software sets `max_len`, a Form B `kl.setst` writes its auxiliary operand `Xs` to `max_len`.
. Otherwise, the Machine sets `max_len`. Entry uses Form A, or Form B where the Machine defines `Xs`; that Form B does not set `max_len`.

The invoking Machine defines any other operand of a `kl.setst`.

_Current_State_ expects only Form B `kl.exec`; each executes these steps:

--
. If `max_len` != 0, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ if either:
.. `cumul_len` {ge} `max_len`; or
.. `last_block_state` is not `None` and `cumul_len` + `KLLEN` − 8 {times} `klstart` {ge} `max_len`.
. `input_base <- 8` {times} `klstart`: 0 when the instruction starts, the point of progress when it resumes.
  Nothing else distinguishes a start from a resumption.
. while (`input_base` < `KLLEN`) do:
.. If `max_len` != 0 then `amount <- min(KLLEN - input_base, block_len - block_base, max_len - cumul_len)`,
   else `amount <- min(KLLEN - input_base, block_len - block_base)`.
   `amount` is always strictly positive, so the loop terminates.
.. if `mode` = `assign` then:
 ... ``block[block_base + amount - 1 : block_base] <- INPUT[input_base + amount - 1 : input_base]``.
.. else, that is if `mode` = `xor_accumulate`:
 ...  XOR ``INPUT[input_base + amount - 1 : input_base]`` to ``state[block_base + amount + state_offset - 1 : block_base + state_offset]``.
.. `input_base <- input_base + amount`.
.. `block_base <- block_base + amount`.
.. If `cumul_len` is not `None`, then `cumul_len <- cumul_len + amount`.
.. If (`block_base` = `block_len`) then:
 ... `process_block(block, state)`, if `process_block()` is not `None`.
 ... `block_base <- 0`.
.. If `max_len` != 0 and `cumul_len = max_len` then:
 ... `finalize()`, if `finalize()` is not `None`.
 ... The instruction is terminated, even if `input_base < KLLEN`.
.. Here, and only here, the instruction may be interrupted while `input_base < KLLEN`, if the invocation gives no `granularity` or `input_base` is a whole multiple of it, with `klstart <- input_base / 8`.
--

Architected state alone determines the interruption points of `kl.exec` in _Current_State_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-interruption-point>>).
They are the byte offsets `k` for which 8 {times} `k` is a multiple of `granularity`, or every byte offset if the invocation gives no `granularity`.
An instruction issued while the locker holds `block_base` {ne} 0 admits only `klstart` = 0: any other `klstart` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_, as in Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-transfer-window-checks>>.

[NOTE]
====
Every iteration except the last fills a block, so a halt leaves `block_base` = 0; the last iteration may leave a partial block.

The recorded `klstart` is therefore an interruption point of the resumption, regardless of the original `block_base`.
An instruction that starts with a partial block may still halt at step 3.i after its first iteration completes that block.

At a halt, `input_base` is a whole number of bytes, so `klstart` never describes a position inside a byte.
====

==== Processing a Final Piece

Where `last_block_state` is not `None`:

 . If `max_len` = 0, the invoking Machine sets `last_block_len` {leftarrow} `block_len` on entering _Current_State_.
   Software then issues a `kl.setst` naming either `last_block_state` or another State that the invoking Machine admits from
   _Current_State_, such as _Hash_Output_.
 . If `max_len` != 0, _Current_State_ absorbs fewer than `max_len` bits (step 1), and software enters
   `last_block_state`, which absorbs the rest.

On entry to `last_block_state` from _Current_State_:

 . If `max_len` = 0, a Form B `kl.setst` invokes `FINAL_BLOCK_ENTRY(0, block_len, unit)` (<<KLE-op-final-block-entry>>), with the `unit` that Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>> admits; with `Xs` = 0, a `kl.exec` in `last_block_state` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (step 1 below).
 . If `max_len` != 0, the `kl.setst` is of Form A and sets `last_block_len` {leftarrow} `max_len` − `cumul_len`.
 ..  If this new `last_block_len` > `block_len`, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_.
 . `finalize()` is not performed.

In `last_block_state`, a single Form B `kl.exec` absorbs `INPUT[last_block_len-1:0]` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>, item 3).
Its only interruption point is 0 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>).
It executes these steps:

--
. If `last_block_len` = 0, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).
. `input_base` {leftarrow} 0.
. while (`input_base` < `last_block_len`) do:
.. `amount` {leftarrow} `min(last_block_len - input_base, block_len - block_base)`.
.. Steps 3.b to 3.f of the `kl.exec` in _Current_State_.
.. If `block_base` = `block_len` and either `input_base` < `last_block_len` or `process_last_block()` is `None`, then:
... `process_block(block, state)`, if `process_block()` is not `None`.
... `block_base` {leftarrow} 0.
. If `process_last_block()` is not `None`, then:
.. `process_last_block(block, state)`,
.. `block_base` {leftarrow} 0.
. `last_block_len` {leftarrow} 0.
--

==== Finalization and Invalid Transitions

A `kl.setst` that transitions the locker from _Current_State_ or `last_block_state` to another State performs
`finalize()` before the transition, if `finalize()` is not `None` and step 3.h has not performed it.
This does not apply where the destination is:

* _Ready_ or an Error State; or
* `last_block_state`, from _Current_State_.

The locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ on:

* leaving `last_block_state`, with `last_block_len` != 0, for a Valid State other than _Ready_;
* where `max_len` != 0 and `last_block_state` is not `None`, leaving _Current_State_ for a Valid State other than _Ready_ or `last_block_state`.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-common-operations]]
=== Common Operations

These macros, like `PROCESS_VLI` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>), insert their behavior into the invoking State or instruction, substituting the supplied parameters and applying any stated changes.
They apply the cited Machine Rules without restating them at each invocation.
`PRESET` (<<KLE-op-preset-value>>) defines a variant of another Machine.

[[KLE-op-load-fixed-field]]
==== `LOAD_FIXED_FIELD`

Invoked by the `kl.setst` that enters a State, or that performs an operation (Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>>), and takes a value of `w` bits as `INPUT`:

. Its native Form is C. Where `w` {le} 128, Forms A and D are admitted too (Rule <<KLE-MGR-form-D-where-C>>); where the invocation passes `B` as a third parameter, Form B is admitted, whatever `w`.
. `field` {leftarrow} `INPUT[w-1:0]` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
. Where the macro is invoked on entering a State, a same-State `kl.setst` into that State that the Machine allows (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>) does the same: it replaces `field` and performs the steps that the Machine adds, and changes nothing else.

[[KLE-op-load-long-field]]
==== `LOAD_LONG_FIELD`

Defines a State _X_ that loads a field of `len` bits over several Form B `kl.exec` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>), with _W_ = `cumul_len`, in bits:

. Entering _X_ zeroes `input_base`, `block_base` and `cumul_len`, zeroes the field where `mode` = `assign`, and clears its flag, if any.
  The Machine admits a same-State `kl.setst`, which repeats these entry operations to restart the load (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>).
. Each `kl.exec` in _X_ invokes +
  `{fournbsp}PROCESS_VLI(mode, len, block, b, state, n,` +
  `{fournbsp}{fournbsp}input_base, block_base, 0, cumul_len, g, process_block(),` +
  `{fournbsp}{fournbsp}None, None, None, finalize())`, +
  with `last_block_state` = `None` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>).
. The load is complete once `cumul_len` = `len`; a field stored as loaded then sets its flag, if any (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>).
. Leaving _X_ before the load is complete leaves the field unconfigured (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, item 5).

[[KLE-op-final-block-entry]]
==== `FINAL_BLOCK_ENTRY`

Invoked by the Form B `kl.setst` with auxiliary operand `Xs` that enters a final-block State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>):

. `Xs` must be a multiple of `unit` with `lo` {le} `Xs` {le} `hi`; `unit` is 8 unless Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>> lets the Machine pass 1.
  Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
. `last_block_len` {leftarrow} `Xs`, the bit length of the final block.
. If `Xs` = 0 (`lo` = 0), `kl.setst` processes the empty final block under Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>, item 2, unless the Machine states otherwise; with `lo` > 0, an empty final block takes that item's bypass instead.
. Otherwise, Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>, item 3, governs the single `kl.exec` that processes `INPUT[last_block_len-1:0]` and sets `last_block_len` {leftarrow} 0.

[[KLE-op-verify-tag]]
==== `SET_TAG_LEN` and `VERIFY_TAG`

`SET_TAG_LEN(cap, lo)` is invoked by the Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_verify` and auxiliary operand `Xs` that enters _Hash_Verify_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>):

. `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `Xs` {ge} `lo`.
. `tag_len` {leftarrow} `min(Xs, cap)`, or `Xs` where `cap` is `None`, which must equal `tag_len_p` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>).
. Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

`VERIFY_TAG(expected, len)`, with `len` = `tag_len` where the invocation omits it, is invoked by the single Form B `kl.exec` that verifies a tag or MAC (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>):

. It verifies `INPUT[len-1:0]` against `expected[len-1:0]`, in constant time with the outcomes of Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>.
. It consumes a value of fixed width `len` from `INPUT` under Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>.


[[KLE-op-preset-value]]
==== `PRESET`

Defines a Machine that is the Machine `parent` with `field`, the IV or nonce that `parent` takes from software for each message, carried in the Provisioning Input instead, as the Machine's PI table shows:

. Provisioning sets `field`, or the Internal State that the Machine forms from it, from the PI, and an import from the Serialized Content; no instruction sets `field`.
  The step of `parent` that loads `field` from `INPUT` does not apply, and the Machine states what replaces it.
. Entering _Ready_ keeps `field`, with any step that the Machine states for it, so a later message is decrypted under the same `field`; software that needs another `field` provisions another locker.
. The Machine only decrypts (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>): the party that provisions the locker encrypts under the key and `field` once, by other means (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
. The Machine is not a `kl.derive` destination (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>): its key and `field` are configured together, by provisioning or import.

[NOTE]
====
Where `parent` is a block cipher mode, such as <<KLE-GCM-mode>> or <<KLE-OCB-mode>>, the Machine protects the confidentiality of `field`.
Since it only decrypts, the uniqueness requirement on `field` of the standard of `parent` binds the party that encrypts, once, under the key and `field`.
A key that software knows, derived into the key field while `field` is kept, would let it recover `field`.
So would the same key in another locker that software can use, if that locker:

* computes the inverse block cipher under the key, such as a decrypting locker of <<KLE-ECB-mode>> or of a CBC Machine (<<KLE-chaining>>), whose zero `IV` leaves the inverse block cipher: with it, software recovers `field`, as the Machine states;
* computes the block cipher under the key, such as an encrypting locker of <<KLE-ECB-mode>>, a locker of <<KLE-keystream-modes>>, or a locker of a CBC, CFB or OFB Machine (<<KLE-chaining>>): with it, software tests guesses of `field`; or
* takes a `field` that software chooses, such as a locker of `parent` or a locker of the Machine provisioned with a guessed `field`: with it, software also tests guesses of `field`.

Software holds the same key in another locker when it can provision, by its SKID, a System Key that the SKS allows for that locker's Machine.
====

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-GCM-mode]]
=== GCM Mode

The GCM mode was designed by David McGrew and John Viega cite:[DBLP-conf-indocrypt-McGrewV04] and became a NIST Recommendation in 2007 cite:[nist-SP-800-38D].
cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}5.1 defines GCM only for block ciphers whose block is 128 bits, such as AES and SM4 (<<KLE-exec-encodings>>).
(((KLE, Machine, GCM)))

Parameters::

* `k` = key size (`k` a multiple of 64).
* `b` = 128 = block size in bits.
* `c` = 32 = counter size in bits.
* Input granularity: 8 bits (`glog` = 3; <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>).

Data Structures::

The Provisioning Input is `key` or a System Key Identifier, followed by zero padding. The private Content holds `J0`, the GHASH accumulator `tag`, and `start_ctr`; it derives `auth_key`. `J0[127:96]` is the big-endian counter, and `start_ctr` is its value before the first counter block. The unified interface supplies `ad_len`, `data_len`, final-piece length, tag length and resumable IV-load state (<<KLE-unified-AEAD>>).

Machine-Specific Functions::
+
--
* `absorb(data)` absorbs data into the hash function by performing:
  . `tag <- tag xor data`.
  . `tag <- Galoismul(tag, auth_key)`.

* `next_ks() -> bits(b)` steps the counter and returns the next keystream block:
  . `ctr <- (int(bswap(J0[127:96])) + 1) mod 2^32^`, with `ctr` a local integer.
  . If `ctr` = `(start_ctr - 1) mod 2^32^` (at most 2^32^ − 2 blocks), the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_, and the calling `kl.exec` performs no further step.
  . Otherwise, `J0[127:96] <- bswap(bin(ctr,32))`, the counter back in big-endian, and `next_ks()` returns `enc_blk(key, J0)`.

* `close_tag(L)` absorbs the length block `L` and masks the tag:
  . `absorb(L)`.
  . `tag <- tag xor enc_blk(key, bswap(bin(start_ctr,32)) @ J0[95:0])`.

* `Galoismul(a, b)` is multiplication in the GHASH field of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}6.3, in the representation of that section.
+
The field is GF(2)[x] modulo `x^128^ + x^7^ + x^2^ + x + 1`.
A 128-bit value `V` represents the element whose coefficient of
`x^8i+j^`, for `0` {le} `i` < `16` and `0` {le} `j` {le} `7`, is `V[8i + (7-j)]`.
+
In other words, the byte order runs forward (“little endian”:
the byte at offset 0 contains the coefficients of `x^0^` through `x^7^`,
the byte at offset 1 contains the coefficients of `x^8^` through `x^15^`,
and so on) while the bit order is reversed within each byte:
in the byte at offset 0, bit 0, the least significant bit, is the coefficient of `x^7^`,
bit 1 that of `x^6^`, and so on.
--

State Machine::

GCM uses the non-prepass subset of <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>. `init_nonce(IV)` forms `J0` as cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1 specifies, derives `auth_key`, records `start_ctr`, and clears `tag`. The AD and payload actions apply `absorb`; the payload action applies `next_ks` and absorbs ciphertext. Finalization absorbs `bswap(bin(data_len,64)) @ bswap(bin(ad_len,64))`, masks the result with `enc_blk(key, bswap(bin(start_ctr,32)) @ J0[95:0])`, and outputs or verifies the `tag_len_p` least significant bits.

////

* *States:*
+
--
Besides _Ready_, _Success_, and _Failure_, the GCM Mode Machines make use of the following States:

 ** _Set_Aux_Value_: Absorb the IV; the `kl.exec` that completes it computes `J0`.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Process Associated Data (AD) into the tag.
 ** _Encrypt_: Encrypt the plaintext and absorb the ciphertext into the tag.
 ** _Enc_Last_Block_: Encrypt the final, fractional block of the plaintext.
 ** _Enc_Tag_Finalize_: Finalize the encryption tag, prepare to return its value.
 ** _Decrypt_: Absorb the ciphertext into the tag and decrypt it.
 ** _Dec_Last_Block_: Decrypt the final, fractional block of the ciphertext.
 ** _Dec_Tag_Finalize_: Finalize the computation of the tag and keep it internally.
 ** _Hash_Verify_: Compare the tag against a user-supplied value, without disclosing it.

The Associated Data (AD) and the plaintext or ciphertext are absorbed into `GHASH`
differently; this places two obligations on the
caller:

* *For the AD*, the caller must zero every bit of the final block beyond the actual end
  of the AD.
  No State processes a fractional last AD block separately.

* *For the plaintext and ciphertext*, the caller must not pass a final block shorter than `b`, zero-padded, to
  _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_.
  Such a block goes through State _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_.

NOTE: Such a padded block would be encrypted, emitting keystream, and absorbed, producing a tag that does not conform to cite:[nist-SP-800-38D]. `GHASH` absorbs the final AD block whole.
--

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Hash_Absorb_; the `kl.exec` that completes the IV makes the second transition (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>).
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_, which restarts the IV (see below).
 ** _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Encrypt_, if encryption is allowed.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Decrypt_, if decryption is allowed.
 ** _Encrypt_ -> { _Enc_Last_Block_ -> } _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Success_.
 ** _Decrypt_ -> { _Dec_Last_Block_ -> } _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Hash_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.

* *Behavior:*
 ** When entering State _Ready_:
  . `auth_key <- enc_blk(key, zeros(b))`;
  . `tag <- zeros(b)`;
  . `tag_len <- 0`;
  . `last_block_len <- 0`.

 ** When entering State _Set_Aux_Value_:
  . A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `Xs` sets the IV length in bits: `len <- Xs`.
  . `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `8` {le} `Xs` {le} `8192`; any other `Xs`, `0` included, transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
  . `tag <- zeros(b)`.
  . A same-State Form B `kl.setst` repeats these operations, restarting the IV (<<KLE-op-load-long-field>>).

 ** State _Set_Aux_Value_ is `LOAD_LONG_FIELD(len, tag, tag, b, process_block(), finalize(), xor_accumulate, b)` (<<KLE-op-load-long-field>>):
    entering it also zeroes `input_base`, `block_base` and `cumul_len`, and each `kl.exec` absorbs the next part of the IV into `tag`, where:
   *** `process_block()` performs `tag <- Galoismul(tag, auth_key)`; a 96-bit IV never fills a block, so it is never multiplied;
   *** `finalize()` is defined as:
    . If `len = 96`, then:
      .. `J0 <- bswap(bin(1,32)) @ tag[95:0]`.
+
The value of `J0` is that of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1 for a 96-bit IV, the byte string `IV {vvert} bswap(bin(1,32))`: the IV occupies bytes 0 to 11 (`J0[95:0]`),
and the counter occupies bytes 12 to 15 as a big-endian integer.

    . Otherwise, let `s <- 128 {times} ceil(len/128) - len`, and the computation of `J0 <- GHASH(IV {vvert} 0^s+64^ {vvert} bswap(bin(len,64)), auth_key)` is completed by performing:
      .. if `block_base != 0`, then `tag <- Galoismul(tag, auth_key)`.
      .. `tag <- tag xor (bswap(bin(len,64)) @ zeros(64))`.
      .. `tag <- Galoismul(tag, auth_key)`.
      .. `J0 <- tag`.
    . `start_ctr <- int(bswap(J0[127:96]))`.
    . `tag <- zeros(b)`.
    . The locker transitions to State _Hash_Absorb_.

 ** When leaving State _Set_Aux_Value_:
  . the `kl.exec` that completes the IV performs `finalize()` (step 3.h of <<KLE-process-VLI>>), which transitions the
    locker to _Hash_Absorb_;
  . a `kl.setst` naming _Ready_ discards the partial IV without `finalize()`, zeroing `tag`, `J0`, `len`,
    `block_base` and `cumul_len` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>);
  . a `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Absorb_ transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_: entering _Hash_Absorb_
    requires a complete IV (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).

[NOTE]
====
The IV is absorbed into `tag`, not into `J0`, and only the finished value is copied to `J0`.
`tag` is free for this: entering _Set_Aux_Value_ zeroes it, which is GHASH's initial value.
The slot of `J0` in the Serialized Content instead carries `len`, `block_base` and `cumul_len` while this State is
active. Accumulating into `J0` would place the running hash and those counters in the same 128 bits, so a CC
exported part-way through a non-96-bit IV could not resume.
====

 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_, (multi-block) Form B `kl.exec` instructions absorb each data block into `tag`:
    `absorb(INPUT)`.

** In State _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_, (multi-block) Form A `kl.exec` instructions encrypt, resp. decrypt, `INPUT` into
   `OUTPUT` and absorb the ciphertext, `OUTPUT`, resp. `INPUT`, into `tag`, with `tmp` a local `bits(b)`:
  . `tmp <- INPUT xor next_ks()`, resp. `tmp <- INPUT`.
  . `absorb(tmp)`.
  . `OUTPUT <- tmp`, resp. `OUTPUT <- tmp xor next_ks()`.

** When entering State _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_, a Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_enc_last_block`, resp. `kl_state_dec_last_block`, performs `FINAL_BLOCK_ENTRY(8, 120, 8)` (<<KLE-op-final-block-entry>>): `last_block_len <- Xs`, a multiple of 8 from 8 to 120; an empty final block takes the bypass below.
+
NOTE: cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] admits any bit length, but numbers the bits of a byte from the most significant, so a truncation to the least significant bits would not give its bit string (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).

** In State _Enc_Last_Block_ or _Dec_Last_Block_, a single Form A `kl.exec` encrypts, resp. decrypts, its `INPUT` into its `OUTPUT` and
   absorbs the zero-padded final block of the ciphertext into `tag`, with `tmp` a local `bits(b)`:
  . In _Enc_Last_Block_:
  .. `tmp <- trunc(INPUT, last_block_len) xor trunc(next_ks(), last_block_len)`.
  .. `absorb(tmp)`.
  .. `OUTPUT <- tmp`.
  . In _Dec_Last_Block_:
  .. `tmp <- trunc(INPUT, last_block_len)`.
  .. `OUTPUT <- tmp xor trunc(next_ks(), last_block_len)`.
  .. `absorb(tmp)`.

** Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>> applies to _Enc_Last_Block_ and _Dec_Last_Block_, which have no successor (item 4): _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, resp. _Dec_Tag_Finalize_, takes the length block as the operand of a Form C `kl.setst`, and that `kl.setst` leaves the final-block State after its `kl.exec`.
For an empty final block, a Form C `kl.setst` from _Encrypt_ into _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, resp. from _Decrypt_ into _Dec_Tag_Finalize_, bypasses the final-block State: `tag` and `J0` are unchanged, and the message ends with the blocks already processed.

** When entering State _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, resp. _Dec_Tag_Finalize_:
  . Under the superseded Machine-specific interface, a Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_enc_tag_finalize`, resp. `kl_state_dec_tag_finalize`, and auxiliary operand `INPUT` supplied the length block:
 .. `INPUT <- bswap(bin(len_in_bits(plaintext), 64)) @ bswap(bin(len_in_bits(AD), 64))`, resp. `INPUT <- bswap(bin(len_in_bits(ciphertext), 64)) @ bswap(bin(len_in_bits(AD), 64))`.
 .. `len_in_bits(plaintext)` counts only the plaintext that _Encrypt_ and _Enc_Last_Block_ processed, resp. `len_in_bits(ciphertext)` only the ciphertext that _Decrypt_ and _Dec_Last_Block_ processed; a final block that a `kl.setst` omitted is not counted.
 . `close_tag(INPUT[127:0])` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>) computes the tag and retains it internally.

** In State _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, a single Form C `kl.exec` writes `tag[tag_len_p-1:0]`, the truncated tag `MSB~tag_len_p~(T)` of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1, to `OUTPUT` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>), and the locker transitions to _Success_.

** Entering _Hash_Verify_ by Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_verify` performs `SET_TAG_LEN(None, 0)` (<<KLE-op-verify-tag>>).

** In _Hash_Verify_, a single Form B `kl.exec` performs `VERIFY_TAG(tag)` (<<KLE-op-verify-tag>>), where `tag[tag_len-1:0]` is `MSB~t~(T)` of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.2 with `t` = `tag_len`.
After either outcome, `tag_len <- 0`.
+
NOTE: Truncated tags are prefixes of one another.
The floor of 64 bits excludes the 32 bits that cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] admits for some applications.
Every verification compares `tag_len_p` bits (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>), but a forgery attempt with a message of {ell} blocks succeeds with probability about {ell} {times} 2^-`tag_len_p`^, and each success reveals information about `auth_key` that makes the next attempt easier.
For `tag_len_p` < 128, the caller applies the limits of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] Appendix C on message length and on failed verifications per key (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>> leaves to the caller a single `t` across lockers that hold the same key by value.
+
NOTE: _Decrypt_ and _Dec_Last_Block_ release plaintext block by block before _Hash_Verify_ checks the tag.
Authenticated decryption under cite:[nist-SP-800-38D], which returns no plaintext on failure, therefore requires Rule <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-SR-unverified-plaintext>>.
This also applies to <<KLE-GCM-with-IV-mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>> (which verifies in _Dec_Tag_Finalize_), <<KLE-OCB-mode>>, <<KLE-OCB-with-nonce-mode>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-wsn>> and <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-N-masking>> under their respective standards.
In OCB, the released plaintext also enables a forgery (<<KLE-accepted-risks>>).
+
NOTE: Two messages that the caller encrypts under the same `key` and IV reveal `auth_key`, which then allows forgeries under every IV until the key is retired (<<KLE-accepted-risks>>).

//

Code examples: <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-GCM-encryption>>, <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-GCM-decryption>>.

////

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-GCM-with-IV-mode]]
=== GCM with Preset IV

The mode of operation (((KLE, Machine, GCM with Preset IV))) “GCM with a Preset IV” is a variant of GCM in which the initialization vector (IV) is provisioned with the key, rather than supplied by software for each message.

This Machine is `PRESET(GCM, IV)` (<<KLE-op-preset-value>>, <<KLE-GCM-mode>>).
Provisioning forms `J0` from the 96-bit IV as cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1 does; `J0` itself is never provisioned.
The differences follow.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[cols="^8%,<15%,<15%,<62%",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field       | Description
|  ii   | `k` or 64     | `key`       | Key value or System Key Identifier
| iii   | 96            | `IV`        | The IV of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1. Only a 96-bit IV is supported.
|  iv   | `pad`         | Padding     | Zero; (−(`kf` + 96)) mod 128 bits.
|===
--

* *Internal State*: {empty}
+
--
Same as for GCM.

Upon provisioning, before the operations of entering _Ready_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-management-no-state-operations>>),
`J0 <- bswap(bin(1,32)) @ IV`, the byte string `IV {vvert} bswap(bin(1,32))` of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1, and `start_ctr <- 1`. `start_ctr` is 1 in every
CC of this Machine: an import whose Serialized Content holds another value completes into Error State _Invalid_
(Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
--

* *Serialized Content*: {empty}
+
--
Same as for GCM.
--

State Machine::

GCM with Preset IV uses the GCM state subset of <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>, omitting _Set_Aux_Value_.
Entering _Ready_ restores the provisioned `J0`; every other action is GCM's.

////

* *States and Allowed Transitions:* {empty}
+
--
Same as for GCM, with the following exceptions:

** There is no State _Set_Aux_Value_, and nothing that GCM specifies for it applies. The transition is from _Ready_ to _Hash_Absorb_.
--

* *State Machine Behavior:* {empty}
+
--
The differences with respect to GCM are:

 ** Entering State _Ready_ also restores the provisioned `J0`, by `J0[127:96] <- bswap(bin(1,32))`, which keeps the IV (<<KLE-op-preset-value>>).
--

////

[NOTE]
====
The rationale of <<KLE-op-preset-value>> applies, with the uniqueness requirement of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}8 on the `key` and `J0`.
A derived `key` would let software recover `J0` from the keystream, and a locker that computes the inverse block cipher would let it recover the IV, and so `J0`, from a single keystream block.

A provisioned `J0` would let software choose the counter blocks that the Machine encrypts: with
`J0` = 0^96^ {vvert} `0xFFFFFFFF`, a decrypt-only locker would output `E(K, 0)`, the GHASH key, and with `J0` = `N` {vvert} 0^32^
the tag mask of IV `N`, so that software could forge tags under a key that it never sees.
====

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-GCM-SIV-mode]]
=== GCM-SIV Mode

The (((KLE, Machine, GCM-SIV))) GCM-SIV mode is described in cite:[DBLP-journals-iacr-GueronLL17] and cite:[RFC8452].
This specification defines GCM-SIV only for AES, whose block is 128 bits, as cite:[RFC8452] does (<<KLE-exec-encodings>>); the definition will be extended to other block ciphers if needed.

Parameters::

* `k` = key size, either 128 or 256: cite:[RFC8452] defines AES-GCM-SIV only for these two.
* `b` = 128 = block size.
* `c` = 32 = counter size.
* Input granularity: 8 bits (`glog` = 3; <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>).
* The authentication tag is `b` = 128 bits long.
* A plaintext or ciphertext holds at most 2^32^ blocks, the final partial block included: the `P_MAX` of cite:[RFC8452] {sect}6. The caller must keep the AD within the `A_MAX` of cite:[RFC8452] {sect}6, 2^36^ bytes (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).

Data Structures::

The Provisioning Input is `key` or a System Key Identifier, followed by zero padding. The private Content holds the 96-bit `nonce`, `SIV`, POLYVAL accumulator `tmp`, and block counter `ctr`; it derives `enc_key` and `auth_key`. The unified interface holds all common message state and final-piece length (<<KLE-unified-AEAD>>).

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `RFC8452_KeyDeriv(k, key, nonce)` derives the per-nonce message-encryption and
  message-authentication keys of cite:[RFC8452] {sect}4:
+
[[KLE-GCM-SIV-KeyDeriv]]
.AES-GCM-SIV Key Derivation
[]
----
function RFC8452_KeyDeriv(k : int, key : bits(k), nonce : bits(96))
    → struct{enc_key : bits(k), auth_key : bits(128)} =
{
  local A : array [0..5] of bits(128);
  local E : bits(k);
  local i : int;

  foreach(i from 0 to k/64 + 1) do:
    A[i] ← enc_blk(key, nonce @ bin(i,32));
  foreach(i from 2 to k/64 + 1) do:
    E[64*i-65 : 64*i-128] ← A[i][63:0];

  return struct { enc_key = E, auth_key = A[1][63:0] @ A[0][63:0] };
}
----
+
The loop makes `k`/64 + 2 calls, four for `k` = 128 and six for `k` = 256, and keeps the low
64 bits of each: these are the “first eight bytes” of cite:[RFC8452] under <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>.
* `absorb(data)` absorbs data into the hash function by performing a step of POLYVAL:
  . `tmp <- tmp xor data`.
  . `tmp <- Montmul(tmp, auth_key)`, with the `Montmul` of the POLYVAL of <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-SCC-POLYVAL>>.
* `next_ks() -> bits(b)` returns the keystream block of the next counter and steps the counter:
  . If `ctr` = 2^32^, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_, and the calling `kl.exec` performs no further step: the block would be one more than a message may hold.
  . Otherwise, `ctr <- ctr + 1`, and `next_ks()` returns ``enc_blk(enc_key, 1 @ SIV[126:32] @ bin\((int(SIV[31:0]) + ctr - 1) mod 2^32^, 32))``, the block of the counter before the step.
* `synth_siv(L)` absorbs the length block `L` and computes the SIV into `tmp`:
  . `absorb(L)`.
  . `tmp <- enc_blk(enc_key, 0 @ tmp[126:96] @ (tmp[95:0] xor nonce))`.

State Machine::

GCM-SIV uses _Ready_, _Set_Aux_Value_, _Set_Aux_Value_2_, _Hash_Absorb_, _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, _AEAD_Prepass_, _AEAD_Prepass_Last_, _Encrypt_, _Enc_Last_Block_, _Decrypt_, _Dec_Last_Block_, _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, _Dec_Tag_Finalize_, _Success_ and _Failure_ from <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>.
`RFC8452_KeyDeriv`, `absorb`, `next_ks` and `synth_siv` define its actions. `synth_siv` uses the internally counted little-endian bit lengths. The first _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ synthesizes and retains the SIV, then starts encryption; the second returns it. _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ compares it.

////

  * *States:*
+
--
Besides _Ready_, _Success_, and _Failure_, the GCM-SIV Machine makes use of the following States:

 ** _Set_Aux_Value_: Set the value of `nonce`.
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_2_: Set the SIV.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Absorb the AD and, on the encryption path, the plaintext into the partial tag `tmp`.
 ** _Encrypt_: Encrypt the plaintext.
 ** _Enc_Last_Block_: Encrypt the final, fractional block of the plaintext.
 ** _Enc_Tag_Finalize_: Finalize the SIV with the AD and plaintext lengths, and return it.
 ** _Decrypt_: Decrypt the ciphertext and absorb the resulting plaintext into the partial tag.
 ** _Dec_Last_Block_: Decrypt the final, fractional block of the ciphertext.
 ** _Dec_Tag_Finalize_: Finalize the tag with the AD and plaintext lengths, then compare it against the SIV.
--

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_.
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_2_ or _Hash_Absorb_.
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_2_ -> _Hash_Absorb_.
 ** For encryption: _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Encrypt_ { -> _Enc_Last_Block_ }.
    The path ends in _Encrypt_ or _Enc_Last_Block_, not in _Success_;
    the next message starts with a return to _Ready_.
 ** The `kl.exec` of _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ synthesizes `SIV` and transitions the locker to _Encrypt_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>).
 ** For decryption: _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Decrypt_ -> { _Dec_Last_Block_ -> } _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>> applies: the `kl.exec` of _Dec_Last_Block_ transitions the locker to _Dec_Tag_Finalize_, which a `kl.setst` from _Decrypt_ enters directly for an empty final block (item 2); _Enc_Last_Block_ has no successor.
 ** A final plaintext or ciphertext block shorter than `b` goes through _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_.

* *Behavior:*
** Upon entering State _Ready_:
  *** The following initialization operations are performed:
   . `nonce <- 0`, `ctr <- 0`, `tmp <- 0`, `last_block_len <- 0` and `SIV <- 0`.

** When entering State _Set_Aux_Value_:
   . A Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `INPUT` is expected.
   . `nonce <- INPUT[95:0]` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
   . (`enc_key`, `auth_key`) `<-` `RFC8452_KeyDeriv(k, key, nonce)`.

** When entering State _Set_Aux_Value_2_:
   . A Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value_2` and auxiliary operand `INPUT` is expected.
   . `SIV <- INPUT[127:0]` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
+
[NOTE]
====
This State is meant to serve the decryption path.
Entering it during encryption is harmless: an externally set `SIV` never feeds the encryption keystream. The encryption path reaches _Encrypt_ only through the _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ `kl.exec`, which overwrites `SIV` with the synthetic value, and it reaches _Enc_Last_Block_ only from _Encrypt_.
Skipping it during decryption leaves `SIV` at the zero that entering _Ready_ set.
====

** In State _Set_Aux_Value_ or _Set_Aux_Value_2_, a `kl.setst` naming that same State transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_
   (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>):
   . The `kl.setst` that enters the State sets the value.
   . The State loads no field by `kl.exec`.

** In State _Hash_Absorb_, each (multi-block) Form B `kl.exec` absorbs its data blocks into `tmp`: `absorb(INPUT)`.
   The caller must zero-pad the following, each separately to a multiple of 16 bytes, as cite:[RFC8452] {sect}4 specifies:
+
--
. The AD.
. On the encryption path, the plaintext.

Every bit of the final partial block beyond the end of its data must be zero.
--

** In State _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, a single Form A `kl.exec` is expected, whose `INPUT` the caller must set to
   `bin(len_in_bits(plaintext), 64) @ bin(len_in_bits(AD), 64)`. It finalizes the SIV:
   . `synth_siv(INPUT[127:0])` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
   . `SIV <- tmp` and `OUTPUT <- SIV`.
   . The locker transitions to _Encrypt_.

** In State _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_, (multi-block) Form A ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected, encrypting, resp. decrypting, `INPUT` into `OUTPUT`:
   . `OUTPUT <- INPUT xor next_ks()`.
   . In _Decrypt_ only, `absorb(OUTPUT)`, which absorbs the plaintext into `tmp`.

** When entering State _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_, a Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_enc_last_block`, resp. `kl_state_dec_last_block`, performs `FINAL_BLOCK_ENTRY(8, 120, 8)` (<<KLE-op-final-block-entry>>): `last_block_len <- Xs`, a multiple of 8 from 8 to 120.
An empty final block skips _Enc_Last_Block_: the message ends in _Encrypt_.

** In State _Enc_Last_Block_ or _Dec_Last_Block_, a single Form A ``kl.exec`` instruction is expected, encrypting, resp. decrypting, `INPUT` into `OUTPUT` as follows (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>):
   . `OUTPUT <- trunc(INPUT, last_block_len) xor trunc(next_ks(), last_block_len)`.
   . In _Dec_Last_Block_ only, `absorb(OUTPUT)`, which absorbs the zero-padded plaintext into the partial tag.
   . In _Dec_Last_Block_, the locker then transitions to _Dec_Tag_Finalize_; the Machine names no successor of _Enc_Last_Block_, so the locker stays in it.

** In State _Dec_Tag_Finalize_, a single Form B `kl.exec` is expected, whose `INPUT` the caller must set to
   `bin(len_in_bits(plaintext), 64) @ bin(len_in_bits(AD), 64)`; it compares the SIV itself (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>):
   . `synth_siv(INPUT[127:0])` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
   . If `tmp` = `SIV`, the locker transitions to _Success_; otherwise, it transitions to _Failure_.

//   Repeating the operations of entering the State would admit a second final block (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).

//

Code examples: <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-GCM-SIV-mode-examples>>.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

////

[[KLE-OCB-mode]]
=== OCB Mode

Only (((KLE, Machine, OCB))) OCB3 is architected, following RFC{nbsp}7253 cite:[RFC7253] and cite:[DBLP-journals-joc-KrovetzR21], and only for AES, as in the AEAD algorithms of cite:[RFC7253] (<<KLE-exec-encodings>>).
The state machine follows RFC{nbsp}7253 except in notation.
Bit positions count from 0 at the least significant bit (<<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>); RFC{nbsp}7253 counts them from 1 at the most significant bit.
A block is a little-endian byte string: its byte 0 is the first byte of the RFC{nbsp}7253 string.
A formula over RFC{nbsp}7253 bit positions is computed on the `bswap` view of the block, either with an explicit `bswap` or in a function or variable whose name ends in `_be`.
Block indices, `index` included, start from 1 as in RFC{nbsp}7253.

Parameters::
+
--
* `b` = 128 = block size.
* `k` = key size. It must be 128, 192, or 256.
* `MAX_BLOCKS` = 2^48^ = maximum number of blocks, a final partial block included, in the AD or in the plaintext.
  This specification sets this bound: cite:[RFC7253] places no limit on the length of a message.
  The caller keeps the uniqueness of the nonce and the recommendation of
  cite:[RFC7253] {sect}5 that a key process at most 2^48^ blocks in total, the associated data included
  (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
* Input granularity: 8 bits (`glog` = 3; <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>).
--

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64      | `key` or System Key Identifier
| iii  | `pad`          | Padding: (−`kf`) mod 128.
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
  ** `key`: `k` bits.
 ** `N`: 120 bits.
 ** `N_len`: 7 bits. Bit length of the nonce. It must not exceed 120.
 ** `HasN`: bit 0 of _StateExtension_, set once `N` holds a loaded nonce (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>).
 ** `L*`, `offset`, `hash_A`, `checksum_P`: 128 bits each.
 ** `L$` and `L[i]` (derived): 128 bits each, for `0` {le} `i` {le} {lfloor}log~2~(`MAX_BLOCKS`){rfloor} = 48; `L[i]` is `L~i~` of cite:[RFC7253]. Rule <<KLE-MGR-recomputed-fields>> applies to them.
 ** `tag_len`: 8 bits. Bit length of the tag. The allowed values are 64, 96, and 128.
 ** `last_block_len`: 7 bits, an integer in the range 0 to 127. The bit length of the final, partial block of the AD, the plaintext or the ciphertext; 0 for an empty final block.
 ** `index`: 49 bits, corresponding to `i` in the loops in RFC{nbsp}7253. It is 0 from the entry into _Ready_ to the entry into _Hash_Absorb_, and then ranges from 1 to `MAX_BLOCKS` + 1 = 2^48^ + 1.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits) | Field
|   i   | `k` or 64   | `key` or System Key Identifier
|   ii  | 120         | `N`
|  iii  |   7         | `N_len`, the bit length of the nonce
|   iv  |   1         | Padding
|    v  | 128         | `L*`; the derived `L$` and `L[i]` are not serialized (Rule <<KLE-MGR-recomputed-fields>>)
|   vi  | 128         | `offset` (current offset)
|  vii  | 128         | `hash_A`
| viii  | 128         | `checksum_P` (used also for the final tag)
|   ix  |  49         | `index`
|    x  |   2         | `tag_len`, encoded as `tag_len`/32 − 2. The value 3 is not produced; an import that carries it transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
|   xi  |   7         | `last_block_len`
|  xii  | `pad`       | Padding: (−(`kf` + 698)) mod 128 = (−(`kf` + 58)) mod 128.
|===
--

//

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `ntz(n)` = the number of trailing zero bits in the base-2 representation of the positive integer `n`.
Formally, `ntz(n)` is the largest integer `x` for which `2^x^` divides `n`.
* `double(S)`: doubling in the OCB3 field of cite:[RFC7253], which is the `double` of <<KLE-CMAC-mode>> for `b` = 128.
* `next_offset()` advances `offset` to the next full block:
+
. If `index` > `MAX_BLOCKS`, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than the AD, the plaintext or the ciphertext may hold.
. `offset <- offset xor L[ntz(index)]`.
. `index <- index + 1`.
* `last_offset()` advances `offset` to the final partial block:
+
. If `index` > `MAX_BLOCKS`, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_, as in `next_offset()`.
. `offset <- offset xor L*`.
* `ocb_tag(X)` computes into `checksum_P` the tag, before truncation, from the last checksum input `X`:
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `checksum_P <- enc_blk(key, checksum_P xor X xor L$) xor hash_A`.
* `nonce_be(N, n)` = the big-endian view of the `n`-bit nonce bit string held in `N`.
  cite:[RFC7253] treats the nonce as a bit string whose first bit is the most significant, so
  when it is serialized into bytes the final, possibly partial byte is left-aligned: its
  `r` = `n` mod 8 most significant bits carry nonce and its remaining bits are padding. With
  `q` = {lceil}``n``/8{rceil} the number of bytes so occupied,
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `nonce_be(N, n)` = `(bswap(N[8q-1:0], q) >> ((8 - r) mod 8))[n-1:0]`, an `n`-bit value.
+
The first bit of the nonce string is `nonce_be(N, n)[n-1]` and the last is bit `0`.
For `r` = 0 this reduces to `bswap(N[n-1:0])`, so a nonce occupying a whole number of bytes is
unaffected. `bswap` alone cannot express the general case, since it is defined only over byte
strings.

//

State Machine::
OCB uses the non-prepass subset of <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>. `next_offset`, `last_offset`, `ocb_tag` and `nonce_be` define its algorithm-specific actions. The Machine derives the nonce-dependent offsets, counts and pads AD/PT/CT, and computes or verifies the tag internally.

////

* *States:*
Besides _Ready_, _Success_, and _Failure_, the Machines implementing OCB Mode use the following States:
+
--
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_: Set the length and the value of the nonce.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Set the tag length, and process the AD.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_: Set the bit length, possibly zero, of the final block of the AD, and process that block. Every message passes through this State.
 ** _Encrypt_: Process the plaintext by absorbing it into the tag and encrypting it.
 ** _Enc_Last_Block_: Provide the last block of the plaintext, then process it.
 ** _Enc_Tag_Finalize_: Return the tag.
 ** _Decrypt_: Process the ciphertext by decrypting it and absorbing the plaintext into the tag.
 ** _Dec_Last_Block_: Provide the last block of the ciphertext, then process it.
 ** _Hash_Verify_: Compare a user-provided value with the internally computed tag.
--

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_.
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_, which restarts the nonce load (see below).
 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ -> _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_.
 ** _Encrypt_ -> _Enc_Last_Block_ -> _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Success_.
 ** _Decrypt_ -> _Dec_Last_Block_ -> _Hash_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** The `kl.exec` of _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_, makes the transition to _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, resp.
    _Hash_Verify_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>).
 ** Every message passes through _Enc_Last_Block_ or _Dec_Last_Block_, with `last_block_len` zero if its last block is a full block, processed in _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_.
+
These States are left by their `kl.exec` or, with `last_block_len` zero, by a Form A `kl.setst` to _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, resp. _Hash_Verify_ (see below).
Any other `kl.setst`, except one naming _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>), transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
 ** Gated States: those that Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>> lists for encryption and decryption.

* *Behavior:*
** Upon entering State _Ready_:
  *** The following initialization operations are performed:
   . `N <- zeros(120)`.
   . `N_len <- 0`.
   . `HasN` is cleared.
   . `hash_A <- zeros(128)`.
   . `checksum_P <- zeros(128)`.
   . `index <- 0`.
   . `last_block_len <- 0`.
   . `tag_len <- 128`.

** When entering State _Set_Aux_Value_:
+
--
. A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `Xs` is expected, where `Xs` must satisfy `6` {le} `Xs` {le} `120`; any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
  Every bit length in that range is admissible (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>): the nonce is a bit string and need not fill whole bytes.
. `N_len <- Xs` and `N <- zeros(120)`, and `HasN` is cleared.
--
[NOTE]
====
cite:[RFC7253] itself places no lower bound on the nonce length. KLE requires at least 6 bits
because a shorter nonce leaves the `bottom` and `Stretch` derivation of cite:[RFC7253] {sect}4.2
without enough entropy to remain sound, as analyzed in cite:[cryptoeprint-2023-326]. An
implementation of this specification is therefore not a general RFC{nbsp}7253 implementation for
nonces shorter than 6 bits.
====

** While in State _Set_Aux_Value_, a Form B `kl.exec`, which processes a single block (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>), loads `N` from `INPUT[8q-1:0]`, with `q` = {lceil}``N_len``/8{rceil}; the other bits of `INPUT` are ignored (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>):
+
--
. `N <- zeros(120 - 8q) @ INPUT[8q-1:0]`, with the `(8 - N_len) mod 8` least significant bits of byte `q-1` cleared:
they pad the final, partial byte of the nonce string, are not part of the nonce, and are ignored.
. `HasN` is set.

A repeated `kl.exec` rewrites `N`.

A `kl.setst` naming _Set_Aux_Value_ in this State restarts the load (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>): it performs the operations of entering the State with its new `Xs`.
--

** When entering State _Hash_Absorb_, which requires `HasN` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>):
+
--
. A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_absorb` and auxiliary operand `Xs` is expected, where `Xs` must be 64, 96 or 128; any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. `index <- 1` and `tag_len <- Xs`.
. `offset <- zeros(b)` – this is `Offset~0~` in the RFC.
. `L* <- enc_blk(key, zeros(b))` – this is `L~*~` in the RFC.
. `L$ <- double(L*)`, `L[0] <- double(L$)`, and `L[i] <- double(L[i-1])` for all `i` with `0 < i` {le} 48, possibly lazily.
--

** In State _Hash_Absorb_, (multi-block) `kl.exec` instructions are expected to be of Form B, absorbing a data block `INPUT` into `hash_A`:
+
--
 . `next_offset()`.
 . `hash_A <- hash_A xor enc_blk(key, INPUT xor offset)`.
--

** When entering State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, a Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_last_block` invokes `FINAL_BLOCK_ENTRY(0, 120, 8)` (<<KLE-op-final-block-entry>>), which sets `last_block_len`, the bit length of the final block of the AD.
An empty final block needs no `kl.exec`: the message continues with the `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_.

** In State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, a single Form B `kl.exec` absorbs the last block of the AD into `hash_A`:
+
--
  . `last_offset()`.
  . `hash_A <- hash_A xor enc_blk(key, pad10(INPUT, last_block_len) xor offset)`.

With `last_block_len` = 0, the final block is empty or already processed, and a `kl.exec` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).
The AD has several successors, so _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ stays after its `kl.exec` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).
--

** On entering State _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_, the Machine computes the nonce-dependent variables of cite:[RFC7253] {sect}4.2:
+
--
 * `local Nonce_be : bits(128)` – this is `Nonce` in the RFC, in the big-endian view.
 * `local Ktop, Ktop_be : bits(128)`
 * `local Stretch_be : bits(192)` – this is `Stretch` in the RFC, in the big-endian view.
 * `local bottom : int`

[]
 . `Nonce_be <- bin(tag_len mod 128, 7) @ zeros(120 - N_len) @ 1 @ nonce_be(N, N_len)`.
 . `bottom <- int(Nonce_be[5:0])`.
 . `Ktop <- enc_blk(key, bswap(Nonce_be[127:6] @ zeros(6)))`.
 . `Ktop_be <- bswap(Ktop)`.
 . `Stretch_be <- Ktop_be @ (Ktop_be[127:64] xor Ktop_be[119:56])`.
 . `index <- 1`.
 . `offset <- bswap(Stretch_be[191-bottom : 64-bottom])`.
--

** In State _Encrypt_, (multi-block) `kl.exec` instructions are expected to be of Form A, absorbing the plaintext `INPUT` into `checksum_P` and encrypting it into `OUTPUT`:
+
--
 . `next_offset()`.
 . `checksum_P <- checksum_P xor INPUT`.
 . `OUTPUT <- offset xor enc_blk(key, INPUT xor offset)`.
--

** In State _Decrypt_, (multi-block) `kl.exec` instructions are expected to be of Form A, decrypting `INPUT` into `OUTPUT` and absorbing the plaintext `OUTPUT` into `checksum_P`:
+
--
  * `local tmp : bits(b)`

[]
  . `next_offset()`.
  . `tmp <- offset xor dec_blk(key, INPUT xor offset)`.
  . `checksum_P <- checksum_P xor tmp`.
  . `OUTPUT <- tmp`.
--

** When entering State _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_, a Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_enc_last_block`, resp. `kl_state_dec_last_block`, invokes `FINAL_BLOCK_ENTRY(0, 120, 8)` (<<KLE-op-final-block-entry>>), which sets `last_block_len`, the bit length of the final block of the plaintext, resp. ciphertext.
These States are the exception to item 2 of Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>: with `Xs` = 0, the `kl.setst` neither processes the final block nor enters the successor, which the Form D `kl.exec` or the Form A `kl.setst` below enters.

** In State _Enc_Last_Block_ or _Dec_Last_Block_, if `last_block_len` is nonzero, a single Form A `kl.exec` is expected, which performs the following, where `P`, the final block of the plaintext, is `INPUT` in _Enc_Last_Block_ and the `OUTPUT` of step 2 in _Dec_Last_Block_:
+
--
. `last_offset()`.
. `OUTPUT <- trunc(INPUT, last_block_len) xor trunc(enc_blk(key, offset), last_block_len)`. // enc_blk(key, offset) is Pad in RFC{nbsp}7253
. `ocb_tag(offset xor pad10(P, last_block_len))`.

If `last_block_len` is zero, a single Form D `kl.exec` is expected instead, which performs `ocb_tag(offset)`.

In both cases `last_block_len <- 0` and the locker then transitions to _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, resp. _Hash_Verify_.
--
+
With `last_block_len` zero, a Form A `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_enc_tag_finalize`, resp. `kl_state_hash_verify`, is also admitted: on entering _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, resp. _Hash_Verify_, it performs the operation of the Form D `kl.exec`, so that both routes leave the same `checksum_P`.
With `last_block_len` nonzero, that `kl.setst` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

** In State _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, a single Form C `kl.exec` is expected.
   It performs `OUTPUT <- trunc(checksum_P, tag_len)`, and the locker transitions to _Success_.
   A `KLLEN` smaller than `tag_len` violates the granularity of the State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).

** In State _Hash_Verify_, a single Form B `kl.exec` completes decryption: `VERIFY_TAG(checksum_P)` (<<KLE-op-verify-tag>>) compares the `tag_len` least significant bits of `INPUT` with those of `checksum_P`, with the `tag_len` set on entering _Hash_Absorb_.

//

Code examples: <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-OCB-mode-examples>>.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

////

[[KLE-OCB-with-nonce-mode]]
=== OCB with Preset Nonce

The mode of operation (((KLE, Machine, OCB with Preset Nonce))) “OCB with Preset Nonce” is a variant of OCB (<<KLE-OCB-mode>>) in which the nonce is
carried in the Provisioning Input, as the IV is in <<KLE-GCM-with-IV-mode>>.
This Machine is `PRESET(OCB, N)` (<<KLE-op-preset-value>>), with `N_len` preset together with `N`, and is otherwise the same as OCB, its Parameters and Machine-Specific Functions included, except as follows.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[cols="^8%,<15%,<15%,<62%",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field       | Description
|  ii   | `k` or 64     | `key`       | Key value or System Key Identifier
| iii   | 120           | `N`         | The nonce, in the format of the `INPUT` of the _Set_Aux_Value_ `kl.exec` of <<KLE-OCB-mode>>.
|  iv   | 7             | `N_len`     | The bit length of the nonce.
|   v   | 1             | Padding     | {empty}
|  vi   | 0 or 64       | Padding     | {empty}
|===
--

* *Internal State*: {empty}
+
--
Same as for OCB.

Provisioning sets `N_len` from the PI, and sets `N` from the PI as the _Set_Aux_Value_ `kl.exec` of <<KLE-OCB-mode>> sets it from its `INPUT`.
An `N_len` outside [6, 120], in the PI or in an imported Serialized Content, causes the locker to transition to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
--

* *Serialized Content*: {empty}
+
--
Same as for OCB.
--

State Machine::

OCB with Preset Nonce uses the OCB subset of <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>, omitting _Set_Aux_Value_. Its provisioned `N` and `N_len` replace the supplied nonce, and entering _Ready_ retains them. The tag-length operation that begins _Hash_Absorb_ occurs in _Ready_.

[NOTE]
====
The rationale of <<KLE-op-preset-value>> applies, with the nonce uniqueness of cite:[RFC7253] on the key and `N`.
A locker that computes the inverse block cipher would let software recover the nonce from the final partial blocks that this Machine decrypts, each of which reveals up to 120 bits of a `Pad`; a locker of OCB takes the nonce that software sets in _Set_Aux_Value_.
====

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-CMAC-mode]]
=== CMAC Functions

{empty}
(((KLE, Machine, CMAC)))
CMAC is a message authentication code built on a block cipher.
Tetsu Iwata and Kaoru Kurosawa designed it, and it became a NIST
Recommendation in May 2005 cite:[nist-SP-800-38B].

Parameters::

* `b` = 64 or 128, the block size of the underlying block cipher (cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}5.2).
* `k` = key size (`k` a multiple of 8).
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
| iii  | `pad`         | Padding: (−`kf`) mod 128.
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits.
 ** `hash`: `b` bits.
 ** `last_block_len`: an integer in the range 0 to `b`, serialized on 32 bits.
 ** `tag_len`: the number of bits that _Hash_Verify_ compares, an integer in the range 64 to `b`, or 0 before it is set; serialized on 32 bits.
 ** `tag_len_p`: the length of every MAC that the locker outputs, and the number of bits that every verification compares (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>): a multiple of 8 from 64 to `b`, and `b` if _KeyType_ = 1; held in _AuxInfo_, not serialized.
 ** `absorbed_any`: a boolean, set once a block has been absorbed in _Hash_Absorb_.
 ** `absorbed_all`: a boolean, set once the last block has been absorbed.

* *MDH: MachinePolicy*: bit 0 permits generating a CMAC (_Hash_Output_), and bit 1 verifying one (_Hash_Verify_); at least one bit must be set (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|    i  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
|   ii  | `b`           | `hash`
|  iii  | 32            | `last_block_len`
|   iv  | 32            | `tag_len`
|    v  | 1 (boolean)   | `absorbed_any`
|   vi  | 1 (boolean)   | `absorbed_all`
|  vii  | `pad`         | Padding: (−(`kf` + `b` + 66)) mod 128.
|===
--

//

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `double(S)` = `bswap(dbl(bswap(S)))`, the doubling of cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}6.1, where
**  `dbl(V)` = `V << 1` if `V[b-1]` = 0, else `(V << 1) xor (zeros(b-8) @ R~b~)`, with +
`R~b~` the constant of {sect}5.3: `R~128~` = `0b10000111` (`x^128^ + x^7^ + x^2^ + x + 1`) for `b` = 128, and +
`R~64~` = `0b00011011` (`x^64^ + x^4^ + x^3^ + x + 1`) for `b` = 64.
** For every block cipher with `b` = 128, SM4 included, `dbl` is the `update_mask` of `AES128_XEX` (<<KLE-XEX-XTS-modes>>),
  and `double` is the OCB3 doubling of <<KLE-OCB-mode>>.
* `gen_subkeys(K) -> (K1,K2)` generates subkeys `K1` and `K2` from `K` as cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}6.1 does:

 . `L <- enc_blk(K, zeros(b))`
 . `K1 <- double(L)`
 . `K2 <- double(K1)`
 . return `(K1, K2)`.

State Machine::

* *States:*
 ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Absorb data into the CMAC function.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_: Set the length of the last block to absorb, then absorb it.
 ** _Hash_Output_: Output the CMAC.
 ** _Hash_Verify_: Compare the CMAC with a value supplied by software, without disclosing it.
 ** _Success_: The CC has completed the CMAC operation.
 ** _Failure_: A CMAC verification has failed.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ -> _Hash_Output_ -> _Success_.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ -> _Hash_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** From any valid state to State _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** Entering State _Ready_:
  *** `last_block_len <- 0`
  *** `tag_len <- 0`
  *** `hash <- zeros(b)`
  *** `absorbed_any <- false`
  *** `absorbed_all <- false`

 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_, a Form B `kl.exec`, which may span several blocks, absorbs each `b`-bit block `P` of `INPUT` in turn (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>):
+
--
   . `hash <- enc_blk(key, hash xor P)`.
   . `absorbed_any <- true`.
--
** When entering State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, a Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_last_block` invokes `FINAL_BLOCK_ENTRY(0, b, 8)` (<<KLE-op-final-block-entry>>), which sets `last_block_len`, the bit length of the last block, with these changes:
+
--
. `Xs` = 0 denotes the empty message: with `absorbed_any` = `true`, it transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. If `Xs` = 0, the empty final block is absorbed at once, without a `kl.exec` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>):
.. `(K1, K2) <- gen_subkeys(key)`,
.. `hash <- enc_blk(key, hash xor pad10(zeros(b), 0) xor K2)`, and
.. `absorbed_all <- true`.
. The `kl.exec` leaves `last_block_len` unchanged: `absorbed_all` records that the last block is absorbed.
--

** In State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, a single Form B `kl.exec` absorbs the last block:
+
--
  . `(K1, K2) <- gen_subkeys(key)`.
  . if `last_block_len` = `b`, then:
    .. `tmp <- hash xor INPUT[b-1:0] xor K1`
  . else, that is if `0` < `last_block_len` < `b`:
    .. `tmp <- hash xor pad10(INPUT, last_block_len) xor K2`
  . `hash <- enc_blk(key, tmp)`
  . `absorbed_all <- true`

A `kl.exec` with `absorbed_all` = `true` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
--

** A `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_output` enters State _Hash_Output_; with `absorbed_all` = `false`, it transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ instead.

** In State _Hash_Output_, a single Form C `kl.exec`, which processes a single block (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>), outputs the CMAC:
+
--
. `OUTPUT <- trunc(hash, tag_len_p)`, the first `tag_len_p`/8 bytes of `hash` and the truncated tag of cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}6.2 when `tag_len_p` < `b`, with output sizing under Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>.
. The CC transitions to State _Success_.
--

** State _Hash_Output_, as a `kl.derive` source (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>), supplies the `b`/8 bytes of `hash`.

** A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_verify` enters _Hash_Verify_ by invoking `SET_TAG_LEN(b, 64)` (<<KLE-op-verify-tag>>).
With `absorbed_all` = `false`, the `kl.setst` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

** In State _Hash_Verify_, a single Form B `kl.exec` verifies the tag by invoking `VERIFY_TAG(hash)` (<<KLE-op-verify-tag>>), which compares `INPUT[tag_len-1:0]` with `hash[tag_len-1:0]`, the first `tag_len`/8 bytes of the CMAC, MSB~Tlen~ of cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}6.2.

//

Code examples: <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-CMAC-mode-example>>.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs]]
=== Hash and MAC Functions, and XOFs

(((KLE, Machine, general hashing)))(((KLE, Machine, MAC functions)))(((KLE, Machine, XOF)))This section defines an interface for hash functions, MAC functions and XOFs.
No interface fits every construction, but the one defined here covers the most common, such as SHA-2 cite:[nist-fips-180-4], SHA-3, and SHAKE cite:[nist-fips-202].

Every Machine that instantiates it pads the message as its referenced standard prescribes:
the caller supplies the message alone, and the `kl.setst` that ends the message completes the
padding and applies any length encoding of that standard.
One instruction sequence serves all of them (<<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-hash-example>>).

It is highly recommended that a new Machine for a hash function, MAC function or XOF work with the same instruction sequence, unless a reason prevents it.

Parameters::

* `b` = block size (`b` a multiple of 8): the size of an absorbed block, which may differ from the size of the internal state. For a sponge function, `b` is the _rate_.
* `t` = output block length (`t` a multiple of 8): the size of the digest, or of each block that the output stage emits between two `update()`. This description assumes `t` {le} `b`.
* `d` = digest length (`d` a multiple of 8) of a fixed-output Machine: `t`, unless the Machine states otherwise, as <<KLE-Ascon-Hash256>> does. An XOF has no `d`.
* `n` = size of the internal state kept by the function (`n` a multiple of 8). For a sponge function, `n-b` is the _capacity_ of the function.
* `k` = key size (`k` a multiple of 8). Present only if keyed, i.e., a MAC.
* `state_offset` = offset into the state at which the block is absorbed.
* `tag_offset` = offset of the tag into the state, 0 unless the Machine states otherwise.
  A Machine must satisfy `state_offset` {le} `tag_offset` and `tag_offset` + `t` {le} `state_offset` + `b` for a sponge, so that every output bit comes from the rate,
  and `tag_offset` + `t` {le} `n` otherwise.
* `g` = granularity: 8 bits in _Hash_Absorb_ and _Hash_Output_, for every instantiation; a State that loads a field, such as _Set_Key_ of <<KLE-HMAC>>, uses it too unless the Machine states otherwise.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)             | Field
.2+|  ii  | If keyed: `k` or 64  | `key` or System Key Identifier
<|          If unkeyed: 0          | none: the Provisioning Input is the MDH only
|  iii | `pad`                   | Padding: (−`kf`) mod 128 if keyed, 0 if unkeyed.
|===
--

* *MDH: MachinePolicy*: unless the instantiating Machine states otherwise, no State is gated by _MachinePolicy_
  (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>), and a non-zero value is unsupported Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits. Present only if keyed, i.e., a MAC.
 ** `state`: `n` bits.
 ** `last_block_len`: int. Bit length of the final piece to be absorbed in _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_.
    In _Hash_Output_Bits_, after the message has ended, the field is called `read_len`: the number of bits still to be read before the next `kl.setst`.
 ** `input_base`/`output_base` (same field): int. Offset within the input or output at which to read or write.
 ** `block_base`: int. Length of the data absorbed so far into the current block or, in _Hash_Output_ and _Hash_Output_Bits_, emitted so far from the current `t`-bit output block.
 ** `out_len`: int. In _Hash_Output_ and _Hash_Output_Bits_, the number of output bits still to be emitted, or 0 for an XOF without bound; zero in every other State.
 ** `cumul_len`: int. Cumulative length of the data absorbed so far.
 ** `block`: `b` bits. Present only if a partial block is kept separately, for instance when inputs are not absorbed directly into `state`.
 ** `Sealed`: Boolean, bit 1 of _StateExtension_, set when a sealed source (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sealed>>) enters the message input, and cleared only on entering _Ready_.
    A _StateExtension_ @ _State_ with `Sealed` set in a State other than _Hash_Absorb_, _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, _Hash_Output_ and _Success_ is not supported by the Machine: an SCC that carries it is invalid Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[cols="^6%,<21%,<18%,<55%",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)          | Field             | Notes
.2+| i  | If keyed: `k` or 64  | `key` or SKID     | --
<|        If unkeyed: 0        | none              | --
|   ii | `n`                  | `state`           | --
|  iii | 16                   | `last_block_len`    | The bit length of the final piece still to be absorbed, 0 once the message ends; in _Hash_Output_Bits_, `read_len`.
|   iv | 16                    | `block_base`     | Amount of data, in bits, stored so far into `block` or accumulated (usually XORed) into `state`; in _Hash_Output_ and _Hash_Output_Bits_, the amount emitted from the current output block.
| v, vi | 32                   | `out_len`         | Unless the Machine uses these bits otherwise, as <<KLE-BLAKE2>> does.
|   vii | 64                   | `cumul_len`       | Cumulative length of the data absorbed so far; its width limits the length of a message (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>). Present only if the padding or another function of the Machine uses it, or under HMAC (<<KLE-HMAC>>).
|  viii | `b`                  | `block`           | Only if the block is stored separately from the `state`.
|    ix | `pad`                | Padding           | Zero; (−(`kf` + `n` + 64 + `c'` + `b'`)) mod 128 bits, where `kf` is 0 if unkeyed, `c'` is 64 if `cumul_len` is present and 0 otherwise, and `b'` is `b` if `block` is present and 0 otherwise.
|===

`input_base`/`output_base` is not serialized: it is set to 8 {times} `klstart` at the start of every `kl.exec`.
--

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `process_block()`: Process `block` into the hash or MAC.
* `finalize()`: Apply to the absorbed message the padding, and any length encoding or final-block processing, of
  the referenced standard, and process `state`, in place, so that it holds the digest, or the first `t` bits of
  output, at `tag_offset`.
* `update()`: Update `state` during the output stage.
  A fixed-output Machine with `d` {le} `t` needs none: its output loop enters _Success_ and returns before it reaches `update()`.

State Machine::

* *States:* _Ready_, _Hash_Absorb_, _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, _Hash_Output_, _Hash_Output_Bits_ and _Success_.

 ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Absorb the message into the hash function.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_: Set the bit length of the final piece of the message, then absorb it.
 ** _Hash_Output_: Output the hash.
 ** _Hash_Output_Bits_: Output the hash a set number of bits at a time.
 ** _Success_: The CC has completed the operation (an XOF reaches it only with a bounded output).

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Hash_Absorb_ -> { _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ -> } _Hash_Output_ -> _Success_.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_, or _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ with `last_block_len` = 0, -> _Hash_Output_Bits_; _Hash_Output_ -> _Hash_Output_Bits_ -> _Success_; and _Hash_Output_Bits_ -> _Hash_Output_Bits_.
 ** The Machine makes no transition into _Hash_Output_, _Hash_Output_Bits_, or the _Hash_Verify_ of a MAC, by itself: a `kl.setst` naming it from _Hash_Absorb_ or _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ ends the message.
 ** From any valid state to State _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** Upon entering State _Ready_:
  *** `last_block_len`, `block_base`, `cumul_len`, `out_len` and `block` are set to zero, and `Sealed` is cleared.
  *** `state`: the initial value is set as specified by the Machine.

 ** The `kl.setst` that enters _Hash_Absorb_ sets `last_block_len` {leftarrow} `b` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>).

 ** State _Hash_Absorb_ invokes macro `PROCESS_VLI` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>) as:
+
`{fournbsp}PROCESS_VLI(mode, 0, block, b, state, n,` +
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp} input_base, block_base, state_offset, cumul_len, 8, process_block(),` +
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp} Hash_Absorb_Last_Block, None, last_block_len, None)`,
+
where:
+
--
** `state_offset` is Machine-defined;
** `cumul_len` is `None` where neither the Machine nor <<KLE-HMAC>> uses it;
** `finalize()` is `None` in this invocation: it is performed once, by the `kl.setst` that ends the message;
** `mode` is:
*** `assign` for a construction that fills a separate `block` and then processes it into `state` through `process_block()`, and
*** `xor_accumulate` for one that absorbs the input directly into `state`, as a sponge does; each instantiation states which it uses.
--
+
A `kl.exec` absorbs any whole number of bytes. Its interruption points are those of <<KLE-process-VLI>>: 0 and, if the locker holds `block_base` = 0 when it is issued, every byte offset.

 ** State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, entered from _Hash_Absorb_:
+
--
. A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_last_block` and auxiliary operand `Xs`, `Xs` = 0 for Form A, sets `last_block_len <- Xs` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>). `Xs` must satisfy:
.. 0 {le} `Xs` {le} `b`;
.. `Xs` is a multiple of 8, unless the Machine admits other bit lengths as Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>> permits.
. Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. If `last_block_len` = 0, nothing more happens: the final piece is empty, and the message is what _Hash_Absorb_ absorbed.
No `kl.exec` is needed; any `kl.exec` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rules <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>> and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-outcome-valid-state>>).
. If `last_block_len` {ne} 0, a single Form B `kl.exec` absorbs the final piece as <<KLE-process-VLI>> specifies for `last_block_state`:
.. unless the Machine states otherwise, the piece is the `last_block_len` least significant bits of `INPUT`;
.. its only interruption point is 0 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>).
--

 ** From _Hash_Absorb_, or from _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ with `last_block_len` = 0, the `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Output_ ends the message:
+
--
. Issued from _Hash_Absorb_, it gives the result of an empty final piece followed by the same `kl.setst`.
. It is of Form A or of Form B, unless the Machine requires Form A.
. A non-zero auxiliary operand `Xs` is the output length in bits, and must:
.. be at most `d` for a fixed-output Machine, and below 2^32^ for an XOF;
.. be a multiple of 8, unless the Machine states otherwise, as <<KLE-KMAC>> does for KMAC128 and KMAC256;
.. be at least the minimum that a MAC may set, as <<KLE-HMAC>> does with 64 bits and <<KLE-KMAC>> with 32 bits for KMAC128 and KMAC256.

Any other non-zero `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_, before `finalize()`.
--
+
In _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, `last_block_len` {ne} 0 means that the final piece is not yet absorbed: a `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Output_, _Hash_Output_Bits_, or the _Hash_Verify_ of a MAC, then transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

 ** In State _Hash_Output_:
 *** Upon entering the state, the following operations are first performed:
 . `finalize()`
 . `block_base <- 0` and `last_block_len <- 0`
 . `out_len <- Xs` for a Form B `kl.setst` with `Xs` {ne} 0; otherwise `out_len <- d` for a fixed-output Machine, and `out_len <- 0` for an XOF.
*** For each Form C `kl.exec` instruction, the
    following operations are performed:
+
--
    * `local amount : int`, `{nbsp}` amount of data to copy from `state` to `OUTPUT`

[]
   . `output_base <- 8 {times} klstart` // 0 when the instruction starts
    . while (`output_base` < `KLLEN`) do:
     .. `amount <- min(KLLEN - output_base, t - block_base)`, and, if `out_len` {ne} 0, `amount <- min(amount, out_len)`
     .. ``OUTPUT[output_base + amount - 1 : output_base] <- `` +
           ``{fournbsp}state[tag_offset + block_base + amount - 1 : tag_offset + block_base]``
     .. `output_base <- output_base + amount`
     .. `block_base <- block_base + amount`
     .. if `out_len` {ne} 0 then:
      ... `out_len <- out_len - amount`
      ... if `out_len` = 0, any bits of `OUTPUT` beyond `output_base` are cleared,
          the locker transitions to _Success_, and the instruction returns.
     .. if (`block_base` = `t`) then:
      ... `update()`
      ... `block_base <- 0`
      ... Here, and only here, the instruction can be interrupted while `output_base` < `KLLEN`, with `klstart <- output_base / 8`.
          A halt here leaves `block_base` = 0.
--
+
The output has as many bits as `out_len` holds on entering the State, or is unbounded for an XOF without bound, and is emitted in any whole number of bytes.
+
The interruption points of `kl.exec` in _Hash_Output_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-interruption-point>>) are 0 and, if the locker holds `block_base` = 0 when the `kl.exec` is issued, any byte offset, whatever `out_len`.

 ** In State _Hash_Output_Bits_:
 *** A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_output_bits` and auxiliary operand `Xs`, issued in _Hash_Output_ or _Hash_Output_Bits_, enters or stays in the State and sets `read_len <- Xs`.
     It performs no `finalize()`, keeps `block_base` and `out_len`, and discards any bits that an earlier `read_len` left unread.
     Issued from _Hash_Absorb_, or from _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ with `last_block_len` = 0, it ends the message: it gives the result of a Form A `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Output_ followed by the same `kl.setst`.
     The output is then that of a Form A entry, unbounded for an XOF, and a Machine that requires Form B into _Hash_Output_, as <<KLE-KMAC>> does for KMAC128 and KMAC256, transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
     A Form A `kl.setst`, `Xs` = 0, or `Xs` {ge} 2^16^ transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
 *** Each Form C `kl.exec` runs the loop of _Hash_Output_ with these changes:
  . `amount` is also at most `read_len`, and `read_len <- read_len - amount` follows `block_base <- block_base + amount`;
  . after the `block_base` = `t` step, if `read_len` = 0, any bits of `OUTPUT` beyond `output_base` are cleared, and the instruction returns, the locker staying in _Hash_Output_Bits_;
  . the instruction can be interrupted wherever `output_base` is a multiple of 8 and less than `KLLEN` and `read_len` {ne} 0, inside a block too, and nowhere else, with `klstart <- output_base / 8`.
+
A Form C `kl.exec` issued with `read_len` = 0 transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
The interruption points of `kl.exec` in _Hash_Output_Bits_ are 0 and any byte offset, whatever `block_base`.
As a `kl.derive` source (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>), the locker supplies the output that follows `block_base`, as in _Hash_Output_, and keeps `read_len`.

 ** A _sealed_ locker, one with `Sealed` set or, in <<KLE-HMAC>>, `KeySealed`, admits no `kl.exec` in _Hash_Output_ and no `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Output_Bits_ or _Hash_Verify_: each transitions it to Error State _Invalid_.
    Its output in _Hash_Output_ is a sealed source (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sealed>>).

The following are Machine-specific, and the code example of <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-hash>> uses none of them:

* the parameters `nn` and `kk` of <<KLE-BLAKE2>>, set by an operation in _Ready_;
* the customization string of <<KLE-Ascon-CXOF128>>, absorbed in _Set_Aux_Value_;
* the key of the NIK variant of <<KLE-HMAC>>, loaded in _Set_Key_;
* the verification of a MAC in _Hash_Verify_ (<<KLE-HMAC>>, <<KLE-KMAC>>).

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-SHA-2]]
=== SHA-2 Family Specific Rules

(((KLE, Machine, SHA-2)))The primitives of the SHA-2 family cite:[nist-fips-180-4] (SHA-224, SHA-256, SHA-384,
SHA-512, SHA-512/224, and SHA-512/256) are instantiations of the algorithm specified in
<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, and only the differences from the behavior described
there are given here.

Parameters::

* The word size `w`, and `b`, `n`, and `t`, as given in <<KLE-SHA-2-parameters>>; the
  internal state consists of the eight `w`-bit chaining variables of
  cite:[nist-fips-180-4].
// Before byte-granular hash inputs:
// * Granularity: 32 bits. It constrains no `KLLEN`, neither in _Hash_Absorb_, since `max_len` = 0 (<<KLE-process-VLI>>), nor in
//   _Hash_Output_, which emits any whole number of bytes (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>). It bounds the
//   `length` of a `kl.derive` of a shared secret (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-checks>> item 6), and sets the interruption points
//   of _Hash_Absorb_ (<<KLE-process-VLI>>).

[[KLE-SHA-2-parameters]]
.Parameters of the SHA-2 family and SM3
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="<,^,^,^,^",options="header"]
|===
| Function    | `w` | `b`  | `n`  | `t`
| SHA-224     |  32 |  512 |  256 | 224
| SHA-256     |  32 |  512 |  256 | 256
| SHA-384     |  64 | 1024 |  512 | 384
| SHA-512     |  64 | 1024 |  512 | 512
| SHA-512/224 |  64 | 1024 |  512 | 224
| SHA-512/256 |  64 | 1024 |  512 | 256
| SM3 (<<KLE-SM3>>) | 32 | 512 | 256 | 256
|===

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*: the MDH only (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, unkeyed).

* *Internal State*:

   ** `state`: `n` bits, holding the eight chaining variables `H~i~` big-endian:
      `state[(i+1)w-1 : iw]` = `bswap(bin(H~i~, w))`, for `0` {le} `i` < 8.
   ** `block` (`b` bits).
   ** `block_base`, `last_block_len` and `out_len` (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).
   ** `cumul_len`, the number of message bits absorbed.

* *Serialized Content*: as prescribed by <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, with `out_len` in Positions v and vi,
  and `cumul_len` and `block` present.

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `process_block()`: applies the compression function of cite:[nist-fips-180-4] {sect}6 for the
  selected function to absorb the current `block` into `state`, message word `j` being
  `int(bswap(block[(j+1)w-1 : jw]))`, for `0` {le} `j` < 16.
* `finalize()`: absorbs the rest of the padding of cite:[nist-fips-180-4] {sect}5.1.1 and {sect}5.1.2 for a message
  of {ell} bits, by steps 3.a to 3.e and 3.g of <<KLE-process-VLI>>, so that `cumul_len` does not count it.
  This rest is the byte `0x80` if `cumul_len` is a multiple of 8 (otherwise the final piece carries the bit 1 already),
  then zero bytes until `block_base` = `b` − 2`w`, then ``bswap(bin(``{ell}``, 2w))``.
