  {ell} is `cumul_len`, or, under HMAC, the length that <<KLE-HMAC>> states.

State Machine::

* *States and Allowed State Transitions:* those of <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>.

* *Behavior:*
 ** Upon entering State _Ready_, `state` is set to the initial hash value of the selected function
    per cite:[nist-fips-180-4] {sect}5.3.
 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_, `PROCESS_VLI` is invoked as in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, with `mode` = `assign`,
    `state_offset` = 0 and `cumul_len` tracked.
+
NOTE: `mode` is `assign` because the SHA-2 family fills a separate `block` and compresses it into `state`.
 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_:
+
--
. The final piece is the {lceil}``last_block_len``/8{rceil} least significant bytes of `INPUT`; the other bits of `INPUT` are ignored.
. Let `q` = `last_block_len` mod 8. If `q` {ne} 0:
.. the last of these bytes carries the final `q` bits of the piece in its `q` most significant positions, since cite:[nist-fips-180-4] orders the bits of a byte from the most significant (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>, second bullet);
.. the Machine replaces the other 8 − `q` bits of that byte by the bit 1 that starts the padding, followed by zeros.
. The `kl.exec` of `last_block_state` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>) absorbs these bytes: its step 3, with all sub-steps, uses 8 {times} {lceil}``last_block_len``/8{rceil} in place of `last_block_len`.
. In place of the increments of step 3.f of the `kl.exec` in _Current_State_, `cumul_len` advances once by `last_block_len` itself, before step 5 clears `last_block_len`.
--
 ** In State _Hash_Output_, the bytes emitted are the chaining variables in the big-endian encoding,
    truncated to the number of bits that `out_len` holds on entering the State (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-SM3]]
=== SM3 Specific Rules

{empty}
(((KLE, Machine, SM3)))
The ShangMi 3 hash function, standardized as GB/T 32905-2016 cite:[GBT-32905-2016], follows the rules
of SHA-256 (<<KLE-SHA-2>>), with the parameters of row SM3 of <<KLE-SHA-2-parameters>>, except that
its initial hash value and compression function are those of the SM3 standard.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-BLAKE2]]
=== BLAKE2 Specific Rules

(((KLE, Machine, BLAKE2)))BLAKE2b and BLAKE2s cite:[RFC7693], unkeyed or keyed, instantiate <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>; only the
differences are given here.
`nn` and `kk` are the digest and key lengths in bytes.

Parameters::

* `w`, `r` (the rounds of `F`), `b`, `n`, `nn~max~`, `kk~max~` and the rotation constants of `G`, as in
  <<KLE-BLAKE2-parameters>>; `t` = 8 {times} `nn`.

[[KLE-BLAKE2-parameters]]
.Parameters of BLAKE2
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="<,^,^,^,^,^,^,^",options="header"]
|===
| Function | `w` | `r` | `b`  | `n` | `nn~max~` | `kk~max~` | (`R1`, `R2`, `R3`, `R4`)
| BLAKE2b  |  64 |  12 | 1024 | 512 |    64     |    64     | (32, 24, 16, 63)
| BLAKE2s  |  32 |  10 |  512 | 256 |    32     |    32     | (16, 12, 8, 7)
|===

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*: the MDH only, or, with a system key (_KeyType_ = 1):
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits) | Field
|  ii  | 64          | System Key Identifier
| iii  | 64          | Padding
|===

`kk` is the length of the key that the SKS returns; a length outside 1 to `kk~max~` transitions the locker to Error State
_Invalid_ (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-system-keys>>). When an import resolves the SKID again, a key whose length differs
from the restored `kk` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
_KeyType_ = 2 is invalid: the Machine holds no key by value.
A caller with its own key absorbs it (see below).
--

* *MDH: MachinePolicy*: without a system key, as in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>.
  With a system key, the Machine is a MAC that only generates tags: bit 0 enables _Hash_Output_
  (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>); _MachinePolicy_ = 0 is invalid Metadata, and any set bit other than bit 0 is unsupported Metadata
  (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).

* *Internal State*:
 ** `nn`, with 1 {le} `nn` {le} `nn~max~`, and `kk`, with `kk` {le} `kk~max~`; an import that writes a value outside
    these ranges transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
 ** `state`: the words `h~i~` of cite:[RFC7693], little-endian: `state[(i+1)w-1 : iw]` = `bin(h~i~, w)`, for `0` {le} `i` < 8.
 ** `block`, `block_base`, `last_block_len`, `out_len`, and `cumul_len`, which also counts a key block, so that
    `cumul_len`/8 is the counter of cite:[RFC7693] {sect}3.2.
 ** `syskey`: with a system key, the key that the SKID resolves to, zero-padded to `b` bits. It is not serialized: the
    SKID in Position i of the Serialized Content stands for it (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>).

* *Serialized Content*: as in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, with `cumul_len` and `block` present, and:
 ** the SKID, if any, in Position i;
 ** `nn` in Position v and `kk` in Position vi, 16 bits each, `nn` in the lower half;
 ** in _Hash_Output_ and _Hash_Output_Bits_, `out_len` in the lower 32 bits of Position vii, the upper 32 bits zero, in place of `cumul_len`, which `finalize()` has consumed.

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `F(f)`: the compression function of cite:[RFC7693] {sect}3.2 on `state`, with message words
  `m~j~` = `int(block[(j+1)w-1 : jw])`, the counter `cumul_len`/8, and the final-block flag `f`.
* `process_block()`: `F(false)`.
* `finalize()` performs the first of the following that applies, then sets `block_base` {leftarrow} 0:
** with `block_base` > 0: zeroes `block[b-1 : block_base]` and performs `F(true)`;
** with `block_base` = 0, which only an empty message leaves, and a system key: `block` {leftarrow} `syskey`,
   `cumul_len` {leftarrow} `b`, `F(true)` and `block` {leftarrow} `zeros(b)`;
** with `block_base` = 0, no system key and `kk` = 0: `F(true)` on the zero `block`;
** with `block_base` = 0, no system key and `kk` {ne} 0: the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_, since
   the caller's key block is missing.

State Machine::

* *States:* _Ready_, _Hash_Absorb_, _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, _Hash_Output_, _Hash_Output_Bits_, and _Success_.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** As in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>; the `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Output_ ends the message held in `block`, also
    the empty one.
 ** From any valid state to State _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior*, where it differs from <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>:
 ** At the completion of a provisioning, before the operations of entering _Ready_, `nn` {leftarrow} `nn~max~` and
    `kk` {leftarrow} the length of the system key, or 0 without one.
    The completion of a system-specific import does the same for each of them that the converted Content does not
    configure (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-completion>>).
    Every other entry into _Ready_ keeps `nn` and `kk`.
 ** When entering State _Ready_, `state` is the IV of cite:[RFC7693] {sect}2.6, with `h~0~` XORed with
    `bin(0x01010000 + kk {times} 256 + nn, w)` ({sect}2.5, {sect}3.3).
 ** In State _Ready_, setting the parameters is an operation, not a State (Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>>):
+
--
. a Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `Xs` sets `nn` {leftarrow} `Xs[7:0]` and, without a system key,
  `kk` {leftarrow} `Xs[15:8]`, and recomputes `h~0~`;
. `Xs` must satisfy 1 {le} `Xs[7:0]` {le} `nn~max~` and `Xs[15:8]` {le} `kk~max~`, and its bits above bit 15 must
  be 0. With a system key, `Xs[15:8]` must be 0.

Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
--
 ** With a system key, the instruction that absorbs the first byte of the message compresses the key block before
    it absorbs that byte: `block` {leftarrow} `syskey`, `cumul_len` {leftarrow} `b`, `F(false)`, and `block`
    {leftarrow} `zeros(b)`.
    That instruction is a `kl.exec` or a `kl.derive` in _Hash_Absorb_, or the `kl.exec` of _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_.
    `cumul_len` = 0 records that the key block is still pending; for the empty message, `finalize()` compresses it
    as the final block.
+
NOTE: `syskey` is in `block` only between two steps of one instruction, which cannot halt between them, so no Serialized Content holds it (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>).
 ** A caller with its own key, and no system key:
+
--
. sets `kk` to the length of the key in bytes by the _Set_Aux_Value_ operation;
. absorbs the key, zero-padded to `b` bits, as the first block of the message (cite:[RFC7693] {sect}3.3);
. for an empty message, absorbs nothing more, and `finalize()` compresses the key block as the final block.
--
+
NOTE: The Machine does not tell the key block from the message: it checks neither that `kk` matches the length of the key nor that the key is padded with zeros, and a mismatch only yields a different hash.
 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_, `PROCESS_VLI` is invoked with `max_len` = 0, `state_offset` = 0 and `mode` = `assign`, with these differences:
+
--
. Step 3.g is postponed: a `block` filled to `block_base` = `b` is kept.
. The iteration of step 3 that absorbs the next byte of the message first compresses that `block` by `F(false)` and sets `block_base` {leftarrow} 0, before step 3.a, so that the counter `cumul_len`/8 does not yet count that byte.
. With a system key, the key block is compressed first, as stated above.
. The interruption points of `kl.exec` in _Hash_Absorb_ are 0 and, if the locker holds `block_base` = 0 or `block_base` = `b` when the `kl.exec` is issued, any byte offset.
A halt leaves the full block held, with `block_base` = `b`, so that its resumption meets that condition.
--

 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ follows <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, with `Xs` a multiple of 8 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
In the `kl.exec` of `last_block_state` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>), step 3.c is postponed in the same way:
+
--
. A full block, including one held from _Hash_Absorb_, is compressed by `F(false)`, with `block_base` {leftarrow} 0, at the start of the loop iteration that absorbs the next byte of the final piece, before its `amount` is computed.
. A final piece that fills its block is held for `finalize()`.
--

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-HMAC]]
=== HMAC

(((KLE, Machine, HMAC)))Hash function Machines suffice to compute HMACs, but an environment may want to hide the HMAC key.
This section extends <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>> accordingly.

`finalize()` of `H` pads as in `H`; for SHA-2 (<<KLE-SHA-2>>) and SM3 (<<KLE-SM3>>) it encodes {ell} = `b` + `cumul_len` bits for the inner hash and `b` + `t` bits for the outer, since `absorb(K0 xor ipad)` and `absorb(K0 xor opad)` are not counted in `cumul_len`.

Parameters::
+
--
* `H` = the underlying hash function.
* `b` = the input block size of `H`: its parameter `b`, which for SHA-3 is the rate (<<KLE-SHA-3-parameters>>).
* `t` = the digest size: the parameter `t` of `H` (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).
* Granularity: same as that of the underlying hash function.

The two padding constants of cite:[nist-fips-198-1] are:

* `ipad` = the byte `0x36` repeated `b`/8 times, and
* `opad` = the byte `0x5c` repeated `b`/8 times,

both `b` bits long. All bytes of each constant are equal, so its
representation does not depend on the byte order of <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>.
--

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
Each HMAC Machine has two variants, which the _Machine_ field distinguishes (<<KLE-exec-encodings>>): by _Type_ for the SHA-2 and SHA-3 HMACs, and by _Mode_ for `SM3_HMAC`.

The PI of the “No Initial Key” (NIK) variant is the MDH alone.

The PI of the “Key In PI” (KIP) variant has the following format:

[cols="^7%,<23%,<70%",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)               | Field
|  ii  | `b` or 64                 | `K0`, the key already reduced to `b` bits (see below), or a System Key Identifier.
| iii  | 0 or 64                   | Padding
|===

The key provisioned or held in a CC is the value `K0` of cite:[nist-fips-198-1] {sect}4, Steps 1 to 3: the key `K` padded with zeros to `b` bits, or, if `K` is longer than `b` bits, `H(K)` padded with zeros to `b` bits.
Whoever provisions the key derives `K0` from it.
With _KeyType_ = 1, the SKS returns `K`, and the KLE unit forms `K0` from it by the same Steps.
A system-specific import (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-completion>>) that configures the `K0` of a NIK CC forms it by the same Steps from the key that the format carries, and sets `HasKey`, as a completed load of `K0` does (Rule <<KLE-MGR-sysfmt-standard>>).
--

* *Internal State*:
  ** `hash_fn_state`: the entire state of `H`, with the following fields:
  *** `state`: `n` bits.
  *** `last_block_len`, `out_len`: int (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).
  *** `input_base`: int. Offset within input/output to read from/write to.
  *** `block_base`: int. Length of the data absorbed so far into the current block or, in _Hash_Output_ and _Hash_Output_Bits_, emitted so far from the current `t`-bit output block (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).
  *** `cumul_len`: int. Cumulative length of data absorbed so far, tracked by every HMAC Machine (for the `K0` load in _Set_Key_, and for the padding of SHA-2 and SM3).
  *** `block`: `b` bits. Included only if a partial block is maintained, for instance when the inputs are not directly absorbed into `state`.
 ** `K0`: `b` bits, the value `K0` as per cite:[nist-fips-198-1] {sect}4, Steps 1 to 3.
 ** `tag_len`: the number of bits that _Hash_Verify_ compares, 64 to `t`, or 0 before it is set.
 ** `tag_len_p`: the number of bits that every verification compares (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>): a multiple of 8 from 64 to `t`, and `t` if _KeyType_ = 1; held in _AuxInfo_, not serialized.
 ** `HasKey` (NIK variant only): Boolean, bit 0 of _StateExtension_, recording that `K0` holds a completely loaded key (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>).
 ** `KeySealed` (NIK variant only): Boolean, bit 2 of _StateExtension_, set when a sealed source (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sealed>>) loads `K0`, and cleared on entering _Set_Key_.
    A _StateExtension_ @ _State_ with `KeySealed` set and `HasKey` clear, or set in _Hash_Output_Bits_, _Hash_Verify_ or _Failure_, is not supported by the Machine: an SCC that carries it is invalid Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).
    The KIP variant uses only `Sealed` (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[cols="^7%,<23%,<70%",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)              | Field
|  i   | variable                 | Serialized Content of the underlying hash function, in its entirety, including its Padding.
| ii   | `b` or 64                | `K0`, or a System Key Identifier.
| iii  | 16                       | `tag_len`
|  iv  | `pad`                    | Padding: (−(`b` + 16)) mod 128, or 48 with a System Key Identifier.
|===

If `H` is a SHA-3 function and _KeyType_ = 1, the `state` of position i is masked as in <<KLE-KMAC>> (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>).
--

//

State Machine:: {empty}
+
--
The NIK variant inserts State _Set_Key_ between _Ready_ and _Hash_Absorb_; both variants add States _Hash_Verify_ and
_Failure_.

The two variants differ only in how the key first reaches the CC.

In the KIP variant, `K0` is carried in the Provisioning Input.
The key is given by value (_KeyType_ = 0), as an SKID (_KeyType_ = 1), or as `b` random bits (_KeyType_ = 2).
A KIP CC cannot be re-keyed.

In the NIK variant, the Provisioning Input carries no key. `K0` must be loaded in State _Set_Key_ before _Hash_Absorb_ can be entered:
entering _Hash_Absorb_ requires `HasKey` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>), and leaving _Set_Key_ before the load completes zeroes `K0` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>).
A loaded key is kept across a return to _Ready_, so that one load of `K0` serves any number of messages.
--
+
* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** For the NIK variant: _Ready_ -> _Set_Key_ -> _Hash_Absorb_, and _Ready_ -> _Hash_Absorb_ once `K0` is loaded
    (`HasKey`); then as in the underlying hash function.
 ** For the NIK variant: _Set_Key_ -> _Set_Key_, which restarts the load (see below).
 ** For the KIP variant: _Ready_ -> _Hash_Absorb_; then as in the underlying hash function.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_, or _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ with `last_block_len` = 0, -> _Hash_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** From any valid state to State _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).
 ** _MachinePolicy_[0] gates MAC generation, and _MachinePolicy_[1] MAC verification (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** When entering _Ready_: `tag_len` {leftarrow} 0.

 ** State _Set_Key_ is `LOAD_LONG_FIELD(b, K0, K0, b, None, None, assign, g)` (<<KLE-op-load-long-field>>), with `g` the granularity of `H`:
    Form B ``kl.exec`` instructions load the `b`-bit `K0`, and a completed load sets `HasKey`.
    Entering _Set_Key_, also by a same-State `kl.setst`, which restarts the load, also clears `KeySealed`.
    A `kl.derive` into `K0` (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>) transfers a whole key `K` of `eff_length` {le} `b`/8 bytes
    (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-checks>> items 1 and 6):
  . it requires `klstart` = 0 and a source of exactly `eff_length` bytes: a source field, or the remaining `kl.exec` output of the source;
  . otherwise, the destination locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_, and the source is unchanged; a keystream of a keystream Machine (<<KLE-keystream-modes>>, OFB of <<KLE-chaining>>), or the output of an XOF without bound, has no length of its own and never meets the requirement of the previous step, so its `kl.derive` into `K0` always has this outcome;
  . it replaces all of `K0`, whatever a `kl.exec` loaded before it: `K0` {leftarrow} `K` zero-filled to `b` bits (cite:[nist-fips-198-1] {sect}4, Step 3);
  . it sets `block_base` {leftarrow} 0 and `cumul_len` {leftarrow} `b`, and sets `HasKey`, and `KeySealed` for a sealed source.

 ** When entering _Hash_Absorb_:
  . `state` is set to the initial value of the underlying hash function.
  . `block`, `block_base` and `cumul_len` are set to zero, and `last_block_len` {leftarrow} `b`, as in `H`.
  . `absorb(K0 xor ipad)`, not counted in `cumul_len`, where `absorb(x)` absorbs `x` as a `kl.exec` in
    _Hash_Absorb_ of `H` does.
  . If `H` keeps `block` apart from `state` (SHA-2, SM3), `block` {leftarrow} `zeros(b)`.
+
NOTE: `K0 xor ipad` is in `block` only between two steps of one instruction, so no Serialized Content holds it (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>).

 ** While in _Hash_Absorb_ and _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, the message is absorbed as in `H`, including the length and
    the bit order of the final piece (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
** When entering State _Hash_Output_ or _Hash_Verify_, the following steps replace the operations of `H` on entering
   _Hash_Output_:
+
--
   * local `inner : bits(t)`

[]
  . `finalize()` of `H`, which leaves the inner hash `H((K0 xor ipad) {vvert} message)` in
    `state[t+tag_offset-1 : tag_offset]`.
  . `inner <- state[t+tag_offset-1 : tag_offset]`.
  . Reinitialize `state` to the initial value of the underlying hash function `H`, and set `block`, `block_base` and
    `cumul_len` to zero.
  . `absorb(K0 xor opad)` as per `H`, not counted in `cumul_len`.
  . `absorb(inner)` as per `H`, which leaves `cumul_len` = `t`, so that the outer hash digests
    `(K0 xor opad) {vvert} H((K0 xor ipad) {vvert} message)`, as
    cite:[nist-fips-198-1] {sect}4 requires.
  . `finalize()` of `H`, `block_base` {leftarrow} 0 and `last_block_len` {leftarrow} 0. The `t`-bit HMAC value is then output in
    _Hash_Output_ as by `H`, with `out_len` set as in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, or verified in _Hash_Verify_.
--
+
A non-zero `Xs` of a Form B `kl.setst` into _Hash_Output_ is at least 64 (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).

** To verify an HMAC where _MachinePolicy_[1] is set (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>):
+
--
   . the Form B `kl.setst` that enters State _Hash_Verify_ invokes `SET_TAG_LEN(t, 64)` (<<KLE-op-verify-tag>>):
     `tag_len` {leftarrow} `min(Xs, t)`, which must equal `tag_len_p`;
   . in State _Hash_Verify_, the single Form B `kl.exec` invokes `VERIFY_TAG(state[tag_offset+t-1 : tag_offset])` (<<KLE-op-verify-tag>>):
     it compares `INPUT[tag_len-1:0]` with `state[tag_offset+tag_len-1 : tag_offset]`, the first `tag_len`/8 bytes of the HMAC.
--

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-SHA-3]]
=== SHA-3 Family Specific Rules

(((KLE, Machine, SHA-3)))The SHA-3 family comprises SHA3-224, SHA3-256, SHA3-384 and SHA3-512, written SHA3-``N``, and the XOFs SHAKE128 and SHAKE256.
All are parameter choices of KECCAK cite:[DBLP-conf-eurocrypt-BertoniDPA13], standardized by NIST cite:[nist-fips-202].
They are instantiations of the Machine of <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>; this section gives only the differences.

//

Parameters::

* `n` = 1600.
* `c` = capacity, `b` = rate = 1600 - `c`, and `t` = the number of output bits taken from
  `state` per squeeze, all as given in <<KLE-SHA-3-parameters>>.

[[KLE-SHA-3-parameters]]
.Parameters of the SHA-3 family
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="<,^,^,^,^,<",options="header"]
|===
| Function |  `c` | `b` = 1600 - `c` | `t` | XOF? | Suffix `D`
| SHA3-224 |  448 |       1152       |  224 |   no | `bin(2, 2)`
| SHA3-256 |  512 |       1088       |  256 |   no | `bin(2, 2)`
| SHA3-384 |  768 |        832       |  384 |   no | `bin(2, 2)`
| SHA3-512 | 1024 |        576       |  512 |   no | `bin(2, 2)`
| SHAKE128 |  256 |       1344       | 1344 |  yes | `bin(15, 4)`
| SHAKE256 |  512 |       1088       | 1088 |  yes | `bin(15, 4)`
|===

`bin(2, 2)` and `bin(15, 4)` are the suffixes that cite:[nist-fips-202] writes as `01` and `1111`, first bit leftmost: for SHA3-``N``, `D[0]` = 0 and `D[1]` = 1.

For the four fixed-output functions, `t` is the digest length, which is `c`/2, and a
single squeeze suffices. For the two extendable-output functions, `t` is the full rate
`b`, since a sponge makes exactly `b` bits available between successive applications of
the permutation.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*: the MDH only.

* *Internal State*:
 ** `state`: `n` bits.
 ** `input_base`: int.
 ** `block_base`: int.
 ** `last_block_len`: int.
 ** `out_len`: int (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).
 ** `cumul_len`: int, under HMAC (<<KLE-HMAC>>) only.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^,<,<",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|   i  | 1600            | `state`
|  ii  | 16              | `block_base`, in bits
| iii  | 16              | `last_block_len`, in bits
|  iv  | 32              | `out_len`, in bits
|   v  | 64 or 0         | `cumul_len`, under HMAC (<<KLE-HMAC>>) only
|  vi  | 0, or 64 with `cumul_len` | Padding
|===

This layout replaces that of <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, in which `last_block_len` precedes `block_base`.
--

//

Machine-Specific Functions:: {empty}
 * `P()`: computes `KECCAK-p[1600, 24]` on `state`, with result in place.
 * `process_block()`: there is no explicit operation to copy data into
   `state`, since the inputs are XORed directly into it; instead, `process_block`
   applies `P()` to the state.
 * `update()`: applies `P()` to the `state` during the digest output stage.

//

State Machine:: {empty}
* *States and Allowed State Transitions:* those of <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>.

* *Behavior:*
 ** Upon entering State _Ready_, `state` is zeroed.
 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_, the input data is XORed directly into `state`: `PROCESS_VLI` is invoked as in
    <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, with `block` = `state`, `mode` = `xor_accumulate`, `state_offset` = 0, and
    `cumul_len` = `None` except under HMAC.
 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, a final piece of 0 to `b` bits is absorbed (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>), so that the
    message may have any bit length. cite:[nist-fips-202] orders the bits within a byte as
    <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>> does, so `last_block_len` need not be a multiple of 8
    (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
 ** Upon transitioning to State _Hash_Output_, `finalize()`:
 . forms the bit string `S` of cite:[nist-fips-202] {sect}5.1 and {sect}B.2:
 .. `S[|D|-1:0]` holds the suffix `D` of <<KLE-SHA-3-parameters>>, with `D[0]` in `S[0]`;
 .. `pad10*1` follows: a 1 in `S[|D|]`, zeros, and a 1 in `S[|S|-1]`;
 .. `|S|` is the smallest length {ge} `|D|` + 2 that makes `block_base` + `|S|` a multiple of `b`;
 . XORs `S[b-block_base-1 : 0]` into `state[b-1 : block_base]`, and applies `P()`;
 . if `|S|` > `b` - `block_base`: XORs `S[|S|-1 : b-block_base]` into `state[b-1 : 0]`, and then applies `P()`;
 . leaves the output in the first `t` bits of the rate.
+
NOTE: `block_base` is less than `b`, since it returns to 0 when it reaches `b`. When `block_base` is a multiple of 8,
`S` fits in the current block: it is the byte `pad_byte` at `state[block_base+7 : block_base]`, with `pad_byte` = `0x06` for
SHA3-``N`` and `0x1F` for SHAKE128 and SHAKE256, XORed with the byte `0x80` at `state[b-1 : b-8]`.
Else, after a final piece whose length is not a multiple of 8, `S` starts within a byte, and a second block is
needed only when `b` - `block_base` < `|D|` + 2.

 ** In State _Hash_Output_, output is extracted as in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>:
 . SHA3-``N``: a digest of `d` bits, or of `Xs` bits after a non-zero `Xs`, at most `t` in either case, after which the locker transitions to _Success_;
 . SHAKE128 and SHAKE256: output of unbounded length, or of `Xs` bits after a non-zero `Xs`, after which the locker transitions to _Success_.

The rest of the Machine follows <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-KMAC]]
=== KMAC128, KMAC256, KMACXOF128, and KMACXOF256

(((KLE, Machine, KMAC)))(((KLE, Machine, KMACXOF)))KMAC is the keyed MAC built on cSHAKE, standardized by NIST in cite:[nist-SP-800-185].
It is defined only over the KECCAK permutation of SHA-3, and this section gives only its differences from <<KLE-SHA-3>>.

Parameters::

* `n` = 1600.
* KMAC128 and KMACXOF128 use `c` = 256 and rate `b` = 1344 bits (168 bytes);
  KMAC256 and KMACXOF256 use `c` = 512 and rate `b` = 1088 bits (136 bytes).
* `t` = `b`, as for the extendable-output functions of <<KLE-SHA-3-parameters>>.

Data Structures::
+
--
A CC does not hold the key `K` and the customization string `S` in their raw form. It
holds the two rate-sized blocks that cite:[nist-SP-800-185] derives from them. Whoever provisions the CC
prepares both, unless the KLE unit forms `key_block` from a System Key or a random `K` (see below):

`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `cshake_block` = `bytepad(encode_string("KMAC") {vvert} encode_string(S), b/8)`, and +
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `key_block{nbsp}{nbsp}{nbsp}` = `bytepad(encode_string(K), b/8)`,

where `bytepad`, `encode_string` and `left_encode` are as defined in cite:[nist-SP-800-185] {sect}2.3:

[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="<,^,^,^",options="header"]
|===
| Function           | rate `b`/8 | maximum \|`K`\| | maximum \|`S`\|
| KMAC128/KMACXOF128 | 168 bytes | 163 bytes | 157 bytes
| KMAC256/KMACXOF256 | 136 bytes | 131 bytes | 125 bytes
|===

Only a `K` and an `S` within these maximums fit in one block each. The Machine does not check that a `cshake_block` or `key_block` carried by value is well formed.

NOTE: cite:[nist-SP-800-185] admits a longer `K` or `S`, which `bytepad` spreads over more than one block,
and unlike the key of HMAC, no shorter value computes the same function.

With _KeyType_ = 1, the PI and the SCC carry a System Key Identifier in place of `key_block` (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>).
The SKS returns the key `K`, and the KLE unit forms `key_block` from it as above.
A `K` longer than the maximum in the table above transitions the locker to
Error State _Invalid_ (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-system-keys>>).
With _KeyType_ = 2 (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-KeyType-field>>), `K` is a random value of `c`/2 bits,
from which `key_block` is formed in the same way.
`cshake_block` is always carried by value.
With _KeyType_ = 1, the `state` field of the Serialized Content holds `state xor state_mask` in every State but _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>); an import removes the mask after SKID resolution, when it converts the content into the internal format.
--
+
* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `b`           | `cshake_block`
| iii  | `b` or 64     | `key_block` or System Key Identifier
|  iv  | 0             | Padding
|===
--

* *Internal State*: as for <<KLE-SHA-3>>, with the addition of `cshake_block` and `key_block`, and with `L` in place of `out_len`:
 ** `cshake_block`, `key_block`: `b` bits each; and
 ** `L`: 32 bits, the output length or bound in bits, or 0 for an unbounded KMACXOF output; in _Hash_Output_ and _Hash_Output_Bits_, the bits still to be emitted, as `out_len` (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>); from _Hash_Verify_ on, the tag length in bits.

* *Serialized Content*: as for <<KLE-SHA-3>>, with `L` in Position iv in place of `out_len`, and `cshake_block`
  and `key_block` appended, followed by an empty Padding. Explicitly:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^,<,<",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits) | Field
|   i  | 1600        | `state`
|  ii  | 16          | `block_base`
| iii  | 16          | `last_block_len`
|  iv  | 32          | `L`
|   v  | `b`         | `cshake_block`
|  vi  | `b` or 64   | `key_block` or System Key Identifier
| vii  | 0           | Padding
|===
--

//

Machine-Specific Functions::

 * `P()`, `process_block()` and `update()` are as in <<KLE-SHA-3>>.
 * `right_encode(x)`, as in cite:[nist-SP-800-185] {sect}2.3, is the value `x`
   written as an unsigned big-endian integer on the smallest possible positive number `m`
   of bytes, followed by a single byte holding `m`.

//

State Machine::

* *States:* _Ready_, _Hash_Absorb_, _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, _Hash_Output_, _Hash_Output_Bits_ and _Success_, and, for `KMAC128` and
  `KMAC256` only, _Hash_Verify_ and _Failure_.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** As in <<KLE-SHA-3>>.
 ** For `KMAC128` and `KMAC256`: _Hash_Absorb_, or _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ with `last_block_len` = 0, ->
    _Hash_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** For `KMAC128` and `KMAC256`, _MachinePolicy_[0] gates MAC generation, and _MachinePolicy_[1] MAC verification (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** `KMACXOF128` and `KMACXOF256`, which serve as key derivation functions, gate no State
    (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>), and a non-zero _MachinePolicy_ is unsupported Metadata for them
    (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>); a `kl.derive` moves their output directly into a key (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>).

* *Behavior:* as in <<KLE-SHA-3>>, with the following differences.

 ** When entering _Ready_: `L` {leftarrow} 0.

 ** When leaving _Ready_ for _Hash_Absorb_, the `kl.setst` first:
  . XORs `cshake_block` into the rate and applies `P()`;
  . XORs `key_block` into the rate and applies `P()`.
+
NOTE: The key enters `state` only within that `kl.setst`, so that the Serialized Content of a CC in _Ready_ is the same
on every implementation and holds no key-dependent `state`.
In _Ready_, `state` is zero, so masking it would output `state_mask`.

 ** When entering _Hash_Output_ or _Hash_Verify_ from _Hash_Absorb_ or _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, the `kl.setst`:
+
--
. checks `Xs` and sets `L` as follows; any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_:
.. for `KMAC128` and `KMAC256`, the `kl.setst` into _Hash_Output_ is of Form B, and sets `L` {leftarrow} `Xs`, the output length in bits, with 32 {le} `Xs` < 2^32^, the lower bound being that of cite:[nist-SP-800-185] {sect}8.4.2;
.. for `KMACXOF128` and `KMACXOF256`, the `kl.setst` into _Hash_Output_ is of Form A or of Form B:
a non-zero `Xs`, a multiple of 8 below 2^32^, sets `L` {leftarrow} `Xs`, the output bound in bits;
a Form A `kl.setst`, or `Xs` = 0, sets `L` {leftarrow} 0, for an unbounded output;
.. for `KMAC128` and `KMAC256`, where _MachinePolicy_[1] is set, the `kl.setst` into _Hash_Verify_ is of Form B, and sets `L` {leftarrow} `Xs`, the tag length in bits, with `Xs` a multiple of 8 and 32 {le} `Xs` {le} `b`, the lower bound being that of cite:[nist-SP-800-185] {sect}8.4.2;
. then performs `finalize()`, which:
.. XORs `right_encode(L)`, or `right_encode(0)` for `KMACXOF128` and `KMACXOF256`, into `state` from bit `block_base` on, as `PROCESS_VLI` with `mode` = `xor_accumulate` absorbs data; the encoding directly follows the last bit of the message, even when `block_base` is not a multiple of 8;
.. then applies the padding of <<KLE-SHA-3>> with `D` = `bin(0, 2)`, the suffix `00` of cSHAKE (`pad_byte` = `0x04` when `block_base` is a multiple of 8), in place of the `bin(15, 4)` of SHAKE;
. sets `block_base` {leftarrow} 0 and `last_block_len` {leftarrow} 0.
--

 ** In State _Hash_Output_, `L` is the `out_len` of <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, whose output loop applies, with these properties:
+
--
* for `KMAC128` and `KMAC256`, `L` need not be a multiple of 8;
* with `L` {ne} 0 on entering _Hash_Output_, the output consists of {lceil}``L``/8{rceil} bytes, which may span several `t`-bit blocks, and its last byte holds the final `L` mod 8 bits, if any, in its least significant bits;
* with `L` = 0, `KMACXOF128` and `KMACXOF256` squeeze arbitrarily many bits and never transition to _Success_.
--

 ** In State _Hash_Verify_, the single Form B `kl.exec` invokes `VERIFY_TAG(state, L)` (<<KLE-op-verify-tag>>): it compares `INPUT[L-1:0]` with `state[L-1:0]`.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-Ascon]]
=== Ascon

(((KLE, Machine, Ascon)))Ascon is a public permutation cite:[DBLP-journals-joc-DobraunigEMS21] standardized by NIST cite:[nist-SP-800-232] together with various cryptographic algorithms designed around it.

[[KLE-Ascon-conventions,Ascon conventions]]Ascon conventions::
The Ascon Machines share:

* `state[0 .. 4]`: the five 64-bit words S~0~ to S~4~ of cite:[nist-SP-800-232], 320 bits, with `state[0]` in bits [63:0]. Each Machine states its rate, the low bits of `state`.
* `ASCON(p)`, which applies the permutation `Ascon-p[p]` of cite:[nist-SP-800-232], of `p` rounds, to the `state`.
* `pad(x,r)`, which appends the bit 1 to the bit string `x`, followed by the bit string `0^j^` where `j` = `(- |x| - 1) mod r`: `pad(x,r) = 0^j^ @ 1 @ x`.

[[KLE-Ascon-AEAD128]]
=== Ascon-AEAD128

Ascon-AEAD128 applies ``pad(``{nbsp}{interpunct}``, 128)`` to non-empty associated data and to the final plaintext or ciphertext block, as cite:[nist-SP-800-232] specifies.
The AEAD interface supplies the meaningful final length and makes the Machine apply this padding (<<KLE-unified-AEAD>>).
Empty AD has no padding or permutation; nonempty AD ending at a block boundary has an additional padded block.

Parameters::

* `b` = 128.
* `k` = 128.
* Input granularity: 1 bit (`glog` = 0; <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>).

Data Structures::

The Provisioning Input is the 128-bit `key` or a System Key Identifier. The private Content holds the five-word `state` and key; the unified interface holds the message counters and final-piece length (<<KLE-unified-AEAD>>). _AuxInfo_ fixes `tag_len_p` to a multiple of eight from 64 through 128 bits, or 128 bits for a System Key.

Machine-Specific Functions::
* `ASCON(p)` and `pad(x,r)` (<<KLE-Ascon-conventions>>).
* `absorb_rate(X)` XORs the 128-bit `X` into the rate: `state[0] <- state[0] xor X[63:0]`, then `state[1] <- state[1] xor X[127:64]`.
* `ASCON_TAG()` computes the 128-bit tag and returns `state[4] @ state[3]` after:
+
--
. `state[2] <- state[2] xor key[63:0]`
. `state[3] <- state[3] xor key[127:64]`
. `ASCON(12)`
. `state[3] <- state[3] xor key[63:0]`
. `state[4] <- state[4] xor key[127:64]`
--

State Machine::

Ascon-AEAD128 uses the non-prepass subset of <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>. [[KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-nonce-setup,Nonce setup of Ascon-AEAD128]]_Set_Aux_Value_ initializes `state` with the 128-bit nonce and key, then applies `ASCON(12)`. The AD and payload actions use `absorb_rate`; each full payload block applies `ASCON(8)`, and entering the payload phase applies domain separation. The final action pads the meaningful payload internally. Tag finalization uses `ASCON_TAG()` and the Machine's `tag_len_p`.

////

* *States:*
 ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Set the nonce, and process the AD.

 ** _Encrypt_: Encrypt the plaintext.
 ** _Enc_Last_Block_: Encrypt the last block of the plaintext.
 ** _Hash_Output_: Return the tag.

 ** _Decrypt_: Decrypt the ciphertext.
 ** _Dec_Last_Block_: Decrypt the last block of the ciphertext.
 ** _Hash_Verify_: Compare a user-provided value with the internally computed tag.

 ** _Success_: The Machine has completed its operation without errors.
 ** _Failure_: The Machine has completed its operation with an authentication failure.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_.
 ** _Encrypt_ -> { _Enc_Last_Block_ -> } _Hash_Output_ -> _Success_.
 ** _Decrypt_ -> { _Dec_Last_Block_ -> } _Hash_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** The Machine makes these transitions itself (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>):
  . a `kl.setst` into _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_, with `Xs` = 0 pads the empty final block and enters
    _Hash_Output_, resp. _Hash_Verify_, directly, as does the Form A bypass naming _Hash_Output_ from _Encrypt_, resp. _Hash_Verify_ from _Decrypt_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>> item 2);
  . the `kl.exec` of _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_, enters _Hash_Output_, resp. _Hash_Verify_;
  . the `kl.exec` of _Hash_Output_ enters _Success_;
  . the `kl.exec` of _Hash_Verify_ enters _Success_ or _Failure_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>).
 ** Gated States: those of Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>, with _Hash_Output_ under encryption and _Hash_Verify_ under decryption.

* *Behavior:*
** Upon entering State _Ready_:
   . `state <- zeros(320)`
   . `tag_len <- tag_len_p` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>)
   . `last_block_len <- 0`

** In State _Ready_, a Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `Xs` is an operation, not a State
   (Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>>): it leaves `tag_len` unchanged, and an `Xs` other than `tag_len_p` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>).
+
NOTE: A truncated tag is the leftmost `tag_len` bits of the 128-bit tag in the bit order of cite:[nist-SP-800-232] {sect}4.2.1, which are the `tag_len` least significant bits of `state[4] @ state[3]`.
Entering _Ready_ restores `tag_len` to `tag_len_p` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>), so every message of the locker uses the same length, and a verification oracle confirms the tag only after about 2^`tag_len_p`^ trials.
Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>> leaves the bound on failed verifications of cite:[nist-SP-800-232] {sect}4.3 R4–R5, and a single `tag_len` across lockers that hold the same key by value, to the caller.

** [[KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-nonce-setup,nonce setup of Ascon-AEAD128]]The `kl.setst` into _Hash_Absorb_ is of Form C. It takes the nonce from `INPUT` and performs:
+
--
  . `state[0] <- 0x00001000808c0001`
  . `state[1] <- key[63:0]`
  . `state[2] <- key[127:64]`
  . `state[3] <- INPUT[63:0]`
  . `state[4] <- INPUT[127:64]`
  . `ASCON(12)`
  . `state[3] <- state[3] xor key[63:0]`
  . `state[4] <- state[4] xor key[127:64]`
--
+
Only `INPUT[127:0]` is used (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
+
NOTE: The key enters `state` only within this `kl.setst` and within `ASCON_TAG()`, each of which completes with the key permuted or masked by permutation output, so no Serialized Content holds it (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>).

** In State _Hash_Absorb_, only (multi-block) ``kl.exec`` instructions of Form B are expected, absorbing a data block `INPUT` into the state as follows:
+
--
 . `absorb_rate(INPUT)`
 . `ASCON(8)`
--
+
If the associated data is empty, the caller absorbs no block, not even a padding block, and no permutation is applied.
No ``kl.exec`` is then needed in this State (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-transfer-window-checks>>).

** Upon entering State _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_, the following domain separation
   operation is performed:
 . `state[4] <- state[4] xor (bin(1,64) << 63)`

** In State _Encrypt_, (multi-block) ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected to be of Form A, encrypting `INPUT` into `OUTPUT` as follows:
+
--
 . `absorb_rate(INPUT)`
 . `OUTPUT <- state[1] @ state[0]`
 . `ASCON(8)`
--
** When entering State _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_, the Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_enc_last_block`, resp. `kl_state_dec_last_block`, and auxiliary operand `Xs` invokes
   `FINAL_BLOCK_ENTRY(0, 127, 1)` (<<KLE-op-final-block-entry>>): it admits any bit length `Xs` of the final block from 0 to 127 and sets `last_block_len <- Xs`, which, unlike item 4 of the macro, the `kl.exec` leaves unchanged.
   `Xs` need not be a multiple of 8 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>): cite:[nist-SP-800-232] orders the bits within
   a byte as <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>> does, so `pad` places the terminating `1` at bit position `Xs` for any `Xs`.
   If `Xs` = 0, the `kl.setst` pads the empty final block, `state[0] <- state[0] xor bin(1,64)` (this is `pad(∅,128)` =
   `zeros(127) @ 1`), and transitions the locker to _Hash_Output_, resp. _Hash_Verify_; otherwise, the State waits for its single Form A `kl.exec`, as described next.

** In State _Enc_Last_Block_, exactly one Form A `kl.exec` is expected, to encrypt the last block.
It performs:
+
--
* `local tmp : bits(128)`

[]
. `tmp <- pad(INPUT[last_block_len-1:0], 128)`
. `absorb_rate(tmp)`
. `OUTPUT <- trunc(state[1] @ state[0], last_block_len)`
.  The state machine then transitions to State _Hash_Output_.
--

** In State _Hash_Output_, only one ``kl.exec`` instruction of Form C is expected, computing the tag into `OUTPUT` as follows:
+
--
 . `OUTPUT <- trunc(ASCON_TAG(), tag_len)`
 . The state machine then transitions to State _Success_.
--
+
State _Hash_Output_ processes a single block (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>) and admits any `KLLEN` {ge} `tag_len`,
a multiple of 128 or not; a smaller `KLLEN` violates the granularity of the State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).
// Not a source for now (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>):
// As a `kl.derive` source (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>), _Hash_Output_ supplies {lceil}``tag_len``/8{rceil} bytes, the least
// significant bytes that the Form C `kl.exec` would write, and then transitions to _Success_.

** In State _Decrypt_, (multi-block) ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected to be of Form A, decrypting `INPUT` into `OUTPUT` as follows:
+
--
* `local tmp : bits(128)`

[]
. `tmp <- (state[1] xor INPUT[127:64]) @ (state[0] xor INPUT[63:0])`
. `state[0] <- INPUT[63:0]`
. `state[1] <- INPUT[127:64]`
. `ASCON(8)`
. `OUTPUT <- tmp`
--

** In State _Dec_Last_Block_, exactly one Form A `kl.exec` is expected, to decrypt the last block.
It performs:
+
--
* `local S_r : bits(128)` – the rate part of the state
* `local P : bits(128)`

[]
. `S_r <- state[1] @ state[0]`
. `P <- zeros(128-last_block_len) @ (S_r[last_block_len-1:0] xor INPUT[last_block_len-1:0])`
. `OUTPUT <- P`
. `absorb_rate(pad(P[last_block_len-1:0], 128))`
. The state machine then transitions to State _Hash_Verify_.
--

** In State _Hash_Verify_, which the decryption path enters by itself (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>), a single Form B `kl.exec` performs
   `VERIFY_TAG(ASCON_TAG())` (<<KLE-op-verify-tag>>): it compares `INPUT[tag_len-1:0]` with the computed tag in constant time, and transitions the locker to _Success_ if they match and to _Failure_ otherwise.

////

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-wsn]]
=== Ascon-AEAD128 with Preset Nonce

Like GCM with Preset IV (<<KLE-GCM-with-IV-mode>>), Ascon-AEAD128 has a variant with a preset nonce:
this Machine is `PRESET(Ascon-AEAD128, nonce)` (<<KLE-op-preset-value>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>).

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | 128 or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
| iii  | 128           | `nonce`
|  iv  | 0 or 64       | Padding
|===
--

* *Internal State*: {empty}
+
--
Same as for Ascon-AEAD128, with in addition:

 ** `nonce`: 128 bits.
--

* *Serialized Content*: {empty}
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)     | Field
|    i | 128 or 64       | `key` or System Key Identifier
|   ii | 64              | `state`[0]
|  iii | 64              | `state`[1]
|   iv | 64              | `state`[2]
|    v | 64              | `state`[3]
|   vi | 64              | `state`[4]
|  vii | 16              | `last_block_len`
| viii | 16              | `tag_len`
|   ix | 128             | `nonce`
|    x | 32, or 96 with a System Key Identifier | Padding
|===

`nonce` is serialized because every transition into _Hash_Absorb_, also after an import, loads it into `state`.
--

State Machine::

Ascon-AEAD128 with Preset Nonce uses the Ascon-AEAD128 subset of <<KLE-unified-AEAD>>, omitting _Set_Aux_Value_. On entry to _Hash_Absorb_, it initializes the state with its provisioned `nonce` as <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-nonce-setup>> specifies.

// NOTE: This Machine protects the confidentiality of the nonce, not against its reuse (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
// A key derived into the key field while the nonce is kept would pair the provisioned nonce with a key it was not
// provisioned with. If that nonce is a masked nonce `N xor K2`, which must stay secret (cite:[nist-SP-800-232] {sect}4.3,
// R2), this is a related-key setting, for which cite:[nist-SP-800-232] {sect}4.2.2 claims no security for nonce masking.
// Software could also test guesses of the nonce if another locker holds the same key with a nonce that software chooses,
// such as a locker of <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>> or one of this Machine provisioned with a guessed nonce.
// Software holds the same key in another locker when it can provision, by its SKID, a System Key that the SKS allows for
// that locker's Machine.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-N-masking]]
=== Ascon-AEAD128 with Nonce Masking

cite:[nist-SP-800-232] also describes a version of Ascon-AEAD128 with _Nonce
Masking_:

Data Structures::
* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | 128 or 64     | `K1` or System Key Identifier
| iii  | 128 or 0      | `K2`; absent when Pos. ii holds a System Key Identifier, which retrieves both `K1` and `K2` (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>)
|  iv  | 0, or 64 with a System Key Identifier | Padding
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
  ** `K1`: 128 bits.
 ** `K2`: 128 bits.
 ** `state`, `tag_len`, `tag_len_p` and `last_block_len`: as in <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, whose
    _Enc_Last_Block_ and _Dec_Last_Block_ states this Machine inherits.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|    i  | 128 or 64     | `K1` or System Key Identifier
|   ii  | 128 or 0      | `K2`, or empty (a single SKID in Pos. i retrieves both keys)
|  iii  | 64            | `state`[0]
|   iv  | 64            | `state`[1]
|    v  | 64            | `state`[2]
|   vi  | 64            | `state`[3]
|  vii  | 64            | `state`[4]
| viii  | 16            | `last_block_len`
|   ix  | 16            | `tag_len`
|    x  | 32, or 96 with a System Key Identifier | Padding
|===
--

The Machine has the Parameters, Machine-Specific Functions and unified-AEAD State subset of <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, with `K1` as
the key `key`. Its nonce setup uses `INPUT[127:0] xor K2` in place of `INPUT`.
`K2` enters `state` only in the nonce setup, inside the masked nonce, before `ASCON(12)`; `K1` enters it wherever `key` does in <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>.

Nonce masking with a set nonce needs no Machine of its own: the Machine of
<<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-wsn>> is provisioned with `K1` as the key `key` and
`N xor K2` as `nonce`, which software computes.
This combination needs `K2` outside the KLF. It cannot use a `K1` and `K2` that a System Key Identifier retrieves, since a System Key Identifier of <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-wsn>> retrieves `key` alone (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>).
All rules of <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-wsn>> apply to this combination.

// NOTE: `K2` stays secret only while no other locker holds `K1` as its key with a nonce that software chooses,
// such as a locker of <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, or one of <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-wsn>> that software provisions.
// Software queries this Machine with every nonce `x {vvert} 0^64^`, and such a locker with every nonce `0^64^ {vvert} y`,
// for 64-bit `x` and `y`, each time on the same associated data and the same plaintext of at least one full block.
// After about 2^65^ queries, the pair with equal ciphertexts and tags gives `K2` = `x {vvert} y`.
// Software holds `K1` in such a locker when it can provision, by its SKID, a System Key that the SKS allows for that
// locker's Machine. `K1` and `K2` are generated independently and serve no other purpose
// (cite:[nist-SP-800-232] {sect}4.3, R1).

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-Ascon-Hash256]]
=== Ascon-Hash256

Ascon-Hash256 cite:[nist-SP-800-232] instantiates <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>; only the differences are given here.
The Machine applies `pad(M, 64)` of cite:[nist-SP-800-232] {sect}5.1 when the message `M` ends.

Parameters::

* `b` = 64, `n` = 320, `t` = 64, `d` = 256, `state_offset` = 0, `tag_offset` = 0.
// Before byte-granular hash inputs:
// * Granularity: `b`. A `kl.derive` of a shared secret into _Hash_Absorb_ therefore admits a single `length`: that of the secret rounded up to a multiple of 8 bytes (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-checks>> items 5 and 6).

Data Structures::

The *Provisioning Input* contains only the MDH.

* *Internal State*:
 ** `state[0 .. 4]` (<<KLE-Ascon-conventions>>), with the rate `state[63:0]`.
 ** `block_base`, `last_block_len` and `out_len` (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<1,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|    i  | 64              | `state`[0]
|   ii  | 64              | `state`[1]
|  iii  | 64              | `state`[2]
|   iv  | 64              | `state`[3]
|    v  | 64              | `state`[4]
|   vi  | 16              | `block_base`, in bits
|  vii  | 16              | `last_block_len`, in bits; `read_len` in _Hash_Output_Bits_
| viii  | 32              | `out_len` in _Hash_Output_ and _Hash_Output_Bits_; in _Set_Aux_Value_ of <<KLE-Ascon-CXOF128>>, `Z_len` in bits [15:0] and `cumul_len` in bits [31:16]; zero otherwise
|   ix  | 0               | Padding
|===

This layout replaces that of <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>.
--

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `ASCON(p)` (<<KLE-Ascon-conventions>>).
* `process_block()` and `update()`: `ASCON(12)`.
* `finalize()`: `state[0] <- state[0] xor (bin(1,64) << block_base)`, then `ASCON(12)`.

State Machine::

* *States and Allowed State Transitions:* those of <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>.

* *Behavior:*
 ** Upon entering State _Ready_:
   . `state[0] <- 0x0000080100cc0002`
   . `state[1] <- zeros(64)`
   . `state[2] <- zeros(64)`
   . `state[3] <- zeros(64)`
   . `state[4] <- zeros(64)`
   . `ASCON(12)`
   . `block_base`, `last_block_len` and Position viii (`out_len`, or the `Z_len` and `cumul_len` of <<KLE-Ascon-CXOF128>>) are set to zero.

 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_, `PROCESS_VLI` is invoked as in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, with `block` = `state`,
    `mode` = `xor_accumulate` and `cumul_len` = `None`.
 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, the final piece need not be a whole number of bytes (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>, first bullet):
    cite:[nist-SP-800-232] orders the bits within a byte as <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>> does, as for <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>.
 ** _Hash_Output_ follows <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>. The digest has 256 bits, or `Xs` bits after a Form B
    `Xs` {ne} 0, and is emitted in any whole number of bytes, with `ASCON(12)` between successive 64-bit words and
    none after the last.

// Before byte-granular hash inputs:
// NOTE: A shared secret that `kl.derive` moves into _Hash_Absorb_ starts on a 64-bit block boundary, so it cannot follow a prefix whose length is not a multiple of 8 bytes, such as a 32-bit counter. A secret whose length is not a multiple of 8 bytes, such as the 66 bytes of secp521r1 or the 57 bytes of ed448, is followed by zero bytes up to the next boundary (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-derive-rule-both-fixed-size>>), and these bytes are part of the hashed message. The byte-granular Machines <<KLE-SHA-3>> and <<KLE-BLAKE2>> have neither limit.

NOTE: A keyed version of this algorithm is not needed.
A trusted entity can provide a CC that has already absorbed secret content, and reuse it for multiple invocations by keeping a copy of the original SCC.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-Ascon-XOF128]]
=== Ascon-XOF128

The Machine is the same as Ascon-Hash256 (<<KLE-Ascon-Hash256>>) with the following differences:

. The IV written to `state[0]` in State _Ready_ is `0x0000080000cc0003`.
. It is an XOF (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>): it has no `d`, and _Hash_Output_ emits output of unbounded length,
  or of `Xs` bits after a Form B `Xs` {ne} 0.
  The first output word follows the `ASCON(12)` of `finalize()`, the first squeezing permutation of
  cite:[nist-SP-800-232] Algorithm 6.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-Ascon-CXOF128]]
=== Ascon-CXOF128

Ascon-CXOF128 differs from Ascon-XOF128 in its IV, `0x0000080000cc0004`, and in the customization string `Z` of
cite:[nist-SP-800-232] {sect}5.3, which precedes the message as the blocks `Z~0~` = `bin(len_in_bits(Z), 64)` and
`pad(Z, 64)`, each 64-bit block followed by `ASCON(12)`.
The Machine absorbs them:

* A Form A `kl.setst` from _Ready_ into _Hash_Absorb_ selects the empty `Z`: it absorbs `Z~0~` = `zeros(64)` and
  `pad(Z, 64)` = `bin(1, 64)`.
* A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `Xs`, from _Ready_ or from _Set_Aux_Value_, enters State _Set_Aux_Value_ for a `Z` of `Xs` bits.
  The State is `LOAD_LONG_FIELD(Z_len, state, state, n, process_block(), None, xor_accumulate, 8)` (<<KLE-op-load-long-field>>): Form B `kl.exec` instructions load `Z` as a long field, with _W_ = `cumul_len`, in bits, absorbing it into the rate as it is loaded, and a same-State `kl.setst` restarts the load.
  With mode `xor_accumulate`, the macro does not zero `state`. The Machine adds:
+
--
. `Xs` must satisfy 0 {le} `Xs` {le} 2048; any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. Entering the State sets `state` to its value in _Ready_ and `Z_len` {leftarrow} `Xs`, and the `kl.setst` then absorbs `Z~0~` = `bin(Xs, 64)`.
. `Z_len` need not be a multiple of 8 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>), as for the message: the last transfer is truncated to `Z_len` − `cumul_len` bits (<<KLE-process-VLI>>).
. With `Z_len` = 0, the load is complete on entry, and any `kl.exec` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. With `Z_len` > 0 and the load complete, a `kl.exec` with an empty transfer window does not reach the Machine and the State is kept (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-transfer-window-checks>>); any other `kl.exec` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
--
* A Form A `kl.setst` from _Set_Aux_Value_ into _Hash_Absorb_:
+
--
. requires `cumul_len` = `Z_len`; otherwise, it transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_, performing no operation;
. applies `pad(Z, 64)`: `state[0] <- state[0] xor (bin(1,64) << block_base)` and `ASCON(12)`;
. sets `block_base`, `Z_len` and `cumul_len` {leftarrow} 0;
. then performs the operations of entering _Hash_Absorb_.
--

The States add _Set_Aux_Value_, which absorbs `Z`, and the Allowed State Transitions add _Ready_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Hash_Absorb_.
The Internal State adds `Z_len`, the bit length of `Z`, and `cumul_len`, the number of bits of `Z` absorbed so far, both serialized in Position viii (<<KLE-Ascon-Hash256>>).
In Ascon-Hash256 and Ascon-XOF128, a `kl.setst` naming _Set_Aux_Value_ transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_
(Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-setst-not-admitted>>).

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-accepted-risks]]
=== Accepted Risks

_This section is informative._

_MachinePolicy_ gates States (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>): a locker cannot enter a State of a direction that its policy disables.
The policy does not prevent the abuse of a mode of operation.
In the modes below, the enabled direction computes the disabled one under the IV or nonce of a message, which the caller sets or the PI fixes, since both directions use the same keystream or the same direction of the block cipher.
In CBC, decryption computes encryption only under an IV that the computation determines.
Preventing this would remove the one-direction policies of these Machines, or require the locker to generate every IV and nonce, so the KLE accepts these risks.
Privileged code that narrows _MachinePolicy_ to give a process a one-direction copy of a CC (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-restrict>>) obtains separate directions only in the other Machines, such as ECB and XEX/XTS, and in CBC only for an encrypt-only copy.

[float="center",align="center",width="100%",cols="<18%,^10%,<72%",options="header"]
|===
| Machines | _Machine_ +
_Policy_ | What the locker still computes
| CBC (<<KLE-chaining>>) | `0b10` | Encryption under an IV that the output determines: software picks the last ciphertext block, decrypts it alone under IV `zeros(b)`, XORs the plaintext block into the output to obtain the previous ciphertext block, and repeats; the last step yields the IV.
| CFB (<<KLE-chaining>>) | `0b01` | Decryption. For each segment, software computes the input block from the IV and the earlier ciphertext, sets it as `IV` by a same-State `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_, encrypts a zero segment, and XORs the output with the ciphertext segment.
| CFB (<<KLE-chaining>>) | `0b10` | Encryption: decrypting a zero segment outputs the keystream segment of the `IV` that software sets on entering _Decrypt_.
| OFB (<<KLE-chaining>>), CTR and XCTR (<<KLE-keystream-modes>>) | `0b01` or `0b10` | Both directions: either bit allows _Operate_, which computes both.
| GCM (<<KLE-GCM-mode>>) | `0b01` | Decryption without verification: encrypting a ciphertext under the IV of its message outputs the plaintext, since both directions XOR the same counter keystream.
| GCM (<<KLE-GCM-mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-with-IV-mode>>), GCM-SIV (<<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>>) | `0b10` | Ciphertext without a tag that verifies: decrypting a plaintext outputs its encryption under the IV, or in GCM-SIV the tag, of the decryption.
| OCB (<<KLE-OCB-mode>>) | `0b01` | The final partial block `P~*~` of a message: _Enc_Last_Block_ XORs its input with `Pad`, so the input `C~*~` outputs `P~*~`.
| OCB (<<KLE-OCB-mode>>, <<KLE-OCB-with-nonce-mode>>) | `0b10` | Ciphertexts that verify, as described below.
| Ascon-AEAD128 (<<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-N-masking>>) | `0b01` | Decryption without verification, at a cost linear in the length of the message, with a `kl.clone` of the locker before each block (quadratic without it). Under the nonce and associated data of the message, software encrypts the known prefix of the plaintext and then a zero block; the last output block XORed with the next ciphertext block gives the next plaintext block.
| Ascon-AEAD128 (<<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-wsn>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-N-masking>>) | `0b10` | Ciphertext without a tag that verifies, at a cost linear in the length of the message, with a `kl.clone` of the locker before each block (quadratic without it). Software decrypts the ciphertext computed so far and then a zero block; the last output block XORed with the next plaintext block gives the next ciphertext block.
|===

A locker of <<KLE-OCB-mode>> or <<KLE-OCB-with-nonce-mode>> with _MachinePolicy_ = `0b10` lets software forge.
_Decrypt_ outputs each plaintext block before _Hash_Verify_ checks the tag, and for a fixed nonce and block index `i`, the map from a ciphertext block to its plaintext block is a fixed permutation `f~i~`.
Given a valid message (`N`, `A`, `C`, `T`) of `m` full blocks and an optional final partial block, software decrypts chosen `m`-block ciphertexts under `N` and `A`, which no Machine prevents (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
It searches for `C'` {ne} `C` such that `f~1~(C'~1~) xor` {ellipsis} `xor f~m~(C'~m~)` equals the checksum of the valid message.
Since the tag depends on the ciphertext only through the checksum, (`N`, `A`, `C'`, `T`) verifies.
Wagner's generalized birthday algorithm (CRYPTO 2002) finds such a `C'` with about 2^23^ block decryptions for `m` {ge} 128, 2^45^ for `m` = 4 and 2^64^ for `m` = 2; for `m` = 1, no such `C'` exists.
If the final block holds `l` > 0 bits, software also chooses `C'~*~` freely, since the checksum depends on `P'~*~` only in `l` of its bits: the search then concerns 128 − `l` bits, about 2^128−`l`^ decryptions for `m` = 1 (2^8^ for a 15-byte final block), and fewer for larger `m`.

A repeated IV or nonce, which Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>> leaves to the caller, can reveal locker state derived from the key.
In <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, two messages encrypted under one key and one IV give, with their tags, a polynomial in `auth_key` whose roots software computes, leaving few candidates (the "forbidden attack" of Joux, 2006).
With `auth_key`, every valid message gives the tag mask of its IV, so software forges under that IV outside the locker.
Since the Machine also accepts IVs of lengths other than 96 bits, whose `J0` is a GHASH under `auth_key`, software can compute an IV whose `J0` is the `J0` of any chosen IV with its 32-bit counter decremented by one; encrypting or decrypting a zero block under it, in any locker of <<KLE-GCM-mode>> that holds the key, outputs the tag mask of the chosen IV.
One repeated IV thus allows forgeries under every IV.
Narrowing _MachinePolicy_, an _ExpirationDate_ and clearing the locker do not revoke them; only retiring the key, in every locker and SCC that holds it, ends them.
<<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>> limits the damage of a repeated nonce to revealing whether two messages are equal.

Two Rules limit the remaining risks:

* Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>> fixes, for the Machines that it lists, the number of bits that every verification of a locker compares, so such a locker that verifies but does not generate confirms a tag only after about 2^`tag_len_p`^ trials; for GCM the bound depends on the message length (<<KLE-GCM-mode>>).
* Rule <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-SR-unverified-plaintext>> forbids software to use the plaintext that an AEAD Machine outputs before the locker reaches _Success_, since that plaintext is unauthenticated until then.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-ECC]]
== Elliptic Curve Cryptography

(((KLE, Machine, Elliptic Curves)))The Elliptic Curve Machines provide:

. Scalar multiplication with known and unknown base point.
. ECDSA, SM2 and EdDSA signature generation and verification.

The x25519 and x448 Machines (<<KLE-X25519-X448>>) provide only the former.

NOTE: KDFs, hash functions and formats vary too much for general KEM or PKE support; among the elliptic curve standards, only SM2 fully specifies an integrated encryption scheme cite:[GMT-0003-4-2012].
Software builds such schemes on scalar multiplication, and `kl.derive` moves the shared secret into the hash function of the KDF, and the KDF output into the key of a symmetric Machine, without exposing them.
A key that is exposed anyway should be provisioned into a locker, and its exposed copy erased at once.

Provisioning sets the `Generator` field to the default base point (“generator”) that the standards listed below define for the curve.
Software may overwrite the field; a Form A `kl.setst` to _Set_Generator_, or one of Form B with zero in the GPR, restores
the default, as does a Form B `kl.setst` to _Ready_ with bit 0 of `Xs` set.

With _MachineExtension_ = 1, the _public-points variant_, `Generator` and `SecondPt` are public fields, which an SCC carries in clear (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-scc-clear-blocks>>): `Generator` always holds the default generator of the curve, so that _Point_Mul_ computes only a public key, and a scalar multiplication of `SecondPt`, which would make a shared secret, transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (_Point_Mul_ below).
In this variant and the sealed one, a Form B `kl.setst` to _Set_Generator_ with a non-zero operand, or to _Ready_ with bit 5 of `Xs` set while `HasSecondPt` is set, transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_, and a provisioning or import whose `Generator` is not the default completes into Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
The variant suits signature generation and verification, and key-pair generation; software uses _MachineExtension_ = 0 for a key exchange and for a custom generator.
It is defined for every Elliptic Curve Machine, and is unsupported Metadata where not implemented (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).

With _MachineExtension_ = 2, the _sealed variant_, `Generator` always holds the default generator, as in the public-points variant, but both points are private, _Point_Mul_ may multiply `SecondPt`, and the shared secret is a sealed source (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sealed>>) that no `kl.exec` emits (_Output_ below).
It suits a key exchange whose key derivation runs in lockers.
It is defined for every Elliptic Curve Machine except those of <<KLE-EdDSA>>, and is unsupported Metadata where not implemented.

All values (integers, coordinates, etc.) are in little-endian format, except the shared secret of the Weierstrass curves that _Output_ emits, which follows the byte order of its standard (see _Output_ below).

Parameters::
+
--
The parameters used in this section are:

 . The bit length `b` of the main fields, such as coordinates, signature R and S fields, scalars;
 . The bit length `h` of the hash used in the signature scheme. The `Hash` field holds:
 .. for the ECDSA curves, `bin(e, h)`, where `e` is the leftmost `min(N, outlen)` bits of the message digest, read as a big-endian integer, as cite:[nist-fips-186-5] {sect}6.4 and {sect}6.4.1, step 2, prescribe:
 ... `N` is the bit length of the group order `n`, and `outlen` that of the digest;
 ... the digest is truncated, not reduced modulo `n`;
 ... the caller performs this truncation and chooses an approved hash function;
 .. for SM2, `bin(e, 256)`, with `e` the big-endian integer of `SM3(Z~A~ {vvert} M)` of cite:[GMT-0003-2-2012], where
   `Z~A~` = `SM3(bswap(bin(entlen~A~, 16)) {vvert} ID~A~ {vvert} bswap(bin(a, 256))
   {vvert} bswap(bin(b, 256)) {vvert} bswap(bin(x~G~, 256)) {vvert} bswap(bin(y~G~, 256)) {vvert} bswap(bin(x~A~, 256))
   {vvert} bswap(bin(y~A~, 256)))`:
 ... `entlen~A~` is the bit length of the identity `ID~A~`, `a` and `b` are the curve coefficients, not the parameter `b`, and (`x~A~`, `y~A~`) is the public key;
 ... the caller computes `Z~A~` and `e`, since every input to `Z~A~` is public; the KLE unit computes neither;
 .. for the Edwards curves, the 64-byte `PH(M)`, used only in pre-hash mode; see <<KLE-EdDSA>>;
 . The bit length `j` of the random number used as nonce in signature generation;
 . The number `u` of coordinates used to represent a point (`u` is 1 or 2); and
 . The number `v` of elements used to represent a signature (`v` is 0, 1 or 2, where `v` = 0
   denotes a curve for which no signature scheme is provided).

The granularity of every Elliptic Curve Machine, X25519, X448 and EdDSA included, is 8 bits: a field can be transferred with any `SEW` from 8 to 64 bits that divides its size, or through the KLIOBUF in any number of bytes.

The parameters for the defined curves are:

* NIST curves cite:[NIST-SP-800-186,nist-fips-186-5]:
  ** secp256r1 (NIST P-256): `b` = `h` = `j` = 256, `u` = `v` = 2.
  ** secp384r1 (NIST P-384): `b` = `h` = `j` = 384, `u` = `v` = 2.
  ** secp521r1 (NIST P-521): `b` = 576 (55 msbs set to zero), `h` = `j` = 576 (55 msbs set to zero), `u` = `v` = 2.
* Edwards curves for signatures cite:[RFC8032,nist-fips-186-5]:
  ** ed25519:  `b` = 256, `h` = 512; `u` = 1, `v` = 2.
  ** ed448: `b` = 456, `h` = 512; `u` = 1, `v` = 2.
* Montgomery curves, for key exchange only cite:[RFC7748]:
  ** x25519 (curve25519): `b` = 256; `u` = 1, `v` = 0.
  ** x448 (curve448): `b` = 448; `u` = 1, `v` = 0.
* Brainpool cite:[RFC5639,RFC8734]:
  ** brainpoolP256r1: `b` = `h` = `j` = 256; `u` = `v` = 2.
  ** brainpoolP384r1: `b` = `h` = `j` = 384; `u` = `v` = 2.
  ** brainpoolP512r1: `b` = `h` = `j` = 512; `u` = `v` = 2.
* SM2 cite:[GMT-0003-1-2012,GMT-0003-2-2012,GMT-0003-3-2012,GMT-0003-4-2012]: `b` = `h` = `j` = 256; `u` = `v` = 2.

For the Edwards curves, a point is the compressed `b`-bit string of cite:[RFC8032] {sect}5 (`u` = 1), and a signature is the pair (`R`, `S`) of such strings (`v` = 2).
So `b` = 456 for ed448, whose strings are 57 bytes.
`h` = 512 because, in pre-hash mode, `Hash` holds the 64-byte `PH(M)`.
`j` = 0, since EdDSA uses no `RndNum` (<<KLE-EdDSA>>).

For the Montgomery curves, a point is its _u_-coordinate alone (cite:[RFC7748] {sect}5, `u` = 1).
Their Machines provide key exchange only (<<KLE-X25519-X448>>), so `v` = 0, and `h` and `j` are not defined.
They are listed apart from the Edwards curves because their points differ, and so does `b` for x448.

All curves here are defined over prime fields.
For secp521r1, the 55 most significant bits of `Scalar`, `Hash` and `RndNum`, of each coordinate of `Generator` and `SecondPt`, and of each component of `Signature` must be zero.
The locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ if a write sets one of them, or if _Point_Mul_, _Sign_Generate_ or _Sign_Verify_ finds one set in a value it uses, a resumed `RndNum` included (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).

A point is _valid_ if:

* each of its coordinates is less than `p`;
* it satisfies the curve equation;
* it is not the neutral element: the point at infinity of a Weierstrass curve, resp. (0, 1) of an Edwards curve;
* where the cofactor is not 1, it lies in the prime-order subgroup; and
* on an Edwards curve, it decodes as cite:[RFC8032] {sect}5.1.3, resp. {sect}5.2.3, prescribes.

_Point_Mul_, _Sign_Generate_ and _Sign_Verify_ check the validity of `Generator` and `SecondPt` when they read them (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
For the Edwards curves, only _Point_Mul_ uses `Generator`, and _Sign_Verify_ checks `SecondPt` only as <<KLE-EdDSA>> states.
An invalid point that an operation checks, and whose encoding satisfies the representation of the curve, is a data error and leads to _Failure_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>), a coordinate not less than `p` included.
A violated representation, such as a set msb of secp521r1, is a configuration error, as stated above.
For the Montgomery curves, the check of <<KLE-X25519-X448>> replaces this one.
--

//

Data Structures::
* *MDH*:
+
--
The Elliptic Curve Machines use the _StateExtension_ field as follows:

[[KLE-ECC-StateExtension]]
.StateExtension bits used by the Elliptic Curve Machines
[cols="^7%,<20%,<73%",width="100%",options="header"]
|===
|  Bit  | Name            | Description
|   0   | `HasSecondPt`   |  A second point has been set.
|   1   | `HasSignature`  |  A signature has been set.
|   2   | `HasHash`       |  A hash has been set.
|   3   | `HasRndNum`     |  A random number has been set. For the EdDSA Machines this bit is `HasHashState` instead (<<KLE-EdDSA>>).
|===

The Elliptic Curve Machines use the _MachineUse_ field as follows:

[[KLE-ECC-MachineUse]]
.MachineUse bits used by the Elliptic Curve Machines
[cols="^7%,<20%,<73%",width="100%",options="header"]
|===
|  Bits   | Name            | Description
|  [1:0]  | `OutputType`    |  Determines what _Output_ emits: 0, a _point_, the product of `Generator`, such as a public key; 1, a _signature_, from `Signature`; 2, a _shared secret_, the product of `SecondPt`, which _Point_Mul_ multiplies whenever `HasSecondPt` is set, whether the point was loaded or copied. Points and shared secrets are emitted from `SecondPt`. The value 3 is reserved: a `kl.exec` or `kl.derive` in _Output_ with that value transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
|  [13:2] | `Progress`      |   Tracks, in an implementation-defined and non-portable unit, the progress of long-running operations such as scalar multiplications. Whenever it is non-zero, the SCC must include an ADS (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-restart>>).
|===

NOTE: Except in the sealed variant, _Output_ explicitly allows the export of a shared secret through `kl.exec`, like any other output, so the caller can read it. Too many protocols process the ECDH shared secret themselves to list them as `kl.derive` destinations. Its `kl.derive` transfers are therefore unrestricted (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>>), and the exclusion of `K1 {vvert} K2` of nonce masking as a destination (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>) applies only to a secret that `kl.exec` cannot read, such as the ML-KEM `sharedkey` or a sealed shared secret.

For ECC Machines, _MachinePolicy_[0] gates signature generation and _MachinePolicy_[1] signature verification, with their Gated States (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>), and _MachinePolicy_ {ne} 0 gates _Set_Hash_, since its field `Hash` exists only then.
All four combinations are admissible, except as <<KLE-X25519-X448>> and <<KLE-EdDSA>> restrict them.
--

The *Provisioning Input* contains only the MDH.
A system-specific import (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-completion>>) checks every `Generator`, `Scalar`, `SecondPt`, `Hash` and `Signature` that it converts, with the checks and outcomes of a completed load of that field.
It sets `HasSecondPt`, `HasHash`, resp. `HasSignature` for a converted `SecondPt`, `Hash` or `Signature` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-sysfmt-standard>>).

* *Internal State*:
+
--
 ** `OutputType`: 2 bits, held in bits [1:0] of the MDH field _MachineUse_.
 ** `Progress`: integer, held in bits [13:2] of the MDH field _MachineUse_ (<<KLE-ECC-MachineUse>>): the
    implementation-defined progress of a long-running operation.
 ** `block_base`: 8 bits, held in bits [15:8] of the MDH field _AuxInfo_. The field _W_ of Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, in bytes, of the “set” and “output”
    operations; it is public, since the instructions that the transfer issues determine it. Provisioning and a system-specific import zero it, and every `kl.setst` zeroes it; an immediate assignment of _Set_Generator_ or _Set_Scalar_ then sets it to the field size.
    It is distinct from the counters `H.block_base` and `H'.block_base` of the hash instances of <<KLE-EdDSA>>.
 ** `Generator` (X, {Y}): one or two `b`-bit strings representing the X {and Y} coordinate{s}.
 ** `SecondPt` (X, {Y}): one or two `b`-bit strings representing the X {and Y} coordinate{s}.
    For signature verification and for multiplication by an unknown point, `SecondPt`
    holds the public key, resp. the peer's point.
 ** `Scalar`: `b`-bit string.
 ** If signature generation or verification is allowed:
  *** `Signature` (R,S): two `b`-bit strings representing the `R` and `S` components.
  *** `Hash`: an `h`-bit string representing the hash.
 ** If signature generation is allowed:
  *** `RndNum`: a `j`-bit string representing the random number.
 ** Additional data, for instance precomputed multiples of the Generator.

A `kl.rstrl` or `kl.rstrv` that leaves both bits of _MachinePolicy_ clear (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-restrict>>) erases
`Signature` and `Hash` and clears `HasSignature` and `HasHash`, since these fields then no longer exist.
One that clears _MachinePolicy_[0] likewise destroys `RndNum` and clears `HasRndNum`.
--

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
The public fields (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-scc-clear-blocks>>) come first: `Signature` and, with _MachineExtension_ = 1, `Generator` and `SecondPt`.
Positions i, i.a, v and vi are present only for the values of _MachinePolicy_ given:

[cols="^7%,<20%,<30%,<43%",width="100%",options="header"]
|===
|   Pos.  | Type                                         | Size (Bits)                               | Field
|     i   | Public                                       | If _MachinePolicy_ {ne} 0: `vb`, else: 0  | `Signature`, R value, followed by S value if `v=2`.
|    i.a  | Public                                       | If _MachinePolicy_ {ne} 0: `p1`, else: 0  | Padding
|    ii   | Public if _MachineExtension_ = 1, else Private | `ub`                                    | `Generator`, X coordinate, followed by Y coordinate if `u=2`.
|   iii   | Public if _MachineExtension_ = 1, else Private | `ub`                                    | `SecondPt`, X coordinate, followed by Y coordinate if `u=2`. Can be the Public Key.
|   iii.a | Public if _MachineExtension_ = 1, else Private | `p3`                                    | Padding
|    iv   | Private                                      | `b`                                       | `Scalar`: `b`-bit string. Can be the Private Key.
|     v   | Private                                      | If _MachinePolicy_ {ne} 0: `h`, else: 0   | `Hash`: an `h`-bit string representing the hash.
|    vi   | Private                                      | If _MachinePolicy_[0] = 1: `j`, else: 0   | `RndNum`. Its width is `j`, the nonce length, both here and in the Internal State.
|   vi.a  | Private                                      | `p6`                                      | Padding
|===

The padding `p1` = (−``vb``) mod 128 and `p3` = (−2 {times} ``ub``) mod 128 ends `Signature`, resp. `Generator` {vvert} `SecondPt`, at a 128-bit boundary; it is 112 for ed448 and 0 for the other curves.
The final Padding `p6` = (−(``b`` + (_MachinePolicy_ {ne} 0) {times} ``h`` + _MachinePolicy_[0] {times} ``j``)) mod 128 ends `Content1`.
The transfer counter `block_base` is not serialized: it is held in the MDH (_AuxInfo_[15:8]).
`SCC_ClearLen1` is (_MachinePolicy_ {ne} 0) {times} {lceil}``vb`` / 128{rceil} with _MachineExtension_ {ne} 1, and (_MachinePolicy_ {ne} 0) {times} {lceil}``vb`` / 128{rceil} + {lceil}2 {times} ``ub`` / 128{rceil} with _MachineExtension_ = 1.
`Signature` holds only a signature loaded for verification, or one that _Sign_Generate_ has completed, or its `R` as <<KLE-EdDSA>> states, or zero.

Which fields are serialized depends only on _MachinePolicy_, never on _State_ or _StateExtension_ (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-length-rule>>):
a field is serialized with whatever it holds, a partial load or an intermediate value included, and its `Has` flag governs only its use.

`RndNum` holds a value only while a signature generation is under way, with `HasRndNum` set, and is zero otherwise.
