A _StateExtension_ @ _State_ with `HasRndNum` set in a State other than _Sign_Generate_ is not supported by the Machine:
an SCC that carries it is invalid Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).

The size in bytes of the Positions other than ii, iv and vi.a is:

`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `varlen` <- (``ub`` + `p3`)/8 + (_MachinePolicy_ != 0) {times} (``vb`` + `p1` + ``h``)/8 + _MachinePolicy_[0] {times} ``j``/8.

If _AuxDataLen_ {ge} 2, the SCC also carries the ADS: `IMPQUAL` and `SIV2` precede `Content1`, and `Content2` follows it (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-SCC>>).

// [%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="<,^,^",options="header"]
// |===
// | Curve | Minimum | Maximum
// | secp256r1, brainpoolP256r1, SM2 |  800 | 2336
// | secp384r1, brainpoolP384r1      | 1184 | 3488
// | secp521r1                       | 1760 | 5216
// | brainpoolP512r1                 | 1568 | 4640
// | ed25519                         |  544 | 1824
// | ed448                           |  944 | 2824
// |===

For ed25519 and ed448 the fields of <<KLE-EdDSA>> follow Position vi.a.
--

//

State Machine:: {empty}
+
--
* *States:*
Besides
_Ready_ (1),
_Success_ (54),
_Failure_ (55),
ECC Machines define:
 ** _Set_Generator_ (2)
 ** _Set_Scalar_ (3)
 ** _Point_Mul_ (4)
 ** _Sign_Generate_ (5)
 ** _Sign_Verify_ (6)
 ** _Set_Hash_ (7)
 ** _Set_SecondPt_ (8)
 ** _Sign_Input_ (9)
 ** _Output_ (10)
--

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
+
--
The _Set_ states are _Set_Generator_, _Set_Scalar_, _Set_Hash_, _Set_SecondPt_ and _Sign_Input_.

 ** _Ready_ -> any _Set_ state.
 ** Any _Set_ state -> any _Set_ state, itself included.
 ** _Ready_ or any _Set_ state -> one of the following, with these entry conditions (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>):
  *** _Point_Mul_: `1` {le} `int(Scalar)` < `n`, where `n` is the order of the group;
  *** _Sign_Generate_: `HasHash` and `1` {le} `int(Scalar)` < `n`; for SM2, `HasHash` and `1` {le} `int(Scalar)` {le} `n` − 2
      (cite:[GMT-0003-2-2012]);
  *** _Sign_Verify_: `HasSecondPt`, `HasHash` and `HasSignature`.
  *** <<KLE-X25519-X448>> replaces the condition of _Point_Mul_ on `Scalar`, and <<KLE-EdDSA>> replaces the conditions of all three States.
 ** _Point_Mul_ or _Sign_Generate_ -> _Output_, made only by the Form D `kl.exec` of the State
    (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>), or -> _Failure_ when a point that the operation uses is not valid
    (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>).
 ** _Output_ -> _Success_, when the output is complete (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>), or on the first committed unit of a `kl.derive` of the shared secret.
 ** _Sign_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** A `kl.setst` into _Set_Generator_, _Set_Scalar_ or _Ready_ is of Form A or B, as the Behavior states, and one into
    _Set_Ctx_ or _Msg_Absorb_ of <<KLE-EdDSA>> of Form B (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-form-mismatch>>).

[none]
* _Set_Generator_ sets a group generator, or the default one; _Set_Scalar_ sets a scalar for scalar multiplication, or a
  private key.
* `HasSecondPt`, `HasSignature` and `HasHash` follow Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, and are held in _StateExtension_
  (<<KLE-ECC-StateExtension>>). `HasRndNum`, also held there, is set only while a signature operation holds `RndNum`,
  which is drawn, never loaded (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>>).
* `Generator` and `Scalar` have no flag. A load of either that is left incomplete zeroes the field
  (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>): a zero `Generator` is not a valid point, and a zero `Scalar` not a configured
  private key.
--

* *Behavior:*

 ** Provisioning sets `Generator` to the default of the curve, and `Scalar` to zero.

 ** The _Set_ states and _Output_ follow Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, with the progress recorded in `block_base`, in
    bytes.

 ** Entering _Set_Generator_:
  . by a Form B `kl.setst` whose operand is zero, Form A included, sets the default generator of the curve, and
    `block_base` {leftarrow} `ub`/8, which completes the load;
  . by a Form B `kl.setst` whose operand is non-zero, erases `Generator` and starts a load: Form B `kl.exec` instructions then
    load the `ub`/8 bytes of `Generator`; with _MachineExtension_ {ne} 0, it transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ instead.

 ** Entering _Set_Scalar_, _Set_Hash_, _Set_SecondPt_ or _Sign_Input_ by a Form A `kl.setst` starts a load of the field
    _X_ that the State sets by Form B `kl.exec` instructions (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>): it erases _X_, `Scalar`
    becoming zero, and clears ``Has``_X_ where that flag exists.

 ** A Form B `kl.setst` into _Set_Scalar_:
  . whose operand is zero, Form A included, acts as the Form A `kl.setst` above;
  . whose operand is non-zero sets `Scalar` to a random private key, and `block_base` {leftarrow} `b`/8, which completes the load;
    no `kl.exec` follows. Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-rbg-failure>> applies if the RBG fails. The key is:
  .. for the NIST and Brainpool curves, an integer _d_ with 1 {le} _d_ < `n`, generated as cite:[nist-fips-186-5]
     Appendix A.2.1 or A.2.2 prescribes;
  .. for SM2, one with 1 {le} _d_ {le} `n` − 2 (cite:[GMT-0003-1-2012]), from an RBG of security strength at least 128 bits;
  .. for the Edwards curves, a `b`-bit string obtained as cite:[nist-fips-186-5] Appendix A.2.3, step 1, prescribes, from an RBG of security strength at least 128 bits for ed25519 and 224 bits for ed448;
  .. for the Montgomery curves, a uniformly random `b`-bit string, from an RBG of security strength at least 128 bits for x25519 and 224 bits for x448.
+
The bits of `Scalar` beyond the key are zero.

 ** *Retry rules* of signature generation:
  . for the NIST and Brainpool curves, the signature is recomputed with a fresh `k` whenever `r` = 0 or `s` = 0
    (cite:[nist-fips-186-5] {sect}6.4.1);
  . for SM2, whenever `r` = 0, `r` + `k` = `n`, or `s` = 0 (cite:[GMT-0003-2-2012]);
  . the Edwards curves use no random secret, and have no retry rules.
  . each retry draws `k` as _Sign_Generate_ does and sets `RndNum` {leftarrow} `bin(k, j)` before it records any further progress.

 ** In State _Point_Mul_, a single Form D `kl.exec` performs a scalar multiplication:
  . The base point `P` is `SecondPt` if `HasSecondPt` is set, and `Generator` otherwise.
  . If `P` is `SecondPt` and _MachineExtension_ = 1, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_.
  . If `P` is not a valid point, the state machine transitions to _Failure_.
  . `SecondPt` {leftarrow} `[int(Scalar)]P`, encoded as in <<KLE-ECC>>, and `HasSecondPt` is set.
  . `OutputType` {leftarrow} _shared secret_ if `P` is `SecondPt`, and _point_ otherwise, and the locker transitions to
    _Output_.

 ** In State _Sign_Generate_, a single Form D `kl.exec` signs, over the base point `Generator`, with private key
    `d` = `int(Scalar)` and message value `e` = `int(Hash)`:
  . If `Generator` is not a valid point, the locker transitions to _Failure_.
  . If `Progress` is zero, the per-signature secret `k`, uniformly distributed in [1, `n` − 1], is generated from the RBG
    (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Prerequisites>>, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-rbg-failure>>) as
    cite:[nist-fips-186-5] Appendix A.3.1 or A.3.2 prescribes, also for SM2; `RndNum` {leftarrow} `bin(k, j)`, and
    `HasRndNum` is set.
  . If `Progress` is non-zero, the interrupted operation is resumed with the `RndNum` held, or restarted
    (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>>).
  . The signature (`r`, `s`) is computed as cite:[nist-fips-186-5] {sect}6.4.1 specifies for the NIST and Brainpool curves,
    and as cite:[GMT-0003-2-2012] specifies for SM2, with the retry rules above.
  . `Signature` {leftarrow} (`r`, `s`), and `HasSignature` is set; `RndNum` is destroyed and `HasRndNum` cleared.
  . `OutputType` {leftarrow} _signature_, and the locker transitions to _Output_.

 ** In State _Sign_Verify_, a single Form D `kl.exec` verifies `Signature` against the public key held in `SecondPt`
    and the message value `e` = `int(Hash)`, as cite:[nist-fips-186-5] {sect}6.4.2 specifies for the NIST and Brainpool
    curves, and as cite:[GMT-0003-2-2012] specifies for SM2:
  . it performs every check those specifications require, the ranges of `r` and `s` included, and checks that
    `SecondPt` and `Generator` are valid points;
  . if a check fails, or the signature equation does not hold, the state machine transitions to _Failure_, else to
    _Success_.

 ** In State _Output_, `OutputType` determines the output and its length:
     *** _point_: `SecondPt`, `ub` bits;
     *** _signature_: `Signature`, `vb` bits;
     *** _shared secret_: the byte string that the standard of the curve defines as the shared secret, in the byte order of
         that standard, which for the Weierstrass curves is the one exception to the little-endian encoding. It is:
      **** for the NIST and Brainpool curves, `Z` of cite:[NIST-SP-800-56Ar3] {sect}5.7.1.2: the X coordinate `x` as a
           big-endian string of `m` = {lceil}log~2~(`p`)/8{rceil} bytes (its Appendix C.2), that is
           `bswap(bin(x, 8m))`, 8``m`` bits (66 bytes for secp521r1);
      **** for SM2, `x~2~ {vvert} y~2~` of cite:[GMT-0003-4-2012], each coordinate a big-endian string of 32 bytes,
           that is `bswap(bin(y, 256)) @ bswap(bin(x, 256))`, `x~2~` in the 32 least significant bytes, emitted first, 512 bits;
      **** for the Montgomery curves, the whole `b`-bit string `K` of cite:[RFC7748] {sect}6, little-endian as that
           standard defines it;
      **** for the Edwards curves, whose standard defines no shared secret, the encoded point (cite:[RFC8032] {sect}5.1.2, resp. {sect}5.2.2).

 ** In State _Output_, Form C `kl.exec` instructions emit the output, ending in _Success_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>),
    in which no `kl.derive` is admitted.
  *** This State allows the export of the shared secret through `kl.exec`, except in the sealed variant, where a Form C
      `kl.exec` with `OutputType` = _shared secret_ transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
      The shared secret is also a `kl.derive` source, unrestricted, or sealed in the sealed variant (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sealed>>), as a field (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>>), of one
      `kl.derive`, and only while `block_base` = 0, before a Form C `kl.exec` has emitted any of it: that `kl.derive`
      transitions the locker to _Success_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-derive-rule-both-fixed-size>>), and one made
      once `block_base` {ne} 0 transitions it to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-checks>> item 2).
      The transfer reads it from its first byte (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-derive-rule-both-fixed-size>>): for
      X25519 and X448, a key field receives the first `eff_length` bytes of the shared secret; for the other curves, a
      key field is not a destination (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>); a message input receives the whole shared secret, with
      `length` equal to the length of the shared secret
      (Rules <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-checks>> item 6 and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>>).
// Before byte-granular hash inputs:
//    field receives the first `eff_length` bytes of the shared secret, and a message input `length` bytes, at least the
//    length of the shared secret and at most that length rounded up to the granularity of the destination, zero beyond
//    the end of the shared secret
// Not a source for now (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>):
//  *** A point or a signature is instead a `kl.exec` output source (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-exec-implies-unrestricted>>).

 ** Entering _Ready_ by a Form B `kl.setst`: bits 0 to 6 of `Xs` select the fields `Generator`, `Scalar`, `SecondPt`,
    `Hash` and `Signature` to copy, reset, erase or keep; a non-zero bit 7 or above transitions the locker to Error State
    _Invalid_.
  . First the copies are made, both from the values held before the instruction, so that bits 4 and 5 together exchange
    the two points:
  .. bit 4: `Generator` is copied onto `SecondPt`, and `HasSecondPt` is set;
  .. bit 5: if `HasSecondPt` was set, `SecondPt` is copied onto `Generator`, or, with _MachineExtension_ {ne} 0, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_; otherwise bit 5 has no effect.
  . Then the fields are reset or erased:
  .. bit 0: `Generator` is reset to the default value;
  .. bit 1: `SecondPt` is erased and `HasSecondPt` is cleared;
  .. bit 2: `Scalar` is erased;
  .. bit 3: `Hash` is erased and `HasHash` is cleared;
  .. bit 6: `Signature` is erased and `HasSignature` is cleared.
  . Every field whose bit is clear is kept. A Form A `kl.setst` sets no bit, and keeps all five fields.

 ** `Progress`, bits [13:2] of _MachineUse_, is the field _P_ of Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>> for _Point_Mul_,
    _Sign_Generate_ and _Sign_Verify_, and, in the EdDSA Machines (<<KLE-EdDSA>>), for a `kl.setst` that enters or leaves
    _Msg_Absorb_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-progress>>).
    Its material is `RndNum`, where the Machine uses it, and the intermediate values that the operation keeps in the ADS;
    for a `kl.setst` that enters or leaves _Msg_Absorb_, these include the expanded seed, the hash states being finalized,
    and `r`, `R`, `k'` and `A` before the operation completes.
    Each event of that Rule that zeroes `Progress` also destroys the material and, where the Machine has it, clears `HasRndNum`.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-X25519-X448]]
=== X25519- and X448-Specific Rules

(((KLE, Machine, X25519)))(((KLE, Machine, X448)))The x25519 and x448 Machines implement the functions `X25519` and `X448` of cite:[RFC7748] {sect}5, and with them
the Diffie-Hellman key exchange of cite:[RFC7748] {sect}6. Of the operations of <<KLE-ECC>> they provide only
scalar multiplication. They differ from the other curves in three respects: a point is a single coordinate,
the scalar is an arbitrary `b`-bit string, and the input point is not checked for validity, only the result. This subsection states the
consequences; everything not restated here follows <<KLE-ECC>>.

Parameters::

* The curves are the Montgomery curves `v^2^` = `u^3^` + `A` {interpunct} `u^2^` + `u` of cite:[RFC7748] {sect}4:
  curve25519 for x25519, with `p` = 2^255^ - 19 and `A` = 486662, and curve448 for x448, with
  `p` = 2^448^ - 2^224^ - 1 and `A` = 156326.
  curve25519 is birationally equivalent to the Edwards curve of ed25519; curve448 is related to that of ed448
  by an isogeny of degree 4. The Machines are nevertheless distinct, and points and private keys are not
  interchangeable between them.
* A point is represented in compressed form, by its _u_-coordinate alone, so the parameter `u` = 1.
  The _u_-coordinate is held as the X coordinate of `Generator` and `SecondPt` of <<KLE-ECC>>.
  A public key and a shared secret are 32 bytes for x25519 and 56 bytes for x448.
* There is no signature scheme: `v` = 0, and `h` and `j` are not defined.
* `X25519(k, u)` and `X448(k, u)` map a scalar and a _u_-coordinate, both `b`-bit strings, to a `b`-bit
  string, decoding their inputs as cite:[RFC7748] {sect}5 prescribes:
 ** the scalar is _clamped_: for x25519, bits 0, 1, 2 and 255 are cleared and bit 254 is set; for x448,
    bits 0 and 1 are cleared and bit 447 is set;
 ** for x25519, bit 255 of the _u_-coordinate is ignored; and
 ** a _u_-coordinate not smaller than `p` is reduced modulo `p`.
* The private key is the unclamped `b`-bit string held in `Scalar`, which holds a
  *configured private key* when it is non-zero. It is not compared with the order of the group.

Data Structures::
+
--
Those of <<KLE-ECC>>, with the following changes:

* *MDH*:
 ** _MachinePolicy_ is not used and must be zero; a non-zero value is unsupported Metadata
    (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>), and a `kl.rstrl` or `kl.rstrv` leaves it zero
    (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-restrict>>).
 ** Of the _StateExtension_ bits (<<KLE-ECC-StateExtension>>) only `HasSecondPt` is used: an SCC
    with any of the bits 1 to 3 set is invalid Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).
 ** `OutputType` (<<KLE-ECC-MachineUse>>) is never _signature_: a multiplication of `Generator` outputs a point, the public key, and one of `SecondPt` a shared secret, `K`; both are a single `b`-bit string.

* *Internal State*: `Generator` and `SecondPt` are each a single `b`-bit string, a _u_-coordinate.
  `SecondPt` holds the peer's public key and, after a scalar multiplication, the public key of the locker
  or the shared secret.
  `Signature`, `Hash` and `RndNum` do not exist.

* *Serialized Content*: Positions ii to iv and vi.a of <<KLE-ECC>>, with `u` = 1; Positions i, i.a, v and vi are always empty.
  `varlen` <- ``b``/8.
--

State Machine::

* *States:* besides _Ready_ (1), _Success_ (54) and _Failure_ (55), only the following States of <<KLE-ECC>>:
 ** _Set_Generator_ (2)
 ** _Set_Scalar_ (3)
 ** _Point_Mul_ (4)
 ** _Set_SecondPt_ (8)
 ** _Output_ (10)

+
_Sign_Generate_, _Sign_Verify_, _Set_Hash_ and _Sign_Input_ are not defined: a `kl.setst` that names one of them
transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-setst-not-admitted>>).

* *Allowed State Transitions:* the _Set_ states are _Set_Generator_, _Set_Scalar_ and _Set_SecondPt_.
 ** _Ready_ or any _Set_ state -> any _Set_ state, itself included, or -> _Point_Mul_, which requires `Scalar` to hold a
    configured private key (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Point_Mul_ -> _Output_, or -> _Failure_ when the result is the all-zero string
    (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>); _Output_ -> _Success_, as in <<KLE-ECC>>.

* *Behavior:*

** Provisioning sets `Generator` to the base point of cite:[RFC7748] {sect}4.1, resp. {sect}4.2, that is, to the
   _u_-coordinate 9 for x25519 and 5 for x448, and `Scalar` to zero.

** `Generator`, `SecondPt` and `Scalar` accept every `b`-bit string: no check is made when they are written
   (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).

** In State _Point_Mul_, a single Form D `kl.exec` performs a scalar
   multiplication, in the following steps, which replace those of <<KLE-ECC>>:

  . The base point `P` is `SecondPt` if `HasSecondPt` is set, and `Generator` otherwise. `P` is not checked for
    validity: `X25519` and `X448` are defined for every `b`-bit string.
  . `R` {leftarrow} `X25519(Scalar, P)`, resp. `X448(Scalar, P)`.
  . If `R` is the all-zero string, the locker transitions to _Failure_, and `SecondPt` and `HasSecondPt` are left
    unchanged. The check is made in constant time; cite:[RFC7748] {sect}6 makes it optional.
  . Otherwise `SecondPt` {leftarrow} `R`, `HasSecondPt` is set, `OutputType` {leftarrow} _shared secret_ if `P` is
    `SecondPt`, and _point_ otherwise, and the locker transitions to _Output_.

** Entering _Ready_ by a Form B `kl.setst`, bits 3 and 6 of `Xs` have no effect: `Hash` and `Signature` do not exist.

** Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>> applies to _Point_Mul_, with no `RndNum` to destroy.

[NOTE]
====
A key exchange is two scalar multiplications with the same `Scalar`: with `HasSecondPt` clear and the default
`Generator`, it yields the public key; after the peer's public key is loaded in _Set_SecondPt_, it yields the shared
secret `K`. cite:[RFC7748] {sect}6 uses `K` as the input of a key derivation function, together with the two public
keys; as a `kl.derive` source in _Output_ (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>), `K` reaches that function without leaving the unit.
A small-order `P` yields the all-zero `R`.
====

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-EdDSA]]
=== EdDSA-Specific Rules

(((KLE, Machine, EdDSA)))EdDSA, standardized in cite:[RFC8032] and cite:[nist-fips-186-5], does not fit the
division of labor used by the other curves of <<KLE-ECC>>, under which the caller hashes
the message and the KLE unit performs only the group arithmetic. Both of its hash
invocations take material the caller does not hold:

`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `r{nbsp}`  = `int(H(dom {vvert} prefix {vvert} M)) mod L`, where `prefix` is secret, and +
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `k'` = `int(H(dom {vvert} R {vvert} A {vvert} M)) mod L`, where `R` = `[r]B` is computed inside.

The Machine must perform the hashing itself. This subsection states the
consequences; everything not restated here follows <<KLE-ECC>>.

Parameters::

* `H` is `SHA-512` for ed25519 and `SHAKE256`, with a 114-byte output, for ed448.
* The private key, or *seed*, is the `b`-bit string held in `Scalar`; `Scalar` holds a
  *configured seed* when it is non-zero.
* `dom` is `dom2(x, ctx)` for ed25519 and `dom4(x, ctx)` for ed448, as defined in
  cite:[RFC8032] {sect}5.1 and {sect}5.2, with `x` = 0 in pure mode and 1 in pre-hash mode.
  For ed25519 in pure mode with an empty context, `dom` is the empty string.

Pure and pre-hash modes::
+
--
In *pure* mode the unit hashes the message itself; in *pre-hash* mode the caller supplies
`PH(M)`, 64 bytes for both curves, in the `Hash` field. Which modes an implementation
offers depends on the hash extensions it provides:

[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="<,<,<",options="header"]
|===
| Curve extension | Also implements | Modes available
| `Zkled25519c` | --           | Ed25519ph only
| `Zkled25519c` | `Zklesha2h`  | Ed25519, Ed25519ctx and Ed25519ph
| `Zkled448c`   | --           | Ed448ph only
| `Zkled448c`   | `Zklesha3h`  | Ed448 and Ed448ph
|===

The curve extension alone must provide enough of `H` to hash the fixed-size inputs above,
which are a few hundred bytes at most. Absorbing a message of arbitrary length requires
the general hashing machinery, and so requires `Zklesha2h` or `Zklesha3h`, respectively.

The _AuxInfo_ field of the MDH (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-MachineUse>>) is used as follows:
bit 0 records the modes that a CC may use: pre-hash mode only if it is 0, and pure mode as well if it is 1;
bits [2:1] hold `msg_pass` (see the State Machine below); bits [7:3] are reserved; and bits [15:8] hold `block_base` (<<KLE-ECC>>).
At provisioning and at import, two cases are unsupported Metadata and raise a KLE unsupported-Metadata exception (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>):
bit 0 = 1 on an implementation that does not offer pure mode for the curve, and a non-zero reserved bit.
This allows an M-mode handler to emulate pure mode where the hardware does not offer it
(<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-error-architecture>>); a handler that cannot resolve the exception, including for a reserved value,
transitions the locker to Error State _Unsupported_.
Provisioning keeps bit 0 of the PI, and a transition to _Ready_ leaves it unchanged.
Provisioning ignores and zeroes bits [2:1] of the PI, and entering State _Ready_ zeroes them.
--

Data Structures::
+
--
In addition to the fields of <<KLE-ECC>>:

* `ctx`: 255 bytes, and `ctxlen`: 8 bits, the context string of cite:[RFC8032]. It is
  optional for ed25519 and mandatory, though possibly empty, for ed448. Both are zero
  upon provisioning and upon entering State _Ready_, and are set in State _Set_Ctx_.
* `msg_pass`: 2 bits, held in bits [2:1] of _AuxInfo_. In State _Msg_Absorb_ it identifies the open pass: 0 for signing
  pass 1, 1 for signing pass 2, and 3 for the verification pass. In any other State it records the last completed pass:
  0 for none (also once _Sign_Generate_ has used the passes), 1 for signing pass 1, 2 for signing pass 2, and 3 for the verification pass.
* `k'`: `b` bits, `bin(int(H(dom {vvert} R {vvert} A {vvert} M)) mod L, b)`, the whole hash read as a little-endian integer and
  reduced modulo `L`, computed by signing pass 2 or by the verification pass, and zero otherwise; it is always below `L`. It is not secret: anyone holding `R`, `A` and `M` can compute it.
* `r`: `b` bits, the secret scalar produced by the first signing pass, reduced modulo the
  group order `L`.

There is no stored pre-hash mode selector: the mode is inferred from the path taken, as
described below.

`RndNum` is not used: the EdDSA nonce is deterministic by construction, being derived from `prefix` and the message.
Its absence is deliberate; `j` = 0, so Position vi of <<KLE-ECC>> is always empty.
Bit 3 of _StateExtension_, `HasRndNum` in the other ECC Machines, is instead `HasHashState` (<<KLE-ECC-StateExtension>>):
it is set on entering State _Msg_Absorb_ and cleared on leaving it, so that it is set exactly while a pass
is open, including across the same-State re-entry that ends one pass and opens the next.
A _StateExtension_ @ _State_ with `HasHashState` set in a State other than _Msg_Absorb_, or clear in _Msg_Absorb_, is not
supported by the Machine: an SCC that carries it is invalid Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>). It does not follow the pattern of
Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, since it records no loaded field.

The clamped scalar `s` and the 32-byte or 57-byte `prefix`, respectively, are derived from the seed
by hashing it with `H`, as cite:[RFC8032] {sect}5.1.5 and {sect}5.2.5 prescribe. An
implementation may recompute them on each use or cache them in the ADS.
The public key `A` is likewise derived from the seed, or cached in the ADS.

* *Serialized Content*: the serialized content of <<KLE-ECC>>, with the following fields
  appended.
+
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<1,<3,<5",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Type    | Size (Bits)  | Field
|  vii  | Private | 8            | `ctxlen`
| viii  | Private | 2040         | `ctx`
|   ix  | Private | `b`          | `r`
|    x  | Private | `b`          | `k'`
|   xi  | Private | `hs`                                | The state of `H`, zero while no pass is open
|  xii  | Private | If _MachinePolicy_[0] = 1: `hs`, else: 0 | The state of `H'`, zero except in signing pass 2
| xiii  | Private | 0 for ed25519, 112 for ed448 | Padding
|===

`hs` is the length of the Serialized Content of the hash Machine of `H`, without a key or MDH and with `cumul_len` present:
for ed25519 that of SHA-512 (<<KLE-SHA-2>>), 1664 bits, and for ed448 that of SHAKE256 (<<KLE-SHA-3>>), 1792 bits with its
padding to a multiple of 128 bits. Positions xi and xii hold the state of `H`, resp. `H'`, in that layout, with
`last_block_len` and `out_len` zero. The state of `H` is always
present, _MachinePolicy_ being never 0 for EdDSA, and that of `H'` whenever _MachinePolicy_[0] is set, so that the length of
`Content1` depends only on _MachinePolicy_ (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-length-rule>>).
A `kl.rstrl` or `kl.rstrv` that clears _MachinePolicy_[0] (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-restrict>>) destroys the state of `H'`, and leaves `HasHashState` unchanged.

`r` and `k'` are present unconditionally, and hold zero whenever no signing operation, resp. no signing or verification
operation, is in progress.

NOTE: The state of an open pass is architected: `msg_pass` identifies the pass, and `Content1` carries `r`, `k'` and the
hash states, so that an export and import, also to a different implementation, resumes the pass where it stopped.
Making the presence of `r` and `k'` conditional on `msg_pass` would only complicate the Machine; `r`, 256 bits for
ed25519 and 456 for ed448, adds little to an SCC of this size, and stays confidential in the encrypted part of `Content1`.
--

State Machine::

* *States:* those of <<KLE-ECC>>, and in addition
 ** _Msg_Absorb_ (11), in which message data is absorbed.
 ** _Set_Ctx_ (12), in which the context string is set.

* *Allowed State Transitions:* those of <<KLE-ECC>>, with the following changes:
 ** _Set_Ctx_ is a _Set_ state: the transitions of <<KLE-ECC>> into and out of the _Set_ states apply to it.
 ** _Msg_Absorb_:
  *** is entered, by the Form B `kl.setst` described below, from _Ready_, any _Set_ state, _Set_Ctx_ included, or _Msg_Absorb_
      itself; the Machine allows this same-State `kl.setst` (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>), which marks a pass boundary;
  *** is left only for _Msg_Absorb_, _Sign_Generate_, _Sign_Verify_, _Ready_ or an Error State; a `kl.setst` naming any
      other State transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
 ** If, in _Msg_Absorb_, a `kl.rstrl` or `kl.rstrv` (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-restrict>>) clears
    _MachinePolicy_[0] during signing pass 1 or 2, or _MachinePolicy_[1] during the verification pass
    (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-policy-narrowing-state>>):
  *** a `kl.setst` that ends a signing pass is admitted only if it names _Ready_, which abandons the pass; otherwise Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>> applies.
 ** _MachinePolicy_ = 0 is invalid for EdDSA (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>), at provisioning and as the
    result of a `kl.rstr*`.
 ** These entry conditions (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>) replace those of <<KLE-ECC>>, in addition to the gating by
    _MachinePolicy_:
  *** _Point_Mul_: `Scalar` holds a configured seed.
  *** _Sign_Generate_: `Scalar` holds a configured seed, and either `msg_pass` = 2 (pure mode) or `msg_pass` = 0 and
      `HasHash` (pre-hash mode).
  *** _Sign_Verify_: `HasSecondPt` and `HasSignature`, and either `msg_pass` = 3 (pure mode) or `msg_pass` = 0 and
      `HasHash` (pre-hash mode).

* *Behavior:*

** In State _Point_Mul_, the Behavior of <<KLE-ECC>> applies, except that the multiplier is the secret scalar `s`
   derived from the seed (cite:[RFC8032] {sect}5.1.5, resp. {sect}5.2.5, steps 1 to 3):
   `SecondPt` {leftarrow} `[s]P`.

** Signature generation and verification use the base point `B` of cite:[RFC8032] {sect}5.1, resp. {sect}5.2, whatever `Generator` holds:
+
--
. `R` {leftarrow} `[r]B`;
. the public key `A` derived from the seed is `[s]B`;
. verification checks its equation over `B`;
. the validity check of `Generator` (<<KLE-ECC>>) applies only to _Point_Mul_.
--

** In pure mode, signature generation takes two passes over the message, and verification one (_Msg_Absorb_ below); for these passes:
+
--
. entering _Ready_ clears `msg_pass`, `r` and `k'`;
. `r`, `k'` and `Signature`, which the passes compute, are not material of _Sign_Generate_ or _Sign_Verify_ under Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>>: a restarted operation uses them as held.
--

** Entering _Set_Ctx_:
  . A Form B `kl.setst` is used, whose `Xs` sets `ctxlen`; a value greater than 255 transitions the locker to Error State
    _Invalid_.
  . If `ctxlen` {ne} 0, Form B `kl.exec` instructions then load the `ctxlen` bytes of `ctx`, whose other bytes stay zero, following Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>> as the
    _Set_ states of <<KLE-ECC>> do, with the progress recorded in `block_base`; a load left incomplete zeroes `ctx` and
    `ctxlen` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>).

** Entering _Msg_Absorb_ (pure mode only) is done by a Form B `kl.setst` whose `Xs` selects the pass:
+
--
. The `kl.setst` requires bit 0 of _AuxInfo_ to be 1 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
. `Xs` is not the `max_len` of <<KLE-process-VLI>>, which is 0.
. Each pass has its own entry conditions (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>) and entry operations:
.. `Xs` = 0, *signing pass 1*:
... requires that signature generation be allowed and that `Scalar` hold a configured seed;
... sets `msg_pass` {leftarrow} 0 and `k'` {leftarrow} 0;
... initializes `H` with `dom {vvert} prefix`.
.. `Xs` = 1, *signing pass 2*:
... requires `msg_pass` = 1;
... initializes `H` with `dom {vvert} R {vvert} A`, with `R` from `Signature` and `A` derived from the seed;
... initializes a second instance `H'` with `dom {vvert} prefix`, as in pass 1; `H'` absorbs the same message bytes and recomputes `r`, which finalization compares with the `r` of pass 1.
.. `Xs` = 2, *verification pass*:
... requires that signature verification be allowed and that `HasSignature` and `HasSecondPt` hold;
... sets `msg_pass` {leftarrow} 3 and `k'` {leftarrow} 0;
... initializes `H` with `dom {vvert} R {vvert} A`, with `R` from `Signature` and `A` = `SecondPt`.
.. Any other value of `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
--
+
Initializing `H`, or `H'`, with a string `x` sets that instance as entering _Ready_ of the hash Machine of `H` does (<<KLE-SHA-2>> for ed25519, <<KLE-SHA-3>> for ed448), with all its counters zero, `last_block_len` and `out_len` included, and then absorbs `x` as a `kl.exec` in _Hash_Absorb_ of that Machine would, counting it in the `cumul_len` of that instance.
At pass finalization, the `finalize()` of <<KLE-SHA-2>> encodes for SHA-512 the length of the whole input of the instance, as cite:[nist-fips-180-4] {sect}5.1.2 requires.

** In State _Msg_Absorb_, Form B ``kl.exec`` instructions absorb message bytes into `H`, and in signing pass 2 also into `H'`:
+
--
. Each `kl.exec` invokes macro `PROCESS_VLI` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>) as +
  `{fournbsp}` `PROCESS_VLI(mode, 0, H.block, H.b, H.state, H.n, input_base, H.block_base, 0, H.cumul_len, 8, H.process_block(), None, None, None, None)`.
. In signing pass 2, it also invokes it with `H'` in place of `H`; the two invocations share `input_base` and run in lock step:
.. each byte is absorbed into `H` and into `H'` before the next one;
.. step 3.i of <<KLE-process-VLI>>, the interruption point, applies only to the invocation for `H`.
. In each invocation:
  *** `H.block`, `H.b`, `H.state`, `H.n`, `H.block_base` and `H.cumul_len` are the buffer, block size, state, state size,
      and partial-block and cumulative-length counters, in bits, of the hash instance `H`, and likewise for `H'`; for
      `SHAKE256`, `H.block` is `H.state` (<<KLE-SHA-3>>);
  *** `H.block_base` is distinct from the byte counter `block_base` of <<KLE-ECC>>, which _Msg_Absorb_ does not use;
  *** `H.process_block()` applies the compression function, resp. the permutation, of `H`;
  *** `H.cumul_len` is tracked, since the unit appends the padding, and for SHA-512 the length, at pass finalization;
  *** `mode` is `assign` for ed25519, whose `H` is `SHA-512` (<<KLE-SHA-2>>), and `xor_accumulate` for ed448, whose `H`
      is `SHAKE256`.
--
+
The interruption points of _Msg_Absorb_ are those of the invocation for `H` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>): 0 and, if `H.block_base` is 0 when the instruction is issued, every byte offset. In
signing pass 2, `H'` may then hold a partial block, which is architected (Position xii).

** Leaving _Msg_Absorb_ for _Msg_Absorb_ (the same-State re-entry above), _Sign_Generate_ or _Sign_Verify_ finalizes the
   current pass:
+
--
   . after pass `Xs` = 0:
   .. `r` {leftarrow} `bin(int(H(dom {vvert} prefix {vvert} M)) mod L, b)`,
   .. `Signature.R` {leftarrow} `[r]B`, and `Signature.S` {leftarrow} 0,
   .. `msg_pass` {leftarrow} 1, and
   .. `HasSignature` is cleared: `Signature.R` holds an intermediate value, and a signature loaded before is discarded; being part of the signature, `R` is public (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-scc-clear-blocks>>), and reveals neither `r` nor the key;
   . after pass `Xs` = 1:
   .. if `int(H'(dom {vvert} prefix {vvert} M)) mod L` {ne} `int(r)`, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_;
   .. otherwise `k'` {leftarrow} `bin(int(H(dom {vvert} R {vvert} A {vvert} M)) mod L, b)` and `msg_pass` {leftarrow} 2;
   . after pass `Xs` = 2: `k'` as above, and `msg_pass` remains 3.
--
+
Leaving it for _Ready_ abandons the pass without finalizing it: no hash is completed and no binding check is made.
A `kl.setst` that enters or leaves _Msg_Absorb_ is long-running; whether it is resumable is implementation-defined (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-preemption>>).

** In State _Sign_Generate_, a single Form D `kl.exec` completes the signature per cite:[RFC8032] {sect}5.1.6, resp. {sect}5.2.6:
+
--
. In *pure* mode (`msg_pass` = 2):
.. `R` is `Signature.R`, which signing pass 1 set;
.. `S` {leftarrow} `(r + k'` {times} `s) mod L`.
. In *pre-hash* mode (`msg_pass` = 0), with `Hash` holding `PH(M)`:
.. `r` {leftarrow} `int(H(dom {vvert} prefix {vvert} Hash)) mod L`;
.. `R` {leftarrow} `[r]B`;
.. `k'` {leftarrow} `int(H(dom {vvert} R {vvert} A {vvert} Hash)) mod L`;
.. `S` {leftarrow} `(r + k'` {times} `s) mod L`;
.. these `r`, `R` and `k'` are intermediate results of the operation (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-progress>>): the fields `r` and `k'` stay zero.
. In both modes:
.. `Signature` {leftarrow} (`R`, `S`), and `HasSignature` is set;
.. `r` and `k'` are destroyed, and `msg_pass` {leftarrow} 0;
.. `OutputType` {leftarrow} _signature_, and the locker transitions to _Output_.
--

** In State _Sign_Verify_, a single Form D `kl.exec` verifies `Signature` per cite:[RFC8032] {sect}5.1.7, resp.
   {sect}5.2.7:
  . in *pure* mode if `msg_pass` = 3, with the `k'` of the verification pass; in *pre-hash* mode if `msg_pass` = 0, with
    `k'` computed internally, `Hash` (holding `PH(M)`) standing in for the message;
  . the checks of step 1 of those sections are performed: `S` < `L`, and `R` and `A` decode; `A` need not lie in the
    subgroup of order `L`;
  . the equation checked is the cofactored one, [2^c^][S]B = [2^c^]R + [2^c^][k']A, with `c` = 3 for ed25519 and 2 for
    ed448 (cite:[nist-fips-186-5] {sect}7.7, step 3, and, in pre-hash mode, {sect}7.8.2, step 4);
  . if a check fails, or the equation does not hold, the locker transitions to _Failure_, else to _Success_; in either
    case `k'` is zeroed.

[NOTE]
====
* Pass 2 recomputes `r` from its own message and invalidates the locker on a mismatch: two signatures sharing `R` over different messages reveal the private scalar `s`.
* Since _Msg_Absorb_ is never left for a _Set_ state, `Scalar`, `ctx`, `Signature` and `SecondPt` stay fixed between the passes, and between the verification pass and _Sign_Verify_.
  This prevents two attacks:
** a different `R` with the same `r`: two signatures with the same `r` and different `k'` reveal `s`;
** a `Signature` substituted after the verification pass, to forge against the `k'` already computed.
* The verification pass records 3, open or completed:
** while open, 3 tells it from signing pass 1, so that the finalization, also after an export and import, is that of the open pass;
** once completed, 3 differs from the 2 of signing pass 2; otherwise _Sign_Generate_ would compute `S` from the public `k'` of the verification pass and the zero `r` that entering _Ready_ leaves, and reveal `s`.
* A completed signing pass 1 records 1, so that neither _Sign_Generate_ nor _Sign_Verify_ uses a `k'` never computed.
* In pre-hash mode `msg_pass` plays no role: `PH(M)` in the `Hash` field feeds both hashes.
====

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-PQC]]
== Post-Quantum Public-Key Cryptography

[[KLE-PQC-ML-KEM]]
=== ML-KEM

(((KLE, Machine, ML-KEM)))NIST announced the _Module-Lattice-Based Key-Encapsulation Mechanism Standard_ in August 2024 cite:[nist-fips-203].
The standard defines several procedures, including:

* `ML-KEM.KeyGen`: Generates an encapsulation key `encapsk` and a corresponding decapsulation key `decapsk`.
* `ML-KEM.Encaps`: Uses the encapsulation key `encapsk` to generate a shared secret key `sharedkey` and an associated ciphertext.
* `ML-KEM.Decaps`: Uses the decapsulation key `decapsk` to produce a shared secret key `sharedkey` from a ciphertext.

ML-KEM comes in three versions, ML-KEM-512, ML-KEM-768, and ML-KEM-1024, with increasing cryptographic strength.
They require RBGs of security strength at least 128, 192, and 256 bits, respectively (cite:[nist-fips-203]).
The sizes of keys and ciphertexts of the three subtypes of ML-KEM are summarized in <<KLE-ML-KEM-sizes>>.

[[KLE-ML-KEM-sizes]]
.Sizes (in bytes) of keys and ciphertexts of ML-KEM
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="^1,^1,^1,^1,^1",options="header"]
|===
| Subtype     | `encapsk` | `decapsk` | `ciphertext` | `sharedkey`
| ML-KEM-512  |       800 |      1632 |          768 |        32
| ML-KEM-768  |      1184 |      2400 |         1088 |        32
| ML-KEM-1024 |      1568 |      3168 |         1568 |        32
|===

The granularity of the ML-KEM Machines is 8 bits: a field can be transferred with any `SEW` from 8 to 64 bits that divides its size, or through the KLIOBUF in any number of bytes.

In State _sharedkey_Output_, one `kl.derive` (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-derive>>) moves the `sharedkey` that _Encapsulate_ or _Decapsulate_ produced into a second, already provisioned locker.
The destination is a key field of a symmetric Machine, or the `kl.exec` input of a hash function, MAC or XOF, that <<KLE-derive-endpoints>> lists as a destination (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>>).
With _MachineExtension_ = 1, the _sealed variant_, `sharedkey` is moreover a sealed source (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sealed>>); the variant is unsupported Metadata where not implemented (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).

Data Structures::

* The *Provisioning Input* contains only the MDH.

* _MachinePolicy_ is not used and must be zero; a non-zero value is unsupported Metadata
  (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>). No State is gated by it (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>), and a
  `kl.rstrl` or `kl.rstrv` leaves it zero (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-restrict>>).

* *Internal State*:
+
--
 ** `decapsk`: 13056 / 19200 / 25344 bits (1632 / 2400 / 3168 bytes), the string `dk_PKE {vvert} ek {vvert} H(ek) {vvert} z` of cite:[nist-fips-203] Algorithm 16,
 where `dk_PKE` has 384·`k` bytes, `ek` has 384·`k` + 32 bytes, and `H(ek)` and `z` have 32 bytes each (`k` = 2, 3, 4).
 ** `encapsk`: 6400 / 9472 / 12544 bits (800 / 1184 / 1568 bytes). It is not stored separately: it is the `ek` sub-field of `decapsk`,
 at byte offset 384·`k` (768 / 1152 / 1536). When only an encapsulation key is held, the other bytes of `decapsk` are zero.
 ** `HasEncapsk`, `HasDecapsk`: Booleans, recording whether `encapsk`, resp. a complete `decapsk`, holds a configured value.
 They are bits 0 and 1 of _StateExtension_, resp., and follow the pattern of Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, with the departures stated below.
 ** `HasCiphertext`: Boolean, bit 2 of _StateExtension_, recording whether `ciphertext` holds a complete value, loaded and
 checked, or computed by _Encapsulate_. It follows the pattern of Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>.
 ** `HasSharedKey`: Boolean, bit 3 of _StateExtension_, recording whether `sharedkey` holds the key that _Encapsulate_ or
 _Decapsulate_ produced, whatever its value, zero included. It is set when that operation completes, and cleared whenever
 `sharedkey` is zeroed. A _StateExtension_ @ _State_ with _sharedkey_Output_ and `HasSharedKey` clear is not supported
 by the Machine: an SCC that carries it is invalid Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).
 ** `ciphertext`: 6144 / 8704 / 12544 bits (768 / 1088 / 1568 bytes).
 ** `sharedkey`: 256 bits.

`encapsk`, `decapsk`, resp. `sharedkey` correspond to `ek`, `dk`, resp. `K` in cite:[nist-fips-203].

In the States that load a long field (_encapsk_Input_, _decapsk_Input_ and _ciphertext_Input_) or emit one (_encapsk_Output_ and _ciphertext_Output_), _MachineUse_ records the number of bytes transferred so far.
It is the transfer counter _W_ of Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, which the ECC Machines keep in `block_base` instead (<<KLE-ECC>>).
--

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<1,<3,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Type | Size (Bits) +
for ML-KEM-512/768/1024 | Field
|    i  | Public  | 6400 / 9472 / 12544   | The `ek` sub-field of `decapsk`, that is `encapsk`.
|   ii  | Public  | 256                   | The `H(ek)` sub-field of `decapsk`.
|  iii  | Public  | 6144 / 8704 / 12544   | `ciphertext`
| iii.a | Public  | 0 / 0 / 0             | Padding
|   iv  | Private | 6144 / 9216 / 12288   | The `dk_PKE` sub-field of `decapsk`.
|  iv.a | Private | 0 / 0 / 0             | Padding
|    v  | Private | 256                   | The `z` sub-field of `decapsk`.
|   vi  | Private | 256                   | `sharedkey`
|  vi.a | Private | 0 / 0 / 0             | Padding
|===

Positions i to iii.a are the public fields and their padding (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-scc-clear-blocks>>), and `SCC_ClearLen1` is 100, 144 and 198 for ML-KEM-512, ML-KEM-768 and ML-KEM-1024.
With only `encapsk` held, Positions ii, iv and v are zero.
The `ek` and `H(ek)` sub-fields hold only a value loaded or computed for them, a partial load included, or zero, and `ciphertext` only a ciphertext loaded or computed by _Encapsulate_: _Decapsulate_ never writes the re-encryption of cite:[nist-fips-203] Algorithm 18 into a field.

These contexts are large: for ML-KEM-512, ML-KEM-768 and ML-KEM-1024 the serialized content is
2432, 3520 and 4768 bytes, that is 152, 220 and 298 blocks of 128 bits; `encapsk` takes no
space of its own, since it is held in the `ek` sub-field of `decapsk`, which the Serialized Content splits into its sub-fields.
--

Machine-Specific Functions::
+
--
The procedures implemented by ML-KEM are taken directly from cite:[nist-fips-203]:

* `ML-KEM.KeyGen()` (cite:[nist-fips-203] Algorithm 19) draws the random seeds `d`, `z` and computes the encapsulation key `encapsk` and the decapsulation key `decapsk` as (`encapsk`, `decapsk`) = `ML-KEM.KeyGen_internal(d, z)` (Algorithm 16).
* `ML-KEM.Encaps(encapsk)` (cite:[nist-fips-203] Algorithm 20) draws the randomness `m` and computes the shared key `sharedkey` and the ciphertext `ciphertext` as (`sharedkey`, `ciphertext`) = `ML-KEM.Encaps_internal(encapsk, m)` (Algorithm 17).
* `ML-KEM.Decaps(decapsk, ciphertext)` computes the shared key `sharedkey` from the decapsulation key `decapsk` and the ciphertext `ciphertext` (cite:[nist-fips-203] Algorithm 21).

The loop of `SampleNTT` (cite:[nist-fips-203] Algorithm 7) iterates an indeterminate number of times (cite:[nist-fips-203] Appendix B).
An implementation bounds it by an implementation-defined limit of at least 280 iterations (cite:[nist-fips-203] Appendix B, Table 4), which a correct execution reaches with a probability of at most 2^-261^.
As in cite:[nist-fips-203], the execution time need not be independent of the number of iterations (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-SCProtection-field>>).
When the limit is reached, the operation ends without output: it destroys its intermediate values and any value drawn for it, and zeroes _MachineUse_.
The state machine then transitions to _Failure_.

NOTE: cite:[nist-fips-203] Appendix B recommends not bounding the loop; KLE requires a bound, as for <<KLE-PQC-ML-DSA>>, so that every operation ends in bounded time.
--

State Machine::
+
--
* *States:*
Besides
_Ready_ (1) and
_Failure_ (55),
ML-KEM Machines define the following States:
 ** _GenerateKeyPair_ (2)
 ** _Encapsulate_ (3)
 ** _Decapsulate_ (4)
 ** _encapsk_Input_ (5)
 ** _decapsk_Input_ (6)
 ** _encapsk_Output_ (7)
 ** _ciphertext_Input_ (8)
 ** _ciphertext_Output_ (9)
 ** _sharedkey_Output_ (10)
--

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** By `kl.setst`, under the entry conditions given below:
  . from _Ready_ or any other State listed above except _Failure_, to another of _Ready_, _GenerateKeyPair_,
    _Encapsulate_, _Decapsulate_, _encapsk_Input_, _decapsk_Input_, _ciphertext_Input_, _encapsk_Output_ or
    _ciphertext_Output_;
  . from _Failure_, only as Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>> allows;
  . in _encapsk_Input_, _decapsk_Input_ and _ciphertext_Input_, to the same State, which restarts the load
    (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>).
 ** On completion of its `kl.exec`: _GenerateKeyPair_ -> _encapsk_Output_; _Encapsulate_ -> _ciphertext_Output_; _Decapsulate_ -> _sharedkey_Output_.
 ** When a `SampleNTT` limit is reached (see above): _GenerateKeyPair_, _Encapsulate_ or _Decapsulate_ -> _Failure_.
 ** Once its field has been completely emitted: _encapsk_Output_ -> _Ready_; _ciphertext_Output_ -> _sharedkey_Output_ if `HasSharedKey` is set, as after _Encapsulate_, and -> _Ready_ otherwise.
 ** On the first committed unit of a `kl.derive` of `sharedkey`: _sharedkey_Output_ -> _Ready_.
 ** Once its field has been completely loaded: _ciphertext_Input_ -> _Ready_, or -> _Failure_ if the ciphertext type check fails;
    _encapsk_Input_ -> _Failure_ if the encapsulation key check fails.
 ** When an import restores an `encapsk` that `HasEncapsk` marks and that fails the encapsulation key check: -> _Failure_.
 ** _sharedkey_Output_ is entered only by these transitions (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>).
 ** ML-KEM Machines never enter _Success_.

* *Behavior:*
+
--
** Provisioning:
  . clears `decapsk` (and with it `encapsk`), `ciphertext` and `sharedkey`;
  . clears `HasEncapsk`, `HasDecapsk`, `HasCiphertext` and `HasSharedKey`;
  . zeroes _MachineUse_.

** Entering _Ready_:
  . zeroes `sharedkey` and _MachineUse_;
  . keeps `decapsk` (and with it `encapsk`), `ciphertext`, `HasEncapsk`, `HasDecapsk` and `HasCiphertext`.

** Leaving:
  . _sharedkey_Output_ zeroes `sharedkey`;
  . _ciphertext_Output_ for a State other than _sharedkey_Output_ also zeroes `sharedkey`.

** Entering:
  . _encapsk_Input_ zeroes the whole `decapsk` field and clears `HasEncapsk` and `HasDecapsk`; the load writes the `encapsk`
    sub-field, so that the locker then holds an encapsulation key only;
  . _decapsk_Input_ zeroes the whole `decapsk` field, with its `encapsk` sub-field, and clears `HasEncapsk` and `HasDecapsk`;
  . _ciphertext_Input_ zeroes `ciphertext` and `sharedkey`, and clears `HasCiphertext`;
  . _Encapsulate_ or _Decapsulate_ zeroes `sharedkey`.

** No `kl.setst` of this Machine takes an auxiliary parameter.

** The input checks of cite:[nist-fips-203] {sect}7.2 and {sect}7.3 are performed when a field is written
   (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>):
  . a field is written when its load completes, and when an import restores a key that `HasEncapsk` or `HasDecapsk`
    marks as configured;
  . a system-specific import (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-completion>>) checks a converted `decapsk`,
    `encapsk` or `ciphertext` as a completed load of that field does, with the same outcomes, and sets `HasEncapsk`,
    `HasDecapsk` and `HasCiphertext` as that load sets them (Rule <<KLE-MGR-sysfmt-standard>>);
  . a `kl.setst` that leaves _encapsk_Input_, _decapsk_Input_ or _ciphertext_Input_ before the field is complete zeroes
    the field (the whole `decapsk` for _decapsk_Input_; Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>): `HasEncapsk` and `HasDecapsk`, resp. `HasCiphertext`, stay clear;
  . departing from Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, excess data in the final loading transfer of `encapsk`, `decapsk` or
    `ciphertext` fails the type check;
  . the checks and their outcomes are, for each field:
  .. `encapsk`: the *encapsulation key check* of cite:[nist-fips-203] {sect}7.2:
  ... it is the type check (`encapsk` has the length required by the parameter set) and the modulus check (`encapsk`
      re-encodes to itself, that is, every coefficient is reduced modulo `q`);
  ... a failure is a *data* error (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>) when the `encapsk` was loaded in _encapsk_Input_,
      or imported with `HasEncapsk` set: the whole `decapsk` field is zeroed, `HasEncapsk` and `HasDecapsk` are
      cleared, and the state machine transitions to _Failure_.
  .. `decapsk`: the *decapsulation key check* of cite:[nist-fips-203] {sect}7.3:
  ... it is the type check and the hash check (the copy of `H(encapsk)` embedded in `decapsk` matches the embedded
      `encapsk`);
  ... a failure is a *configuration* error: the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_;
  ... when the load completes, the encapsulation key check is also performed on the embedded `encapsk`;
  ... a completed load that passes the decapsulation key check sets `HasDecapsk`, and also `HasEncapsk` if the embedded
      `encapsk` passes the encapsulation key check; a failure of that check only leaves `HasEncapsk` clear.
  .. `ciphertext`: when the load completes, the *ciphertext type check* of cite:[nist-fips-203] {sect}7.3:
  ... a `ciphertext` loaded without excess data passes it: `HasCiphertext` is set, and the state machine transitions
      to _Ready_;
  ... a failure is a *data* error: `ciphertext` is zeroed, `HasCiphertext` stays clear, and the state machine
      transitions to _Failure_.

+
NOTE: These checks do not affect the implicit rejection of `ML-KEM.Decaps`: a ciphertext that passes the type check but
does not decapsulate correctly still yields the `z`-derived shared key, and the state machine still transitions to
_sharedkey_Output_.

** In States _GenerateKeyPair_, _Encapsulate_ and _Decapsulate_ the operation is long-running:
  . _MachineUse_ is the field _P_ of Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>>, and, when it is non-zero, the interrupted
    operation is resumed or restarted as per that Rule;
  . its random values and intermediate values are kept in the ADS
    (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-progress>>);
  . it destroys each intermediate value as soon as it is no longer needed;
  . before completion, it destroys its remaining random values and intermediate computation data, except its
    designated outputs, such as `z` in `decapsk`, and the retention exceptions of cite:[nist-fips-203] {sect}3.3:
    the key-generation seed `(d,z)` and the public matrix Â;
  . this destruction governs operation material; pre-existing architected input fields remain as the Machine
    specifies;
  . internal representations of stored `encapsk` or `decapsk`, such as decoded values or masked shares, may remain
    in the ADS (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Auxiliary-Data-Section>>); an additional computation result does not become
    a stored key representation merely because it is computed from a key alone;
  . no other completed-operation intermediate may remain in the ADS; retained secret key representations and the
    seed receive the key's protection and are destroyed when the corresponding key is replaced or erased.

** In State _GenerateKeyPair_, a Form D `kl.exec` generates a new key pair with `ML-KEM.KeyGen` of
   cite:[nist-fips-203] (Algorithm 19):
  . besides a `SampleNTT` limit (see above), only the RBG can make it fail: Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-rbg-failure>>
    applies if the RBG fails;
  . otherwise `decapsk` (and with it `encapsk`) receives the new key pair, `HasEncapsk` and `HasDecapsk` are set, and
    the state machine transitions to _encapsk_Output_, to emit the public key;
  . leaving _encapsk_Output_ keeps the key pair, whatever State follows; entering _encapsk_Input_ or
    _decapsk_Input_ discards it (see above).

** Entering _Encapsulate_ requires `HasEncapsk` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>). In it, a Form D `kl.exec` applies
   `ML-KEM.Encaps` of cite:[nist-fips-203] (Algorithm 20) to `encapsk`:
  . it draws one random value, from which it derives both the shared key and the ciphertext;
  . besides a `SampleNTT` limit, only the RBG can make it fail: Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-rbg-failure>> applies
    if the RBG fails;
  . otherwise it stores the two values in `sharedkey` and `ciphertext`, `HasSharedKey` and `HasCiphertext` are set, and
    the state machine transitions to _ciphertext_Output_.

** Entering _Decapsulate_ requires `HasDecapsk` and `HasCiphertext` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>). In it, a Form D
   `kl.exec` applies `ML-KEM.Decaps` of cite:[nist-fips-203] (Algorithm 21):
  . it runs for any ciphertext, and fails only on a `SampleNTT` limit;
  . it places in `sharedkey` the shared key derived from the ciphertext or, by implicit rejection, the one derived from
    `z`, sets `HasSharedKey`, and the state machine transitions to _sharedkey_Output_;
  . nothing but the value of `sharedkey` may depend on which of the two keys it placed:
  .. not the execution time;
  .. not any field of the locker, _MachineUse_ included;
  .. not the _AuxDataLen_ of an SCC exported while the operation is under way;
  . the _SCProtection_ level of the locker (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-SCProtection-field>>) governs the other physical
    leakage of the operation.
+
NOTE: A side channel of the decryption or of the re-encryption within _Decapsulate_, such as one that shows whether the
decrypted message equals a value the caller chose, is a plaintext-checking oracle. With a few thousand chosen
ciphertexts, it lets a caller recover `dk_PKE`, and with it the use of `decapsk`. The _SCProtection_ level of the
locker sets how far _Decapsulate_ resists it. The value of `sharedkey` itself, which a caller
can test through `kl.derive`, is no such oracle: cite:[nist-fips-203] `ML-KEM.Decaps` returns it to its caller, and
ML-KEM is IND-CCA2 secure under that interface.

** In States _encapsk_Input_, _decapsk_Input_ and _ciphertext_Input_, Form B `kl.exec` instructions load `encapsk`,
   `decapsk` and `ciphertext`:
  . following Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, with the progress recorded in _MachineUse_;
  . the parameter set and the State fix the size of each field (<<KLE-ML-KEM-sizes>>).

** In States _encapsk_Output_ and _ciphertext_Output_:
  . Form C `kl.exec` instructions emit `encapsk` and `ciphertext`, following Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, with the
    progress recorded in _MachineUse_;
  . entering _encapsk_Output_ requires `HasEncapsk`, and entering _ciphertext_Output_ requires `HasCiphertext`
    (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).

** No `kl.exec` emits `sharedkey`:
  . besides the instructions that move or copy the whole CC, an export and import, `kl.clone`, `kl.rename` and
    `kl.swap`, only `kl.derive` (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-derive>>) moves it, as a field source
    (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>>), and only in State _sharedkey_Output_ (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>);
  . that State admits no `kl.exec`, and one `kl.derive`, which transitions the locker to _Ready_
    (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-derive-rule-both-fixed-size>>);
  . the `kl.derive` reads the field from its first byte: a key field receives its first `eff_length` bytes, and a
    message input the whole field, with `length` = 32 (Rules <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-derive-rule-both-fixed-size>>,
    <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-checks>> item 6 and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>>).
// Before byte-granular hash inputs:
//  whole field, with `length` = 32, since the granularity of every listed destination divides 32 bytes (Rules ...).
--

//

[[KLE-PQC-ML-DSA]]
=== ML-DSA

(((KLE, Machine, ML-DSA)))NIST announced the _Module-Lattice-Based Digital Signature Standard_ in August 2024 cite:[nist-fips-204]; it has three variants: ML-DSA-44, ML-DSA-65, and ML-DSA-87.

[[KLE-ML-DSA-sizes]]
.Sizes (in bytes) of keys and signatures of ML-DSA
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="^1,^1,^1,^1",options="header"]
|===
| Subtype   | Private Key | Public Key | Signature Size
| ML-DSA-44 |     2560    |    1312    |     2420
| ML-DSA-65 |     4032    |    1952    |     3309
| ML-DSA-87 |     4896    |    2592    |     4627
|===

They require RBGs of security strength at least 128 (192 recommended), 192, and 256 bits, respectively, for `xi`
(cite:[nist-fips-204] {sect}3.6.1); an ML-DSA-44 CC with a 128-bit RBG has security category 1.

The granularity of the ML-DSA Machines is 8 bits: a field can be transferred with any `SEW` from 8 to 64 bits that divides its size, or through the KLIOBUF in any number of bytes.

The principal functions offered by ML-DSA are:

* `ML-DSA.KeyGen`: Generates a public-private key pair.
* `ML-DSA.Sign`:   Generates a digital signature for a given message.
* `ML-DSA.Verify`: Verifies a digital signature for a given message.
* `HashML-DSA.Sign`:   Generates a digital signature for a given pre-hashed message.
* `HashML-DSA.Verify`: Verifies a digital signature for a given pre-hashed message.

The KLE ML-DSA Machines execute the core lattice operations of signing and verification, specifically `ML-DSA.Sign_internal` and `ML-DSA.Verify_internal` as defined in cite:[nist-fips-204] (Algorithms 7 and 8), with an _external_ message representative μ:
the caller supplies μ in State _mu_Input_, and the steps of these algorithms that compute μ from `tr` and the formatted message `M'`
are not performed. Consequently the Machine never receives the message or the context string `ctx`, and holds `tr` only within `privkey`.

Message hashing and domain formatting (cite:[nist-fips-204] {sect}5) are structured as follows, with `ctx` a context string of at most 255 bytes, chosen by the protocol, `|ctx|` its length in bytes, and `OID(PH)` the DER encoding of the object identifier of `PH` (cite:[nist-fips-204] {sect}5.4):

* *Pure ML-DSA:* The caller formats the domain-separated message string `M' = 0x00 {vvert} bin(|ctx|, 8) {vvert} ctx {vvert} M` and computes the 64-byte message representative `mu = SHAKE256(tr {vvert} M', 64)`, which is supplied to the KLE unit via State _mu_Input_.
* *HashML-DSA (Pre-Hash):* The caller pre-hashes message `M` with the hash function `PH` specified in the _AuxInfo_ field of the MDH, constructs `M' = 0x01 {vvert} bin(|ctx|, 8) {vvert} ctx {vvert} OID(PH) {vvert} PH(M)`, and computes `mu = SHAKE256(tr {vvert} M', 64)` to be loaded into State _mu_Input_.
For FIPS conformance, cite:[nist-fips-204] ({sect}5.4 and {sect}5.4.1) requires `PH(M)` to be computed within a FIPS 140-validated cryptographic module.
That module may be the KLE unit itself, if the unit is FIPS 140-validated.
* *Public Key Hash (`tr`):* `tr = SHAKE256(pubkey, 64)`. It is embedded in `privkey` (cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 24), where `ML-DSA.KeyGen` places it; the caller computes it from the public key.

[NOTE]
====
*The caller's duties in forming μ.*
The message representative μ and the string `M'` are formed as stated above, following cite:[nist-fips-204] {sect}5.2 to 5.4 and Algorithms 2 to 5, 7 and 8,
with `PH` the function that _AuxInfo_ records.
The caller is expected to:

. obtain the public key `pk` of the CC: the key it loaded in State _pubkey_Input_ or, for a CC that holds a private key, the key
  emitted in State _pubkey_Output_ after _GenerateKeyPair_ or _compute_pubKey_, or a copy of it from an authenticated source,
  such as a certificate;
. compute `tr` from `pk` once, and keep it with the CC;
. for each message, choose the mode that _AuxInfo_ selects (pure if _AuxInfo_ = 0), form `M'` with the context string that the
  protocol prescribes, compute μ, and load it in State _mu_Input_, before entering _Sign_Generate_ or _Sign_Verify_;
. use, when verifying, the same `ctx` and mode as the signer; and
. compute `PH(M)` for HashML-DSA within a cryptographic module of the required validation level (see above).

The Machine cannot check these steps: a μ formed with a wrong `tr`, `ctx` or mode yields a signature that the
verifier rejects, but it never discloses or weakens the private key.
====

Data Structures::
+
--
* The *Provisioning Input* consists only of the MDH.
* _MachinePolicy_[0] gates signature generation and _MachinePolicy_[1] signature verification, with their Gated States (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).

* The _AuxInfo_ field is zero for pure `ML-DSA`. For `HashML-DSA` it records the pre-hash function
`PH`, in the format of bits [15:0] of the MDH (_Machine_, _MachinePolicy_ and _MachineExtension_),
which the caller chooses among the hash functions and XOFs that cite:[nist-fips-204] {sect}5.4 admits for the parameter set.
The Machine never reads _AuxInfo_: it neither computes `PH` nor checks or enforces its use.
It keeps the field unchanged, also through provisioning and transitions to _Ready_, so that the MDH, in the clear, informs the recipients of the CC,
which need not implement `PH`. The caller always computes μ with SHAKE256, whatever `PH` is (cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 7).

* *Internal State*:

** _MachineUse_, in the MDH, is the transfer counter _W_ of Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, in bytes, in the States that load or emit a long field.
  In States _GenerateKeyPair_, _compute_pubKey_, _Sign_Generate_ and _Sign_Verify_, it is the field _P_ of
  Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>> for their long-running operations, and encodes their progress in an implementation-defined way.
** `HasPrivKey`, `HasPubKey`: Booleans, recording whether `privkey`, resp. `pubkey`, holds
  a configured value. They are bits 0 and 1 of _StateExtension_, resp., and follow the pattern of Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, with the departures stated below.
** `Hedged`: Boolean, bit 2 of _StateExtension_, recording the signing mode selected on entering _Sign_Generate_.
** `HasMu`: Boolean, bit 3 of _StateExtension_, recording that `mu` holds a completely loaded μ. It follows the pattern of
  Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>.
** `privkey`: 2560/4032/4896 bytes (20480/32256/39168 bits) for ML-DSA-44/65/87, resp.
** `pubkey`: 1312/1952/2592 bytes (10496/15616/20736 bits) for ML-DSA-44/65/87, resp.
** `signature`: 2420/3309/4627 bytes (19360/26472/37016 bits) for ML-DSA-44/65/87, resp.
** `mu`: 64 bytes (512 bits), the external message representative μ
** `rnd`: 32 bytes (256 bits), the random value of hedged signing; it is non-zero only while a hedged signing operation is interrupted (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>>), and zero otherwise, including for deterministic signing.

* Besides `rnd`, the internal state may contain several additional variables, for instance intermediate values of the computation of the NTT.
Their definition and their size are implementation-dependent.
An interrupted operation keeps them in the ADS, never in the Serialized Content (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-progress>>), so that it can resume on the same implementation; without them, it restarts (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-restart>>).
When an operation completes, it destroys the values drawn for it, `xi` and `rnd` included, and its intermediate values; values computed from `privkey` or `pubkey` alone, in any representation, masked shares included, may be kept in the ADS until that field is replaced or erased (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Auxiliary-Data-Section>>); KLE treats them as a representation of that key and protects them as the key itself, although cite:[nist-fips-204] {sect}3.6.3 names only the seed and the matrix Â as values that may be kept.
--
+
* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<1,<3,<5",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Type | Size (Bits) +
for ML-DSA-44/65/87 | Field
|   i   | Public  | 10496 / 15616 / 20736            | `pubkey`
|  ii   | Public  | 19360 / 26472 / 37016            | `signature`
| ii.a  | Public  | 96 / 24 / 104                    | Padding
|  iii  | Private | 20480 / 32256 / 39168            | `privkey` (contains `tr`)
| iii.a | Private | 0 / 0 / 0                        | Padding
|  iv   | Private | 512                              | `mu`
|   v   | Private | 256                              | `rnd`
|  v.a  | Private | 0 / 0 / 0                        | Padding
|===

Positions i to ii.a are the public fields and their padding (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-scc-clear-blocks>>), and `SCC_ClearLen1` is 234, 329 and 452 for ML-DSA-44, ML-DSA-65 and ML-DSA-87.
`pubkey` holds only a public key loaded or computed, a partial load included, or zero, and `signature` only a signature loaded or completed, or zero: a rejected iteration of `ML-DSA.Sign_internal` writes no field.

These contexts are large: for ML-DSA-44, ML-DSA-65 and ML-DSA-87 the serialized content is
6400, 9392 and 12224 bytes, that is 400, 587 and 764 blocks of 128 bits, padding included.

Only `HasPrivKey`, `HasPubKey`, `Hedged` and `HasMu`, in _StateExtension_, record state: nothing records whether `signature` holds a value.
--

//

Machine-Specific Functions::
+
--
The procedures implemented by ML-DSA are taken directly from cite:[nist-fips-204]:

* `ML-DSA.KeyGen()` (cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 1) draws the random seed `xi` and computes the public key `pubkey` and the private key `privkey` as (`pubkey`, `privkey`) = `ML-DSA.KeyGen_internal(xi)` (Algorithm 6).
* `ML-DSA.Sign_internal(privkey, mu, rnd)` generates the digital signature `signature` from private key `privkey`, message representative `mu`, and randomness `rnd` (cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 7).
* `ML-DSA.Verify_internal(pubkey, mu, signature)` verifies the signature `signature` against public key `pubkey` and message representative `mu` (cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 8).

The loops of `ML-DSA.Sign_internal`, `RejBoundedPoly`, `RejNTTPoly` and `SampleInBall` iterate an indeterminate number
of times (cite:[nist-fips-204] Appendix C). An implementation bounds each by an implementation-defined limit of at least
that of cite:[nist-fips-204] Table 3: 814 iterations of the loop of `ML-DSA.Sign_internal`; 481 iterations, or 481 bytes
of XOF output, for `RejBoundedPoly`; 298 iterations, or 894 bytes of XOF output, for `RejNTTPoly`; and, for
`SampleInBall`, 121 iterations of the while loop of its step 8 within one iteration of its for loop, or 221 bytes of XOF
output for the whole call. A correct execution reaches these limits with probability of about 2^-256^ or less
(cite:[nist-fips-204] Appendix C). As in cite:[nist-fips-204], the execution time need not be independent of the number of iterations
(<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-SCProtection-field>>).
A limit reached ends the operation in _Failure_, without output: its intermediate values and the values drawn for it, `xi` and `rnd` included, are destroyed, and
_MachineUse_ is zeroed.

NOTE: cite:[nist-fips-204] Appendix C recommends not bounding these loops; KLE requires the bound, so that every
operation ends in bounded time, and the limits of Table 3 are reached by a correct execution with negligible
probability.
//
// NOTE: With deterministic signing, the execution time may reveal information about μ, but not about the private key (cite:[nist-fips-204] {sect}3.6.1, footnote 3); hedged signing does not.
//
// NOTE: The challenge `c` and response `z` of a rejected iteration of `ML-DSA.Sign_internal`, and which check rejected it,
// reveal the private key (cite:[DBLP-journals-tches-ZhouWSY25], cite:[DBLP-conf-host-KarabulutAA21],
// cite:[DBLP-conf-sacrypt-UlitzschMTS22]); hence the progress of _Sign_Generate_ reveals at most the number of completed
// iterations, as the execution time does.
--

State Machine:: {empty}
+
--
* *States:*
Besides
_Ready_ (1),
_Success_ (54),
_Failure_ (55),
ML-DSA Machines define:
 ** _GenerateKeyPair_ (2)
 ** _pubkey_Output_ (3)
 ** _pubkey_Input_ (4)
 ** _mu_Input_ (6)
 ** _Sign_Generate_ (8)
 ** _Sign_Output_ (9)
 ** _Sign_Verify_ (10)
 ** _Sign_Input_ (11)
 ** _privkey_Input_ (12)
 ** _compute_pubKey_ (13)

* *Allowed State Transitions:*

** By `kl.setst`, subject to Rules <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>, <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>> and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-success-failure-cannot-be-set-directly>> and to the entry conditions below: between any two different States listed above, and, in _privkey_Input_, _pubkey_Input_, _mu_Input_ and _Sign_Input_, to the same State, which restarts the load. _Sign_Output_ is entered only on completion of _Sign_Generate_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>).
** On completion of its `kl.exec`: _GenerateKeyPair_ -> _pubkey_Output_; _compute_pubKey_ -> _Ready_; _Sign_Generate_ -> _Sign_Output_; _Sign_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
** Once its field has been completely emitted: _pubkey_Output_ -> _Success_; _Sign_Output_ -> _Success_.
** Once its field has been completely loaded, with excess data in the final transfer: _pubkey_Input_ or _Sign_Input_ ->
   _Failure_.
** When an implementation-defined loop limit is reached (see above): -> _Failure_.

* *Behavior:*

** Provisioning:
  . clears `privkey` and `pubkey`, and clears `HasPrivKey` and `HasPubKey`;
  . zeroes _MachineUse_;
  . leaves the locker in _Ready_.

** Entering _Ready_:
  . clears `signature`, `mu`, `rnd` and `HasMu`, and zeroes _MachineUse_;
  . keeps `privkey`, `pubkey`, `HasPrivKey` and `HasPubKey`.

** In State _GenerateKeyPair_, a Form D `kl.exec` generates a new key pair with `ML-DSA.KeyGen` of
   cite:[nist-fips-204] (Algorithm 1), from a seed `xi` drawn from the RBG (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-rbg-failure>>):
  . the key pair replaces `privkey` (which embeds `tr`) and `pubkey`, and `HasPrivKey` and `HasPubKey` are set;
  . the state machine transitions to _pubkey_Output_, with _MachineUse_ zeroed; leaving it keeps the key pair.

** Loading `privkey`, and system-specific imports:
  . entering _privkey_Input_ clears `HasPrivKey` and `HasPubKey` and erases `pubkey`;
  . a system-specific import (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-completion>>) that converts a `privkey` checks it
    as a completed load does, with the same outcomes, and sets `HasPrivKey`;
  . such an import drops any `pubkey` that the format carries: `pubkey` stays zero and `HasPubKey` clear, as entering
    _privkey_Input_ leaves them, and _compute_pubKey_ then derives `pubkey`;
  . a system-specific import that converts a `pubkey` without a `privkey` sets `HasPubKey`, as a completed load does
    (Rule <<KLE-MGR-sysfmt-standard>>).

** Entering _pubkey_Input_:
  . requires `HasPrivKey` to be clear (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>), and clears `HasPubKey`;
  . when the load completes without excess data, `HasPubKey` is set.

** In States _privkey_Input_, _pubkey_Input_, _mu_Input_ and _Sign_Input_, Form B `kl.exec` instructions load `privkey`,
   `pubkey`, `mu` and `signature`, respectively, following Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, with the progress recorded in
   _MachineUse_, in bytes.

** The checks of the loaded fields:
  . a `privkey` with a coefficient of `s1` or `s2` outside [−{eta}, {eta}] (cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 25) is a
    configuration error: the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ when `privkey` is written, by a completed load
    or by an import with `HasPrivKey` set (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>);
  . every `pubkey` of the correct length is well formed (cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 23);
  . departing from Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, excess data in the final loading transfer of `pubkey` or
    `signature` is a data error (cite:[nist-fips-204] {sect}3.6.2): the field is zeroed, `HasPubKey` stays clear for a
    `pubkey`, and the state machine transitions to _Failure_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>);
  . a `signature` is not checked when it is loaded: as in cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 8, `ML-DSA.Verify_internal`
    decodes it (Algorithm 27), and rejects a malformed `signature`, for which the decoding returns {falsum}, as one that
    does not verify (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>).
// , like one that does not verify against `mu`, a data error on which _Sign_Verify_ transitions to _Failure_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>).

** Entering _compute_pubKey_ requires `HasPrivKey` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>). In it, a Form D `kl.exec`:
  . derives `pubkey` from `privkey`: it decodes it with skDecode (cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 25) and applies steps 3, 5,
    6 and 8 of Algorithm 6;
  . checks that the public key hashes to `tr`, embedded in `privkey`; if not, the locker transitions to Error State
    _Invalid_;
  . writes the derived public key into `pubkey` and sets `HasPubKey`;
  . transitions to _Ready_, which keeps the key pair.

** When entering _Sign_Generate_, by a Form B `kl.setst` whose `Xs` selects the signing mode of
   cite:[nist-fips-204] {sect}3.4:
  . the State requires `HasPrivKey` and `HasMu` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>);
  . `Xs` = 0, for instance with `Xs` = `X0`, selects _hedged_ signing and sets `Hedged`;
  . any other `Xs` selects _deterministic_ signing and clears `Hedged`;
  . the `kl.setst` zeroes _MachineUse_, so that its progress is counted from entry and tracks the current operation, not the transfer that preceded it.

** In State _Sign_Generate_, a Form D `kl.exec` signs with `ML-DSA.Sign_internal` of cite:[nist-fips-204]
   (Algorithm 7):
  . the operation is long-running, and _MachineUse_ is its field _P_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>>);
  . _MachineUse_ reveals at most the number of completed iterations of the signing loop; the challenge, the response and
    the failed check of a rejected iteration must not be observable, through _MachineUse_ or otherwise;
  . if _MachineUse_ is zero, the operation starts, with `rnd` drawn from an approved RBG
    (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-rbg-failure>>) if `Hedged` is set, and `rnd` = `zeros(256)` otherwise;
  . if _MachineUse_ is non-zero, the interrupted operation is resumed with the `rnd` held, or restarted
    (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-progress-discard>>);
  . on completion, the output of `ML-DSA.Sign_internal` is written in `signature`, and the locker
    transitions to _Sign_Output_.

** Entering _Sign_Verify_ requires `HasPubKey` and `HasMu` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>). In it, a Form D `kl.exec`
   verifies `signature` with `ML-DSA.Verify_internal` of cite:[nist-fips-204] (Algorithm 8):
  . if it returns `true`, the state machine transitions to _Success_, otherwise to _Failure_;
  . nothing is written to `signature`;
  . departing from Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, _Sign_Verify_ has no entry condition on `signature`; it verifies the
    value that `signature` holds:
  .. the signature last loaded in _Sign_Input_ or computed by _Sign_Generate_ since _Ready_ was last entered;
  .. zero, if there is none or if the last load was left incomplete.

** In _pubkey_Output_ and _Sign_Output_:
  . Form C `kl.exec` instructions emit `pubkey`, resp. `signature`, recording the progress in _MachineUse_, in bytes, and
    end in _Success_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>);
  . entering _pubkey_Output_ requires `HasPubKey` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).

** _MachinePolicy_ may be narrowed using `kl.rstrl` or `kl.rstrv` (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-policy-narrowing-state>>).
--

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-derive-endpoints]]
== Derivation Endpoints of the Machines

<<KLE-defined-derivation-endpoints>> lists the architected `kl.derive` endpoints of the Machines of this Chapter.

[[KLE-defined-derivation-endpoints]]
.Derivation Endpoints of the Architected Machines
[float="center",align="center",width="100%",cols="<42%,<16%,<14%,<28%",options="header"]
|===
| Machine(s) | Field/Operation | Role  |  State

| <<KLE-ECB-mode,ECB mode>>, <<KLE-chaining,Chaining/feedback modes>>, <<KLE-keystream-modes,Keystream generation>>, <<KLE-GCM-mode,GCM mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode,GCM-SIV mode>>, <<KLE-CMAC-mode,CMAC mode>>, <<KLE-OCB-mode,OCB mode>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128,Ascon-AEAD128>> | `key` | destination | _Ready_

| <<KLE-XEX-XTS-modes,XEX/XTS modes>> | `key1 {vvert} key2`    | destination | _Ready_

| <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-N-masking,Ascon-AEAD128 with Nonce Masking>> | `K1 {vvert} K2`, except of a shared secret that Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>> restricts | destination | _Ready_

// Not admissible pairs for now:
// | <<KLE-GCM-mode,GCM mode>>       | `kl.exec` input (the IV: at most the `len`/8 − `cumul_len`/8 bytes still to absorb, with the granularity of _Set_Aux_Value_), except of a shared secret that Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>> restricts | destination | _Set_Aux_Value_
//
// | <<KLE-OCB-mode,OCB mode>>       | `N` ({lceil}``N_len``/8{rceil} bytes), except of a shared secret that Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>> restricts | destination | _Set_Aux_Value_

| <<KLE-HMAC,HMAC>>, NIK variant  | `K0`                   | destination | _Set_Key_
