| Exactly these hash functions, MACs and XOFs: <<KLE-SHA-2,SHA-2>>, <<KLE-SM3,SM3>>, <<KLE-BLAKE2,BLAKE2>>, <<KLE-SHA-3,SHA-3 and SHAKE>>, <<KLE-HMAC,HMAC>> on SHA-2, SHA-3 and SM3, <<KLE-KMAC,KMAC and KMACXOF>>,
<<KLE-Ascon-Hash256,Ascon-Hash256>>, <<KLE-Ascon-XOF128,Ascon-XOF128>> and <<KLE-Ascon-CXOF128,Ascon-CXOF128>>
| `kl.exec` input, of a shared secret only (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>>) | destination | _Hash_Absorb_

// Not an admissible pair for now:
// | <<KLE-CMAC-mode,CMAC>>, with any 64- or 128-bit block cipher | `kl.exec` input, except of a shared secret that Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>> restricts (a derived value cannot end the message: the last block is supplied by `kl.exec` in _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_) | destination | _Hash_Absorb_

| <<KLE-ECC,Elliptic Curve>>
| the shared secret as _Output_ emits it, formed from `SecondPt` | source      | _Output_ with `OutputType` = _shared secret_ and `block_base` = 0
// Not an admissible pair for now:
// | `Scalar`, except of a shared secret that Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>> restricts | destination | _Set_Scalar_

| <<KLE-PQC-ML-KEM,ML-KEM>>       | `sharedkey`            | source      | _sharedkey_Output_

| <<KLE-keystream-modes,Keystream generation>>, <<KLE-chaining,OFB>> | the keystream (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-exec-implies-unrestricted>>) | source | _Operate_

| <<KLE-CMAC-mode,CMAC>>, and the hash functions, MACs and XOFs listed above as destinations | the `kl.exec` output (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-exec-implies-unrestricted>>) | source | _Hash_Output_, and _Hash_Output_Bits_ where the Machine has it
// Before the simplification of the admissible pairs:
// | Any Machine | `kl.exec` output (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-exec-implies-unrestricted>>) | source | any State in which Form C `kl.exec` emits output, except ECC _Output_ with `OutputType` = _shared secret_, whose source is the field
|===

A source and a destination form a pair only as Rules <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>>–<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-exec-implies-unrestricted>> and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sealed>> admit.
For now, the admissible pairs move:

* an ML-KEM `sharedkey`, or the shared secret of an x25519 or x448 Machine (<<KLE-X25519-X448>>), into a listed key of a
  symmetric Machine; a restricted shared secret does not go into `K1 {vvert} K2` of nonce masking;
* a shared secret of any ECC or ML-KEM Machine into the input of a listed hash function, HMAC, KMAC or XOF, as the
  input of a key-derivation method of cite:[NIST-SP-800-56Cr2]; for the other ECC Machines, whose shared secret is
  not a uniform string, this is the only destination, as cite:[NIST-SP-800-56Ar3] {sect}5.8 requires for `Z`;
* the keystream of a keystream Machine, or the output of a CMAC, hash function, MAC or XOF Machine, into a listed key of a
  symmetric Machine, as the key-derivation methods of cite:[NIST-SP-800-56Cr2] and cite:[nist-SP-800-108-r1] produce it;
* a sealed source (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sealed>>) as above, but never into the key of keystream generation, OFB or CMAC, whose outputs are
  sources, nor into `K1 {vvert} K2`; a hash, MAC or XOF input, or an HMAC `K0`, that receives it makes its locker sealed.

A destination key field still sets its own length conditions (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-checks>> item 6): the `K0` of
HMAC takes only a source of exactly `eff_length` bytes, which a keystream or the output of an XOF without bound is not, so
either one into `K0` invalidates the destination alone.
A locker of a Machine that Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>> lists, whose `tag_len_p` is shorter than the full length, is not a destination.
Every such `kl.derive` is uninterruptible (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-interruptibility-classes>>).

// NOTE: The `K2` of <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-N-masking>> is not an endpoint for a restricted shared secret (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-shared-secret>>). It only masks a nonce that the caller chooses.
//
// NOTE: _Msg_Absorb_ of EdDSA (<<KLE-EdDSA>>) is not an endpoint: feeding it by `kl.derive` would add no flexibility to the protocols that use EdDSA, only software complexity and locker pressure.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
